# INV-03-03 — Machine, device placement, clocks and containment

## Current summary

**Current cross-track status:** INV03-01 claim20's final selection/closure is
reopened by the extension-versus-hybrid comparison. The accepted machine/memory
contracts below and hybrid's conditional composition remain candidate evidence;
references below to completed architecture no longer imply final selection.
No separate campaign in this investigation is assigned.

- **Question and current direction:** Q-03-03 owns machine/device placement,
  clocks, boot and containment inside host code generation plus captive-VM
  execution with an EL1 shim. Selected QEMU device components and conventional
  PC/PIIX-derived ACPI do not select a complete QEMU runtime or a code generator.
  Host PIC/PIT/RTC/IOAPIC and resident LAPIC/event services retain their reviewed
  ownership interfaces; no adaptation-cost or speed ranking is established.
- **Accepted architectural contracts:** question8 events/waits (claims63–64/66,
  root Returns42/44/46), question9 clock/timer composition (61–62, Returns40–41),
  question10 boot/lifecycle (69, Return48) and question12 containment/resources
  (70, Return49) are independently accepted within their named premises and
  cross-question dependencies. Question11 devices/backends is independently accepted in73/root Return52;
  INV03-01 claim20 now accepts their current combined architecture.
- **Boot and events:**69 selects the parameterized direct-loader map, installed
  Ubuntu/Debian root admissions, version0 AP mailbox/startup32, protected firmware,
  S5 and optional cold reconstruction reboot. Claim66 supplies immutable
  immediate-RSM firmware and closed/locked SMRAM. Required receipt, masking,
  routing and wake contracts are accepted; question5 still owns precise delivery.
  Actual image admission and emitted loader conformance belong to implementation.
  No stock boot, arbitrary guest real-mode firmware or optional lifecycle beyond
  the recorded envelope is claimed.
- **Devices already settled in scope:** serial60 has independent Return39;
  entropy56 has root Return35; host-RAM/block completion and truthful file

- **Network status:**57–58 accept the conditional virtio-net/packet interface and

  Claim59 rejects unchanged libslirp as a complete substitute. Claim65/Return43
  identifies unprivileged Darwin ICMPv6;67/Return45 accepts scoped reply/error,
  DNS and allocation interfaces. Claim68/Return47 accepts connected route-MTU
  and separate receive composition plus the named DNSConfiguration provider.
  Claim71/Return50 accepts the exact missing IPv6 fragmentation/extension paths.
  Claim72/root Return51 accepts the finite normalizer/reassembly/packetizer
  construction, including full non-jumbo UDP lengths. Claim73/root Return52
  accepts the composed ordinary IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP/ICMP device/backend contract

- **Containment:**70/Return49 accepts role-specific manifests: execution/device
  worker without networking, fallible libslirp worker with authorized TCP/UDP,

- **Evidence limits:** source/conditional findings, accepted architectural

- **Combined devices decision:**73/root Return52 accepts the staged required-device

- **Next action:** carry these accepted contracts through claim20's completed

## Charter coverage and component boundaries

The fixed architecture is host translation with captive in-VM execution and
shims. This investigation proposes machine/device components inside it. Choosing
QEMU device models neither chooses TCG nor imports the QEMU CPU execution loop,
softMMU, helper placement or synchronization runtime. Any QEMU components actually
linked together must use compatible dependencies; the inspected common pin is
not a requirement that every generator, semantic library or machine component
come from QEMU. Even within that pin, each crossed host/shim boundary needs its
own contract. Claim 25's source recheck does not prove a linked build.

| Track 03 charter question | Current contribution here | Remaining composition / conformance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 — virtual CPU/topology (with INV-03-01) | Claim69 boot CPU/map/topology manifest and accepted clock/event contracts bind the accepted virtual profile | Preserve agreement with INV03-01 discovery/control state; actual emitted tables require conformance checking. |
| 8 — interrupts, waits, coordination | Claims63–64/66 and root Returns42/44/46 accept routing, retention, INIT/SIPI/SMI and wait/progress construction | Bind question5 precise delivery to the accepted receipt and boot contracts; no complete runtime claim. |
| 9 — clocks/timers | Claims61–62 and root Returns40–41 accept active-time reference, phase/receiver folding and timer ownership | Preserve CPU frequency/MSR and event observation-cut agreement; measured timing fidelity remains unestablished. |
| 10 — boot/lifecycle | Claim69/root Return48 accepts direct-loader/map/input admission, AP startup, S5 and explicit reboot/optional-lifecycle scope | Validate actual image inputs and loader implementation; no stock-boot observation is inferred. |
| 11 — device interfaces/backends | Serial60, entropy56 and INV03-04 block26–29 accepted in scope; network57–59/65/67–68/71 establish interfaces and exact gaps | Claim72/Return51 and73/Return52 accept normalizer and selected device/network composition; bind the accepted contract into the combined architecture and validate implementation separately. |
| 12 — containment/exhaustion | Claim70/root Return49 accepts per-role capabilities, trusted mediation, resource failure and teardown under named OS/private-policy premises | Maintain accepted trust/grant boundaries in network composition; actual launcher/profile conformance belongs to implementation, not a generic demand for more denial probes. |

## Evidence-history reading rule

The sections below retain the source findings, their original method/budget and
review sequence. A historical stop describes that pass's scope, not today's
remaining-work order. Later reviewed claims supersede earlier hypotheses where
explicitly stated (notably claim 29's sleep policy). The current summary and
charter table above are authoritative; preserved source facts are not promoted
to architecture acceptance by this cleanup.


## Assumptions and breadth

**Inferred design boundary.** Device placement cannot be selected independently
of [translator recovery](INV-03-02.md), [all-writer coherence](INV-03-04.md), and
[the combined architecture](INV-03-01.md). A guest MMIO instruction is a CPU
transaction with a recoverable commit point; a DMA completion is a memory writer
and an event producer. Host TSO assumptions for translated execution do not
cover either host device workers or their queues.

The target retains unmodified stock Linux, direct boot, SMP, serial, block,
network, entropy, timer/APIC and shutdown. A device driver absent from the
stock initrd is a compatibility problem even if present as an installable
module. No requirement is silently traded away for a smaller machine.
UEFI/Windows are implications only. Optional reset, CPU hotplug, migration,
suspend and snapshots are not claimed: their scope disposition belongs in
INV-03-01 and Q-00-02/09, before planning promises them.

## Supporting contracts

**CLM-03-03-01 — Source-backed: BSP loading has an explicit separate ABI.**
[Linux v6.12 boot protocol §1.15](https://docs.kernel.org/6.12/arch/x86/boot.html#bit-boot-protocol-1)
requires zero-initialized `boot_params`, copied/examined setup header, 64-bit
entry at loaded kernel start plus `0x200`, paging enabled, identity mapping of
`init_size`, parameters and command line, specified flat GDT segments, IF clear,
and RSI pointing at parameters. Header alignment and relocation fields constrain
placement. This supplies a loader contract, not evidence that a loader exists.

**CLM-03-03-02 — Inherited accepted source/observed limits.**
[INV-01-34 claims 01–11](INV-01-34.md#supporting-contracts-and-claims) establish
ACPI mailbox feasibility, the Linux 5.19 upstream feature floor and the inspected
Ubuntu/Debian startup paths. The Ubuntu path requires compatibility32 and
paging-off protected32 before long mode returns. The finite fixture does not
execute a stock image. MADT mailbox version 0 is a cold-start mechanism, not
CPU recycling or kexec support. The owning record and its direct ACPI/Linux
citations remain authoritative; this proposal does not widen those findings.

**CLM-03-03-03 — Source-backed: a reduced QEMU machine is an available model,
not a drop-in solution.** [QEMU v10.0.0 `hw/i386/microvm.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/v10.0.0/hw/i386/microvm.c)
constructs virtio-mmio, serial and interrupt infrastructure and has configurable
legacy/platform components. [Upstream microvm documentation](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/system/i386/microvm.html)
describes its minimized platform and direct-kernel use; this moving documentation
was inspected September 19, 2026, and is orientation, while v10.0.0 source is
the adaptation reference. Reusing it does not establish mailbox firmware,
our 64-bit BSP handoff, the chosen guest CPU or a new accelerator boundary.

**CLM-03-03-04 — Source-backed: ACPI can enumerate modern virtio-mmio.**
[Linux v6.12 `drivers/virtio/virtio_mmio.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/virtio/virtio_mmio.c),
`virtio_mmio_acpi_match`, recognizes `LNRO0005`; `virtio_mmio_probe` obtains
MMIO/IRQ resources and validates transport version. Its command-line device
path is separately configuration guarded. Driver source availability does not
prove distribution configuration, built-in availability or initrd contents.

**CLM-03-03-05 — Source-backed: virtio ordering, reset and storage semantics
remain mandatory.** [VIRTIO 1.2](https://docs.oasis-open.org/virtio/virtio/v1.2/virtio-v1.2.html),
§§2.6–2.7, 4.2 and 5.2, specifies queue ownership/publication, MMIO registers,
reset and block cache/flush behavior. Completion is not interchangeable with
persistence. A writeback configuration needs a truthful flush path; reset must
finish before a queue is reused. Negotiation determines which optional features
are legal. These are source contracts, not a host-memory barrier recipe.

**CLM-03-03-06 — Source-backed: inherited QEMU devices already carry lifetime
and storage failure machinery.** [QEMU v10.0.0 `hw/virtio/virtio.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/v10.0.0/hw/virtio/virtio.c)
contains ring mapping caches, RCU retirement and explicit publication barriers.
[Same release `block/file-posix.c`, `handle_aiocb_flush`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/v10.0.0/block/file-posix.c)
uses `qemu_fdatasync` and latches buffered flush failure because later success
cannot establish recovery of lost dirty data. Preserve this error path when
adapting; its existence is not proof of the selected macOS filesystem/device's
power-loss durability. That exact lower contract remains to be audited.

**CLM-03-03-07 — Source-backed: clock discovery depends on CPU profile.**
[Linux v6.12 `arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c`, `native_calibrate_tsc`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c)
conditions CPUID leaf `0x15` frequency discovery on vendor and feature availability,
then uses numerator, denominator and crystal frequency. Merely advertising a
frequency leaf is insufficient if other CPU identity or calibration paths differ.

**CLM-03-03-08 — Source-backed: reuse does not establish containment.**
[QEMU security documentation](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/system/security.html#non-virtualization-use-case),
inspected September 19, 2026, explicitly disclaims isolation guarantees for TCG
and does not treat shared-memory vhost-user/vfio-user process separation as a
security boundary. Its least-privilege guidance requires resource restriction,
not merely a subprocess. Apple's [App Sandbox](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/app-sandbox)
is a candidate restriction mechanism; the retrieved overview does not establish
our Hypervisor entitlement, file-descriptor, networking or worker combination.
No working macOS containment profile is claimed.

**CLM-03-03-09 — Source-backed, accepted: the inherited flush helper
needs a macOS durability decision.** [QEMU v10.0.0 `util/osdep.c`,
`qemu_fdatasync`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/v10.0.0/util/osdep.c)
calls `fdatasync` when configured, otherwise `fsync`; it does not request
`F_FULLFSYNC`. Apple's [archived `fcntl(2)` manual](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man2/fcntl.2.html)
distinguishes the latter as a request to flush drive buffers after filesystem
synchronization and notes that a device may ignore it. The current macOS 26.4
SDK `usr/include/sys/fcntl.h`, `F_FULLFSYNC`, still describes that drive-flush
request. The old manual's filesystem list is not evidence about APFS support.
**Inferred consequence:** retaining QEMU's error latching is necessary but not
sufficient for the proposed persistence claim. Select an explicit flush path and
supported storage contract; a successful syscall alone is not an observed
power-loss guarantee. Silent fallback after an unsupported full flush cannot
retain a stronger advertised promise.

**CLM-03-03-10 — Source-backed, accepted: named sandbox API is not a
custom confinement contract.** The installed macOS 26.4 SDK
`usr/include/sandbox.h`, `sandbox_init`, documents `SANDBOX_NAMED`, reserves
other flags, and marks the interface deprecated/unsupported. A custom profile
would therefore be a separately evidenced undocumented mechanism; it is not
excluded by the current workflow, nor established by that declaration. App
Sandbox or a narrowly restricted worker remains a candidate requiring actual
HVF/signing/resource compatibility and host/peer denial evidence. The inspected
SDK root was `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX26.4.sdk`.

## Proposed combined mechanism — Hypothesis, not accepted behavior

| Responsibility | Initial placement and implementation direction | Required interaction / current uncertainty |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Machine construction | Host loader and QEMU-derived ACPI/table construction; fixed RAM/MMIO layout generated from one machine description | Validate all ranges and arithmetic before allocation. E820, ACPI reservations, mailbox, kernel/initrd/decompressor work area and private shim memory must not overlap. RSDP discovery and exact FADT/DSDT content remain to be specified. |
| BSP/AP boot | Host initializes x86 BSP state to the 64-bit boot ABI; shim implements accepted mailbox responder; translator executes actual AP trampoline | One CPU-ID map must generate CPUID topology, MADT entries, mailbox destinations, APIC IDs and vCPU slots. Native vCPU creation is distinct from x86 AP startup. No shortcut past startup32. |
| Interrupts | Shim owns per-vCPU local APIC and pending event state; current provider revision keeps IOAPIC routing in the host with PIC and device models | GIC notification is a wake signal, not the x86 interrupt model. Preserve masking, priority, EOI/level retrigger and halted-vCPU races through the architecture's event protocol. Full APIC register semantics remain open. |
| Clocks/timers | One VM-wide reference epoch/rate; shim computes TSC and local deadlines; host supplies scheduling/wakeup service | Same scale and epoch across vCPUs, per-vCPU architectural write offsets, cancellation generation and explicit past-deadline handling. Frequency discovery must agree with the CPU profile. Host preemption delays delivery, not the clock. |
| Serial | Reuse host 16550-style state/backend, accessed through exact-once port-I/O transactions | Synchronous status reads and UART side effects must survive exits without replay. Stream output is not a new firmware interface. |
| Block/network/entropy | Host modern virtio-mmio devices backed by existing QEMU models; begin with split queues, one block queue and one network RX/TX pair, minimal negotiated features | Guest drivers and initrd must actually support this transport. Reuse block graph, network backend and entropy provider; do not invent another ring parser. Throughput is unmeasured. |
| Shutdown | ACPI-described shutdown register/operation handled by host lifecycle owner | Concrete FADT/DSDT/driver path and S5 behavior must be audited. A magic emulator-only command is insufficient for unmodified Linux. |
| Isolation | VM worker distinct from UI/controller, with only VM resources; narrow validated IPC; no guest-supplied host paths | Worker crash may terminate this VM. Same-user process separation alone does not restrict filesystem, peers or network. macOS enforcement remains a design blocker. |

**Inferred protocol requirements for this candidate.** Every host request carries
VM lifetime, queue/device lifetime and request identity. The host snapshots and
validates untrusted descriptors (bounds, arithmetic overflow, chain length and
access direction) before using them; no cached guest pointer survives resource
retirement. A replayed request cannot repeat an MMIO side effect. A completion
first makes data and device status visible, then publishes completion, then
signals the event; the event consumer rechecks pending work before sleeping.
These are proposed proof obligations, not a claim that identifiers alone prevent
races or that C atomics automatically synchronize host and VM mappings.

**Inferred all-writer consequence.** Device input must enter guest RAM through
the same coherence authority as guest/helper writes in INV-03-04. Copying into
a host alias bypasses guest CPU write faults: it can modify page tables, executable
pages and their aliases without notification. Initial direction is bounded bounce
buffers for device reads into guest memory, followed by a coordinated commit that
pins destinations, checks the lifetime and applies code/page-table invalidation
before completion. Host kernel I/O must not DMA directly into untracked RAM.
Guest transmit/disk-write buffers can also race malicious guest mutation; memory
safety must not depend on guest ownership rules being obeyed. Selection of the
actual freeze/commit protocol is a coupled blocker with INV-03-04.

**Superseded hypothesis (explicitly revised by claim 29 below): time contract.** Use a single monotonic virtual reference and
integer/rational conversion for TSC and all timer models, retaining enough width
to avoid overflow. The initial proposed policy is elapsed time across host sleep,
no real-time delivery promise, and no independently mutable per-vCPU epochs except
architectural TSC write offsets. Resume delivers already-expired events according
to the device's pending/coalescing rules. No native counter has yet been shown to
supply that policy through HVF sleep and vCPU scheduling; either choose and review
a host reference/offset protocol or revise the policy before acceptance. HPET,
PIT, RTC and/or ACPI PM timer requirements cannot be removed until the selected
kernel's calibration and wallclock paths are traced. Native timer feasibility
alone does not settle the guest clock contract. Performance clocks stay in Track 05.

**Hypothesis: failure contract.** Invalid device input, exhausted bounded queues,
translator crashes or unresponsive vCPUs may terminate the VM, preserving host
and peer resources. Teardown stops admissions, marks the lifetime dead, quiesces
or kills workers, drains callbacks and only then releases mappings. Failed block
requests return truthful error status; failed flush cannot be converted to later
success or acknowledged durability. Transparent restart, completed-write rollback
and recovery of half-written requests are not promised. Backend failure cannot
turn an already acknowledged durable flush into permission to lose those writes.

## Alternatives, cost and falsifiers

- **Host all device registers:** minimizes shim model adaptation and provides a
  useful reference, but incurs exits for hot APIC/timer accesses. Retain as the
  comparison for Q-04-08; no speed ranking without qualified measurements.
- **All models in the shim:** might reduce exits but moves host I/O APIs, parsing
  and QEMU object/event-loop assumptions into a constrained execution domain.
  Provisional rejection is adaptation complexity, not a measured cost claim.
- **Virtio PCI / broader PC machine:** preferred fallback if stock transport,
  initrd or firmware discovery invalidates the smaller platform. Additional bus
  machinery may be cheaper than a new compatibility burden. No silent stock-guest
  narrowing to preserve virtio-mmio.
- **New minimal devices:** avoided because existing queue/storage/network behavior
  already carries difficult errors and lifetime rules. QEMU reuse brings GPL and
  other per-component license obligations; warnings, not a selection veto.
- **UEFI/Windows:** direct Linux boot plus mailbox startup does not supply UEFI
  services or Windows SMP/driver contracts. A later option would need firmware,
  additional machine devices and a separately established CPU-start path; no
  compatibility inference is made here.

Concrete falsifiers are: a target stock image cannot enumerate/root-mount the
chosen transport; direct entry or AP startup needs an unsupported transition;
clock discovery chooses an unimplemented fallback; host writes bypass coherence;
a reused device retries committed MMIO; queue reset admits an old completion;
a successful guest flush lacks the promised backing-store guarantee; or a VM
worker can access unauthorized host/peer resources. Each reopens the affected
choice, not automatically the host-translator family.

## Method, limitations and review

Source-only exploration, September 19, 2026, author `/root/machine`, separate from
architecture author `/root`. Budget: 15 minutes of primary-source inspection and
one document; stopping condition: concrete provisional direction plus evidence
limits, no implementation or experiment. Inspected workflow, scope, Track 03,
Track 01 and INV-01-34, then Linux boot/virtio/TSC source, VIRTIO 1.2, QEMU machine,
queue/storage source and security guidance. Sought contrary evidence specifically
in startup32, clock discovery branches, queue retirement, failed flush behavior
and the stated security boundary.

The QEMU `acpi-microvm.c` source and detailed Apple documentation were not
retrievable through this audit's tools; network-restricted shell retrieval also
failed. No missing content was inferred from filenames or an overview page.
Consequently exact ACPI shutdown and macOS containment/time/durability contracts
remain open. No observed result, timing qualification or independent replication
is claimed. Shared QEMU ancestry cannot be the only eventual correctness oracle;
protocol/specification checks and independent fault/ordering tests must cover the
new cross-domain boundaries. [Independent findings review](../reviews/INV-03-03-findings.md) by
`/root/translator` accepts source findings 01–08 and the unresolved-blocker
assessment, not the architecture. Subsequent source supplements 09–10 were
independently inspected by this author from root's source leads and accepted by
that reviewer in Return 1. Their concrete durability/containment follow-up remains open; the subsequent
stock-transport pass below was prioritized first.


## Follow-up method — stock transport source audit

Exploratory source-only follow-up, author `/root/machine`, September 19. Budget:
30 minutes, at most two source-package downloads of at most 5 MiB each, 45-second
network timeout each; no package installation, execution of downloaded scripts,
VM or image modification. Question: does the working Debian distribution's
standard initramfs tooling actually include/load virtio-mmio and virtio-blk for
the proposed ACPI platform? Inspect the official generator and enumeration path;
stop at a directly sourced inclusion/discovery argument or an image-specific
missing prerequisite. Source conclusions must distinguish stock defaults from
actual generated initrd contents and go to independent review in this thread.


**CLM-03-03-11 — Source-backed, accepted: default Debian initramfs
construction explicitly selects virtio-mmio, but is not an inspected initrd.**
[Debian trixie initramfs-tools 0.148.4 source archive](https://deb.debian.org/debian/pool/main/i/initramfs-tools/initramfs-tools_0.148.4.tar.xz),
`conf/initramfs.conf`, defaults to `MODULES=most`, permitting configuration
overrides. `mkinitramfs` dispatches that value to `auto_add_modules` in
`hook-functions`; its `base` class explicitly requests `virtio_pci virtio_mmio`.
The `block` class selects block drivers through the `blockfuncs` symbol filter,
including `blk_mq_alloc_disk`. `_call_dracut_install` invokes the distribution's
module copier with optional-module handling; requesting a module does not prove
its successful inclusion. [Linux v6.12 virtio_blk](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/block/virtio_blk.c),
`virtblk_probe`, calls `blk_mq_alloc_disk`, supporting that selection route.
The exact built module and dracut-install result were not inspected here.

**CLM-03-03-12 — Source-backed/Inferred, accepted: ACPI autoload has a
standard route distinct from command-line MMIO.** [Linux v6.12 `platform.c`,
`platform_uevent`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/base/platform.c)
uses the ACPI modalias when applicable; `platform_match` checks ACPI matches.
The virtio-mmio module publishes its `LNRO0005` ACPI match (claim 04).
[systemd v257 `80-drivers.rules`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/systemd/systemd/v257/rules.d/80-drivers.rules)
loads modules on add events with a MODALIAS. This is a conditional source path:
the particular initrd must contain the module, alias metadata, loader/rule and
triggering services, and the machine must publish valid ACPI resources. No
specific generated Debian initrd or downstream systemd package is established.
Thus disabling `VIRTIO_MMIO_CMDLINE_DEVICES` is not a source-level reason to
reject the ACPI candidate; source defaults do not prove the actual boot path.

Follow-up outcome: one 116-KiB-class official source archive and its descriptor
were retrieved with the declared limits. The complete archive SHA256 matched
its official `.dsc`; source was read directly through Python tarfile, never
installed or executed. Recoverable reading copies are
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-initramfs-tools_0.148.4.tar.xz` and `.dsc`. This
narrows the blocker from an unknown stock-default module policy to inspecting
an actual target initrd and its build/configuration provenance. The pass stops
at that image-specific boundary as declared. The parent architecture owns
selection of concrete bootable image artifacts; no universal Debian compatibility
or machine acceptance is claimed. Claims 11–12 are independently accepted in findings-review Return 2; actual
initrd and stock-boot compatibility remain unestablished.

## Concrete Debian installer inventory — September 19

**Method:** the summary's authorized 45-minute, 128-MiB read-only image inventory;
60-second limit per retrieved file, no VM/package installation or guest-content
modification. Retrieved the official netboot initrd, its checksum manifest, the
versioned matching kernel and versioned manifest (53,027,483 bytes total, below
128 MiB). Python gzip/newc inspection read every archive member through the
trailer and verified only zero padding remained; XZ module metadata and existing
startup scripts were read, never executed. This is primary artifact inspection,
not a new runtime experiment. Stop condition reached: a concrete module inventory
and boot-time autoload path, plus an explicit missing block-driver prerequisite.

**CLM-03-03-13 — Source-backed, accepted: exact stock installer artifact
matches the inherited Debian kernel.** The official Debian installer build
`20250803+deb13u7`, amd64 netboot, contains kernel `6.12.107+deb13-amd64`, package
`6.12.107-1`, matching INV-03-01 claim 04 and INV-01-34's working Debian target.
The initrd's `etc/lsb-release` identifies this installer build and `netboot`;
`var/lib/dpkg/status` names that kernel package, while module vermagic agrees.
The separately retrieved bzImage version string identifies the same kernel and
package, with boot protocol `0x020f` and `xloadflags=0x007f`. Header inspection
is not evidence of successful loader, decompression or BSP/AP execution.

| Official build artifact | Bytes | SHA256, matched to versioned manifest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [initrd.gz](https://deb.debian.org/debian/dists/trixie/main/installer-amd64/20250803+deb13u7/images/netboot/debian-installer/amd64/initrd.gz) | 40,723,557 | `57303d157cffce3fa402301667fbc9b2280aa7372082b081e777375ae49da5f2` |
| [linux](https://deb.debian.org/debian/dists/trixie/main/installer-amd64/20250803+deb13u7/images/netboot/debian-installer/amd64/linux) | 12,142,528 | `2b2358b37674d2505350528875bb17afae2a36522a9e8a9417eaca65a7da0e08` |

The [versioned SHA256SUMS](https://deb.debian.org/debian/dists/trixie/main/installer-amd64/20250803+deb13u7/images/SHA256SUMS)
contains both hashes. The initrd was initially retrieved through `current`; its
full hash matches the versioned manifest, so the claim does not rely on that
moving alias. This is an additional concrete **installer** image case for the
same kernel, not substitution of an installer for installed-system/workload
compatibility or an exclusive support boundary.

**CLM-03-03-14 — Source-backed, accepted: MMIO/network/entropy drivers
and autoload machinery are present; virtio block is absent.** The initrd has
2,511 members. Under `usr/lib/modules/6.12.107+deb13-amd64/` it contains:

- `kernel/drivers/virtio/virtio_mmio.ko.xz`, whose module metadata includes
  `alias=acpi*:LNRO0005:*` and no module dependency;
- `kernel/drivers/net/virtio_net.ko.xz`, with `net_failover` dependency, and both
  `net_failover.ko.xz` and its `failover.ko.xz` dependency;
- `kernel/drivers/char/hw_random/virtio-rng.ko.xz`, with virtio device-4 alias;
- binary module alias/dependency indexes, plus modprobe, udevd and udevadm.

`init` mounts `/sys`, then invokes `usr/lib/debian-installer/start-udev` through
the usr-merged `/lib` path. That script starts udevd and triggers `add` events;
its actual `80-drivers.rules` loads modules from `MODALIAS`. This closes the
previously hypothetical presence of the startup loader/rule/trigger for **this
archive**. It does not establish emitted firmware resources, live driver binding,
network operation, entropy quality or the full binary alias-resolution behavior.

No archive member supplies `virtio_blk`, and its name is also absent from the
binary dependency/alias indexes. The matching kernel configuration has
`CONFIG_VIRTIO_BLK=m`, so this initrd alone cannot demonstrate immediate virtio
block availability. Its `/init` uses an initramfs installer environment rather
than mounting an installed virtio root filesystem. A netboot installer can obtain
additional components later, but the matching storage component and retrieval
path have not yet been audited. Choosing virtio-PCI does not itself add the
missing block driver. Do not modify the initrd or silently discard this negative
inventory to manufacture a stock root-mount result.

Reading copies: `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-debian-netboot-initrd.gz`,
`...-linux`, `...-versioned-SHA256SUMS`, `...-inventory.txt`; extracted script/status
reading copies use `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-initrd-` prefixes. Original image
bytes were not changed. Independent review can rebuild the entire inventory from
the versioned artifact and manifest without relying on the author's listing.
Claims 13–14 are accepted by the existing independent reviewer in Return 3.
The resulting missing-storage-component question is addressed in the following
source pass; neither pass establishes stock boot.

## Storage-component source audit method

Authorized follow-up September 19, author `/root/machine`: 30 minutes, at most
32 MiB downloaded with 60-second per-file limits. Read the official installer
component index, exact matching storage udeb and embedded component-loading
scripts; no installation, guest execution or initrd modification. Stop at a
source-backed normal module/retrieval path or its first missing prerequisite.
The concrete image inventory already reached independent acceptance; this pass
must not silently promote source inspection to observed installer operation.

**CLM-03-03-15 — Source-backed, independently accepted: the matching official storage
component supplies virtio block.** The inspected [Debian installer package index](https://deb.debian.org/debian/dists/trixie/main/debian-installer/binary-amd64/Packages.xz)
selects `scsi-modules-6.12.107+deb13-amd64-di`, version `6.12.107-1`, priority
`standard`, kernel version `6.12.107+deb13-amd64`. Its exact
[official udeb](https://deb.debian.org/debian/pool/main/l/linux-signed-amd64/scsi-modules-6.12.107+deb13-amd64-di_6.12.107-1_amd64.udeb)
(4,769,396 bytes, SHA256
`b201c50ad94ea599509ebd41afff9ea8129bb34b8edc3b1e41230813bbb90515`)
matched the index and contains `lib/modules/6.12.107+deb13-amd64/kernel/drivers/block/virtio_blk.ko.xz`.
Its module metadata names virtio device 2, has no module dependency and matches
that kernel vermagic. The package additionally depends on matching kernel-image,
scsi-core, cdrom-core and ata components; those package contents were not all
independently inventoried. A package-level dependency is not the same as the
virtio block module's own symbol dependency.

**CLM-03-03-16 — Source-backed/Inferred, independently accepted: normal component
selection can supply that driver without changing the initrd.** The image's
status records anna `1.99`, hw-detect `1.171`, and net-retriever `1.61`.
[Official anna 1.99 source](https://deb.debian.org/debian/pool/main/a/anna/anna_1.99.tar.xz),
`anna.c::choose_modules`, excludes mismatched kernel packages and marks matching
standard-priority packages for installation. Its installation pass retrieves,
SHA256-checks and unpacks selected packages. `retriever.c` passes each package's
index filename to the configured retriever. The image's actual `net-retriever`
script fetches from the configured mirror and checks index/Release consistency;
this is a source path conditional on working network, mirror configuration and
matching component availability, not observed networking or a universal unattended
installation guarantee. Installer selection/low-memory/user choices can still
change the component set.

[Disk-detect 1.171](https://deb.debian.org/debian/pool/main/h/hw-detect/disk-detect_1.171_amd64.udeb)
(21,524 bytes, SHA256
`3df7895ffd8c2d8a0e21d7119b029e3aa65faada049bdce231ee9399b641880b`, matched
to the same index) calls the image's `hw-detect` script. That script regenerates
module dependencies and invokes `update-dev` to trigger discovery after modules
become available. Its separate early `anna-install virtio-modules` heuristic is
PCI-vendor-specific and is **not** the argued path for ACPI virtio-mmio. The
standard matching `scsi-modules` selection plus device-2 alias and existing MMIO
transport supplies the relevant conditional chain. Exact runtime binding and
block operations remain implementation-validation obligations only after the
machine/interface design itself is established.

**Disposition:** the absent initial `virtio_blk` is an installer staging
prerequisite with an identified official component and ordinary retrieval path;
it is not a source-level kill of ACPI virtio-mmio, nor evidence that the stock
installer already boots here. Do not present the compressed initrd as a standalone
virtio-root initramfs. The budget stopped at this source chain; downloaded bytes
were 4,943,512 in this pass, below 32 MiB, with no live fetch or VM handle.
Reading copies: `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-debian-udeb-Packages.xz`,
`...-scsi-modules.udeb`, `...-disk-detect.udeb`, `...-anna_1.99.tar.xz`, plus script
copies from the unchanged initrd. Claims 15–16 are independently accepted in findings-review Return 4; that
acceptance covers source facts and the conditional chain only.

**Retained source snapshot:** the exact source/package/image artifacts are retained
outside Git in the [checksummed manifest](/Users/liaozping/orca/natemu-raw/track03-source-audit-20260919/manifest.json).
The manifest records copy integrity; the official URLs and package/image identity
above remain the provenance. These retained downloads are source evidence, not
executed experiment artifacts.

## No-PIC reference-clock source pass

September 19, author `/root/machine`: resume the recorded 45-minute source-only
trace of Linux v6.8/v6.12 firmware no-PIC selection, ACPI PM discovery and early
TSC/LAPIC calibration. Read upstream source only; at most 8 MiB new downloads,
60 seconds per file. Stop at a concrete source chain or the first missing
discovery/delay/calibration premise. No guest execution or implementation.

**CLM-03-03-17 — Source-backed, independently accepted: no-PIC firmware does not
eliminate the earliest legacy timer accesses.** In both
[Linux v6.8 setup.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/setup.c)
and [v6.12 setup.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/setup.c),
`tsc_early_init` precedes `acpi_boot_table_init`, `early_acpi_boot_init` and
`acpi_boot_init`. In the ordinary native-platform path, a Haswell-derived profile
without usable frequency leaves/MSR calibration reaches `quick_pit_calibrate`;
`legacy_pic` initially points at the default PIC. The code therefore can access
ports 0x61, 0x43 and 0x42 before ACPI can establish the later no-PIC state.
This is a boot-order finding, not a claim that a full PIT must be implemented.
A machine omitting it must define safe absent-port reads/writes and show bounded
calibration failure; firmware flags alone are not that argument. Sources:
[v6.8 TSC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c),
[v6.12 TSC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c),
[v6.8 PIC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/i8259.c),
[v6.12 PIC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/i8259.c).

The firmware distinction is material. Clearing MADT `PCAT_COMPAT` allows the PIC
probe; it does not itself set `null_legacy_pic`. `probe_8259A` writes interrupt
masks and replaces the PIC only when the returned master mask differs from the
probe value. Conversely setting PCAT_COMPAT skips the probe and retains the PIC
assumption. The hardware-reduced ACPI branch directly sets the null PIC and
changes timer/pre-vector initialization hooks; it is a different platform
contract, not an interchangeable bit for a conventional PM-timer machine.
Sources: [v6.8 ACPI boot](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/acpi/boot.c),
[v6.12 ACPI boot](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/acpi/boot.c),
and the PIC sources above. This pass does not establish that hardware-reduced
ACPI permits the proposed FADT PM-register contract.

**CLM-03-03-18 — Source-backed/Inferred, independently accepted: a PM reference can
support the later calibration chain, but does not independently pace the LAPIC
calibration loop.** Both inspected kernels' `acpi_parse_fadt` discover the PM
I/O port from the extended FADT timer address (requiring System I/O address
space), falling back to the legacy field when that address is zero. Both exact
inherited package configs have `CONFIG_X86_PM_TIMER=y`. `acpi_pm_read_early`
returns zero without a discovered port, otherwise a verified read masked to
24 bits. Sources: ACPI boot above,
[v6.8 PM header](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/include/linux/acpi_pmtmr.h),
[v6.12 PM header](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/include/linux/acpi_pmtmr.h);
exact package/config identities remain those in INV-03-01 claim 04.

The already reviewed TSC fallback measures the reference across five `udelay`
calls. Their nominal 50 ms is not an independent timing guarantee: when early
TSC calibration failed, `tsc_enable_sched_clock` did not select the TSC delay
implementation; the default delay function is an instruction loop. The comment
naming `tsc_early_delay_calibrate` is not a call or proof of a successful early
calibration. Sources: TSC above,
[v6.8 delay.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/lib/delay.c),
[v6.12 delay.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/lib/delay.c).
The reference ratio can work without that loop lasting exactly 50 ms, but the
emulator must supply progressing, consistently related TSC/PM reads and an
interval suitable for the source's sampling/wrap constraints. That relationship
is an unresolved machine-time premise, not a demonstrated timing result.

With `tsc_khz` successfully established and a progressing TSC, both
[v6.8 APIC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/apic/apic.c)
and [v6.12 APIC](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/apic/apic.c)
`calibrate_APIC_clock` can pace their polling by TSC instead of jiffies from a
global clockevent. The handler samples the LAPIC countdown, TSC and PM reference;
`calibrate_by_pmtimer` validates or rescales the sampled countdown. PM sampling
is not the wait-loop termination condition. If TSC calibration fails and no
global clockevent advances jiffies, simply providing a readable PM timer does
not release that loop. A concrete counterexample is zero `tsc_khz`, no global
tick, and an otherwise correctly advancing PM counter. Countdown semantics,
frequency, wrap and actual local interrupt delivery remain device obligations.

**Stop and consequence:** stopped at the first missing machine premise: explicit
absent legacy-port semantics before ACPI discovery, coupled to a progressing
TSC/PM clock and a usable fallback sampling interval. The trace strengthens the
conditional no-PIT alternative but does not select it. It also identifies the
LAPIC bootstrap dependency that a PM-only register listing would miss. No source
finding contradicts INV-03-01 claim 06's conditional alternative. No stock boot,
firmware compliance, vendor-patch coverage or timer implementation is accepted.
The 24 source downloads total 494636 bytes, below 8 MiB;
all completed within the 45-minute pass, with no live fetch/VM handle. Reading
copies are in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-clock`, named by version and path.

Claims 17–18 and their conditional dependency/counterexample arguments are
independently accepted in findings-review Return 5; no complete timer or firmware
contract is accepted.

## Existing legacy-device reuse comparison

September 19, author `/root/machine`: 45-minute source-only comparison of pinned
QEMU legacy PIT/PIC/RTC reuse against the no-PIT PM-reference route. At most
8 MiB official source downloads, 60 seconds per file. Inspect device composition,
initialization dependencies, clock/event/interrupt boundaries and Linux discovery.
Stop with a provisional machine choice on adaptation surface or one decisive
unresolved comparison. No compiled implementation, VM or performance experiment.

**CLM-03-03-19 — Source-backed, independently accepted: the legacy clock/interrupt
register models are reusable host objects, not a new device implementation.**
In QEMU v10.0.0, [`microvm.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/microvm.c)
constructs an ISA bus and connects the software i8259 PIC, i8254 PIT and
MC146818 RTC alongside virtio-mmio devices. Their object APIs depend on QEMU's
QOM/qdev, ISA I/O regions, clock/timer service and IRQ callbacks, not an emulated
PCI transaction for every device access. The inspected software PIT creates one
virtual-clock timer for channel 0 and a four-port I/O region; its exported
initializer connects its output to an ISA IRQ or explicit alternate callback.
Sources: [`i8254.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/timer/i8254.c),
[`i8254.h`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/include/hw/timer/i8254.h),
[`i8259.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/intc/i8259.c),
[`isa-bus.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/isa/isa-bus.c).
This supports retaining the existing host device framework already needed for
serial/virtio, rather than extracting or reimplementing each state machine.
It is not a build or ABI-portability result.

PIT reuse must include port **0x61**, not just ports 0x40–0x43. QEMU's
[`pcspk.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/audio/pcspk.c)
register model reads PIT channel 2 output/gate and writes its gate. Its realization
registers the port regardless of whether an audio card is configured; voice
operations are guarded. [`pc.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/pc.c)
links that object to the PIT. Thus a headless candidate can reuse the register
behavior without requiring sound output. Merely copying microvm's PIT creation
call would omit this inspected PC wiring and would not establish Linux's
channel-2 calibration behavior.

The [`MC146818 model`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/rtc/mc146818rtc.c)
initializes calendar state, supplies CMOS ports 0x70/0x71, creates periodic/update
timers using the configured `rtc_clock`, and routes its IRQ through the ISA bus
or an intercept callback. It brings register update, alarm/periodic and
read-side-effect behavior already implemented upstream; it also brings reset,
notifier, clock selection and machine-object dependencies. In
[Linux v6.12 RTC code](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/rtc.c),
CMOS reads/writes use those ports, `mach_get_cmos_time` calls `mc146818_get_time`,
and platform `rtc_cmos` registration remains available when no matching PNP
entry exists and firmware has not disabled the legacy RTC. This is a concrete
wallclock interface reuse route, not proof that every selected guest initialization
callback or RTC configuration has been traced.

**CLM-03-03-20 — Inferred provisional choice, independently reviewed: retain the legacy
clock island for the initial machine.** Select a conventional, non-hardware-
reduced ACPI PC-compatible clock/interrupt contract with host-resident QEMU
software PIC, PIT, port-0x61 register model and RTC. Keep the current modern
virtio-mmio transport candidate and direct BSP/AP boot direction; adding these
ISA objects does not require silently switching transport or installed-image
scope. Reuse conventional PC ACPI/table construction and its fixed-register
models as the firmware reference. Do not copy microvm's FADT unchanged: its
[`acpi-microvm.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/acpi-microvm.c)
sets hardware-reduced ACPI and describes GED sleep registers. The conventional
[`acpi-build.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/acpi-build.c)
instead constructs PM event/control/timer I/O fields and RTC century information
from machine objects. Their required objects/registers must be selected together.

| Alternative | Reuse and remaining adaptation | Provisional disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Host QEMU PIC/PIT/0x61/RTC with conventional ACPI | Reuse counters, register state, calibration-visible reads, IRQ outputs and CMOS wallclock; adapt existing host port-I/O, common virtual clock and interrupt callback boundary | Current placement hypothesis: removes the specified absent-PIT bootstrap obligations and supplies a global-tick fallback; total captive-boundary adaptation cost remains unranked |
| No PIT/PIC, ACPI PM-reference route | Fewer device objects, but retains absent-port semantics before firmware discovery, no-PIC selection, usable uncalibrated delay interval and TSC-before-LAPIC bootstrap proof; RTC/wallclock still needs a choice | Retained alternative; total adaptation/code/performance cost is unranked against the legacy-device candidate |
| Conventional PC/PIIX source as a component/wiring reference | Reuses more complete chipset/ACPI/interrupt wiring, but also carries PCI southbridge, board/firmware initialization and devices beyond this clock choice | Reference/provider option, not an end-to-end runtime fallback; each selected component still needs the direct-boot/EL1 boundary contract |

The preference is an **unverified placement/cost hypothesis**. Existing device
implementations can replace some new register/calibration semantics, while their
host/shim transaction and timing adapters add work. No matched captive-boundary
comparison establishes lower total semantic work, source size or execution cost;
the no-PIT alternative remains unranked on those measures. Linux's
[v6.12 PIT initialization](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/i8253.c)
can register a global PIT clockevent when APIC calibration needs it, and stops it
when it is not needed. The reviewed TSC/LAPIC sources already provide PIT-based
paths. These facts make the retained clock island a stronger starting point for
calibration than merely exposing a PM counter, without claiming calibration
success under arbitrary host scheduling.

**Placement and exact adaptation boundary — Hypothesis.** Keep these low-rate
legacy objects in the host VM worker with the other reused devices; do not port
their QOM/timer implementation into the EL1 shim. Shim port-I/O accesses become
ordered, exact-once transactions into the host I/O address space. Channel-0 IRQ0,
RTC IRQ8 and PIC cascade/acknowledgement must retain conventional routing and
mask/EOI behavior; the existing proposed shim IOAPIC/LAPIC/event layer receives
identified IRQ line transitions and PIC interrupt acknowledgement results.
Do not deliver PIC and IOAPIC copies as independent guest interrupts: routing,
ExtINT and acknowledgement ownership are one contract. Global/device timer
callbacks retain lifetime/cancellation gates before entering that layer.
Timer-intensive workloads may pay host exits; no latency or overhead claim is
made and a later measured reason could move a device closer to the vCPU.

PIT uses `QEMU_CLOCK_VIRTUAL`; RTC uses the configurable `rtc_clock`. They do not
automatically share the proposed shim TSC or its sleep/pause policy. Adapt one
explicit clock service before asserting frequency coherence. Keeping the PIT
removes the no-PIT loop-bootstrap dependency, not the shared-time, scheduling,
interrupt-ordering or cancellation obligations. Likewise source reuse does not
establish macOS confinement or block durability.

**Historical stop and consequence:** the pass reached the requested provisional
clock-island choice. It stops before pretending to have a complete firmware
bundle. The subsequent claims 21–24 inspected a concrete conventional
FADT/PM/shutdown provider and IRQ0/SCI/PIC-ack wiring consistent with these objects,
comparing existing PIIX4 versus a smaller reusable ACPI register provider. This
must include direct-boot register initialization that ordinary firmware would
otherwise perform, and must not assume the entire PC board initializer can run
unchanged. If a smaller composition requires more custom correctness work than
whole PC-substrate reuse, prefer that existing substrate rather than deleting
necessary semantics. Direct-boot RSDP/table placement remains part of that step.

Reading copies: `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-machine-reuse`, QEMU v10.0.0 plus the
explicit v6.12 Linux files. One guessed `hw/timer/i8254_common.h` URL returned
404; the actual public/internal headers were then obtained. A download-script
syntax error occurred before that script issued requests and was corrected.
The 20 successful downloads total 374,756 bytes, within the 8 MiB/45-minute limit; no live
fetch or execution handle remains. No downloaded binary or source was executed.

Findings-review Return 6 accepts claim 19 and choice 20's provisional adaptation
rationale. QEMU v10.0.0 device sources and the separately pinned translator are
distinct provenance; this is not cross-version build/ABI compatibility evidence.

## Conventional ACPI/interrupt bundle source pass

September 19, author `/root/machine`: resume recorded 45-minute source-only
FADT/PM/shutdown/IRQ0/SCI/PIC-ack and direct-boot initialization comparison.
At most 8 MiB upstream source, 60 seconds per file. Inspect QEMU v10.0.0 PC/PIIX
composition and linked firmware/guest consumers; prefer coherent existing
substrate if a smaller assembly adds custom semantics. Stop at a concrete
provisional bundle or first unresolved initialization coupling. No runtime.

**CLM-03-03-21 — Source-backed, independently accepted: the PC/PIIX PM register and
firmware bundle has an explicit initialization dependency.** QEMU v10.0.0
[`PIIX4 PM`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/acpi/piix4.c)
`pm_io_space_update` derives its 64-byte PM I/O base from PCI configuration 0x40
(masked to 64-byte alignment), and enables decode only when configuration 0x80
bit 0 is set. Realization creates the region disabled; reset clears that enable.
The PM base exposed to the conventional FADT builder is therefore state produced
by initialization, not a permanently enabled constant. In the versioned
[SeaBIOS rel-1.16.3 PCI initializer](https://github.com/coreboot/seabios/blob/rel-1.16.3/src/fw/pciinit.c),
`piix4_pm_config_setup` sets SCI line 9, programs PMBASE, enables PM decode and
sets up SMBus decode; `piix4_pm_setup` registers the PM timer at base+8.
This independently identifies work normally performed by firmware. The SeaBIOS
version is a source reference, not a claim that this precise firmware ships with
every inspected QEMU build.

With SMM disabled and `smm-compat=false`, QEMU initializes PM1 control as
`acpi_only`; its reset sets SCI_EN. The conventional FADT builder publishes a
zero SMI command/ACPI-enable command when SMM is disabled. These are explicit
native ACPI-mode paths: **do not enable SMM or assume a guest SMI handler** to
make direct boot work. Sources: PIIX4 above,
[ACPI core](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/acpi/core.c),
[PC FADT builder](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/acpi-build.c).
Disabling SMM does not itself turn on the separate PCI PM I/O decode bit.

**CLM-03-03-22 — Source-backed, independently accepted: conventional shutdown has a
matching AML/register path.** The PC DSDT builder emits `_S5` with PM1 sleep type
zero. `acpi_pm_cnt_write` treats SLP_EN with that type as a guest shutdown
request. Linux v6.12 `acpi_power_off` calls `acpi_enter_sleep_state(S5)`; the
legacy ACPICA sleep implementation writes PM1 control first with sleep type,
then with sleep enable. Sources: PC builder and ACPI core above,
[Linux sleep.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/acpi/sleep.c),
[ACPICA hwsleep.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/acpi/acpica/hwsleep.c).
This gives a reusable unmodified-guest shutdown interface, conditional on complete
valid tables and enabled registers. The host lifecycle owner must convert the
model's request into coordinated VM termination; it is not a storage-flush or
successful guest-shutdown observation. Initial direction omits S3/S4 advertising
using the existing model/table settings, consistent with lifecycle scope; it
must not leave unsupported sleep methods advertised accidentally.

**CLM-03-03-23 — Source-backed, independently accepted: legacy IRQ wiring includes
routing and acknowledgement semantics, not just vector injection.**
[`PIIX`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/isa/piix.c)
connects PM SCI output to ISA IRQ9. The ordinary
[`gsi_handler`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/x86-common.c)
forwards an ISA line to both PIC and IOAPIC. In the software
[`IOAPIC`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/intc/ioapic.c),
input IRQ0 maps to redirection entry/GSI2; the
[MADT builder](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/acpi-common.c)
can emit the corresponding IRQ0→GSI2 override and sets PCAT_COMPAT while PIC is
present. Table and device mapping must agree exactly. Linux's
[SCI setup](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/acpi/boot.c)
defaults absent explicit SCI override to level/low and establishes its routing;
copying a numeric SCI field without the trigger/polarity contract is insufficient.

IOAPIC ExtINT delivery obtains a vector by `pic_read_irq`; ordinary PIC delivery
also calls that acknowledgement function after local-APIC acceptance checks in
[`x86-cpu.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/x86-cpu.c).
The IOAPIC's level/remote-IRR behavior and EOI broadcast can retrigger a still
asserted line. A load-bearing call-order caveat: `ioapic_service` calls
`ioapic_entry_parse` before testing `!info.masked`, and parsing an ExtINT entry
calls `pic_read_irq`. Thus unchanged helper reuse does **not** prove that PIC
acknowledgement occurs only after final LAPIC admission. The adapter must inspect
and control every such parse/delivery path so it cannot acknowledge speculatively
or twice; a parsed vector is not a side-effect-free query. This is an explicit
new bridge obligation, not an assertion that upstream QEMU's own machine behavior
is defective. Local EOI also requires an ordered return path to the owning IOAPIC. The upstream software IOAPIC emits
MSI-format address/data through its configured address space; that is a concrete
adaptation seam, not automatically a native GIC interrupt.

**CLM-03-03-24 — Source-backed/Inferred, independently accepted: direct boot must
finalize the reused table build, not copy unlinked blobs.** QEMU's
[`bios-linker-loader.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/acpi/bios-linker-loader.c)
emits allocation, pointer relocation and checksum commands; the conventional
ACPI builder supplies table/RSDP/linker blobs and rebuilds them using current
machine state. SeaBIOS's
[`romfile_loader.c`](https://github.com/coreboot/seabios/blob/rel-1.16.3/src/fw/romfile_loader.c)
executes those command classes before guest use. A direct loader must perform
equivalent checked relocation/checksum work against its chosen reserved guest
physical ranges. Linux v6.12
[`bootparam.h`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/bootparam.h)
contains `acpi_rsdp_addr`, and the x86 default root-pointer accessors in ACPI boot
read/write that field. This is a concrete handoff route; complete address-map,
reservation, table validity and exact vendor startup remain unproved.

### Provisional bundle and placement revision

**Hypothesis, independently reviewed as a provisional composition:** use the existing i440FX/PIIX-family PC
substrate and conventional ACPI builder as the provider bundle, with software
PIC/PIT/0x61/RTC, the PIIX4 PM function, and software IOAPIC retained in the host
worker. This preference advances choice 20: retain the integrated chipset/PCI
configuration and table machinery instead of creating a standalone synthetic
PM-register device with hand-authored matching AML. The inspected
[`pc_piix.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/v10.0.0/hw/i386/pc_piix.c)
and PIIX sources supply those object connections. The board name alone does not
mean its ROM boot, CPU execution or all optional devices run unchanged.
The existing direct-boot/AP-mailbox path remains; modern virtio-mmio remains the
initial candidate and requires its ACPI descriptions to be incorporated in the
same final DSDT/resource map. No silent switch to virtio-PCI or image narrowing.

**Placement revision:** keep **IOAPIC in the host**, superseding the earlier
proposed shim IOAPIC placement. Its register state, GSI remap, remote-IRR,
PIC ExtINT acknowledgement and callback scheduling then remain within the reused
host models. The shim still owns per-vCPU LAPIC/event state and local timer
execution. Adapt the IOAPIC MSI-format output to a validated lifetime-tagged
LAPIC-delivery request; carry level EOI back to the host owner. Adapt PIC legacy
output/acceptance/acknowledgement at that same event boundary. One authoritative
routing state determines which path is eligible; do not inject both PIC and
IOAPIC copies blindly. This is less new controller semantics than extracting
the IOAPIC into EL1, while still requiring a reviewed cross-boundary delivery/
EOI protocol. It adds host MMIO/EOI transactions; performance remains unmeasured.

The proposed direct-loader initialization sequence is now concrete:

1. Construct and reset the selected host PC/PIIX objects with SMM off and the
   native ACPI-only PM mode; disable unsupported S3/S4 advertisement. Initialize
   topology/APIC IDs consistently with the CPU profile and mailbox table.
2. Allocate nonoverlapping PM/SMBus and device I/O ranges, apply the ordinary
   device configuration write paths for PMBASE/decode and SCI9, and initialize
   enabled PCI/ISA routing as required. Do this **after reset and before the table
   snapshot**, rather than changing fields that bypass model update callbacks.
3. Build ACPI from those actual object properties, preserving the IRQ0→GSI2
   mapping, PIC presence, SCI and RTC information. Apply linker allocations,
   relocations and checksums in reserved guest ranges, with virtio-mmio and AP
   mailbox descriptions from the same machine description. Publish RSDP through
   boot parameters only after the final table graph is coherent.
4. Start the BSP through the accepted direct-boot ABI. Subsequent guest PM
   configuration writes, timer accesses, IRQ acknowledgements and shutdown use
   the same host models; a supported reset must rerun the required initialization
   sequence and never retain stale table/device lifetime references.

This is a source-derived preparation recipe, not executable code or boot evidence.
**Stop condition reached:** a concrete provider/routing bundle is identified,
with the direct-boot coupling made explicit. Its first remaining architectural
seam is the host PIC/IOAPIC ↔ shim LAPIC admission/EOI/acknowledgement protocol,
including halted-vCPU wake and level retrigger. Whole-substrate reuse reduces
new device semantics but does not prove the loader's completed table graph,
all enabled PCI/device initialization, memory-map placement or event protocol.
Findings Return 7 reviewed those boundaries in the stated source/conditional
scope; the later interface work follows below.

## Host device authority and interrupt handshake

September 19, author `/root/machine`: authorized 45-minute source/protocol pass
on single host device ownership and the host PIC/IOAPIC↔shim LAPIC bridge.
Inspect the selected QEMU pin, at most 4 MiB new sources and 60 seconds per file.
Trace fixed/ExtINT offers, PIC acknowledgement, EOI/retrigger, paused/HLT wake
and coordinator-dependent DMA. Stop at a conditional interface or precise
unresolved ownership cycle. No implementation, compilation or VM execution.

**CLM-03-03-27 — Source-backed, independently accepted in Return 9: delivery classes have different
acknowledgement and EOI rules.** Intel SDM Vol. 3A revision 092 §§13.5.1, 13.8.1,
13.8.4 and 13.11.2 describe ExtINT's external-controller INTA vector and distinguish
it from fixed interrupts queued through LAPIC IRR/ISR. ExtINT bypasses those
registers; it is not completed by the fixed-interrupt LAPIC EOI path. Local LINT
ExtINT is level-sensitive, whereas an MSI-format ExtINT notification is edge-
sensitive; the bridge must retain the originating route/class rather than erase
it into a vector integer. The manual describes one system ExtINT source and one
processor configured for LVT ExtINT; this sampled normal configuration is not
permission to silently ignore invalid or changed guest programming.
[Primary Intel manual](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).

At the selected QEMU pin, `pic_read_irq` mutates PIC acknowledgement state through
`pic_intack`, including cascade/auto-EOI/spurious behavior. `apic_eoi` removes
the highest in-service vector and conditionally broadcasts EOI for a level vector
unless directed-EOI suppression is set. `ioapic_eoi_broadcast` clears matching
remote-IRR and may service an asserted line again. These are operations to
preserve, not independent idempotent notifications. Sources:
[hw/intc/i8259.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/i8259.c);
[hw/intc/apic.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/apic.c);
[hw/intc/ioapic.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/ioapic.c);

There is also a specific adaptation boundary: the inspected IOAPIC parses ExtINT
by calling `pic_read_irq` before its mask check, then emits an MSI-format message;
`apic_bus_deliver` handles its ExtINT case through the common `apic_set_irq` tail.
That unchanged path is not source proof of the Intel distinction above or of
our deferred cross-boundary IACK. This observation concerns reuse into the new
bridge, not a blanket audit verdict on upstream QEMU. A pure routing parse and
an explicit external-acknowledgement transaction must replace that side-effecting
parse at every enabled call site before claiming this protocol realized.

**CLM-03-03-28 — Inferred conditional interface, independently accepted in Return 9: one host owner
with typed transactions can separate device state from guest event acceptance.**
Select one host device-owner thread for the reused register/timer/IRQ/IACK/EOI
callbacks. vCPU host requests enqueue to it; no vCPU thread invokes a QEMU
register callback directly. Timer expiry and external backend arrival enqueue
work for that owner instead of calling device state concurrently. Translation
jobs consume copied inputs and have no direct guest-RAM/device authority.
The lockless PM-timer flag permits upstream BQL-free reads but does not override
this adapter's single-owner rule. PM reads still need the separately specified
shared clock/lifetime contract; single ownership alone does not establish time.

### Message ownership and progress

Every queued transaction names VM/device lifetime, request serial, source class,
route generation and vCPU identity where applicable. The owner records committed
results in the bounded request slot until its consumer acknowledges receipt;
transport duplication returns that result, never reruns IACK, EOI or MMIO. Slots
are not reused until callback/reply ownership is drained, with namespace wrap
handled under the existing lifetime protocol. Reset/teardown cancels unperformed
work and drains committed work before replacing device identity; committed
controller effects are never casually rolled back.

The host owner does not synchronously wait for a vCPU to execute or for the RAM
coordinator to quiesce observers. A request needing guest-RAM authority obtains
that grant asynchronously **before entering** a callback that can access RAM.
While awaiting the grant, the owner continues independent IRQ/control/arrival
work. Callbacks under a grant must finish bounded local work or submit asynchronous
backend work and return; they cannot retain the grant while waiting for the
backend. A request already belonging to DRAIN_SERVICE borrows its existing
authority, never acquires another epoch. Completion requiring RAM commit queues
for the corresponding grant and publishes data/status before its IRQ offer.

This is a selected callback-admission discipline, not proof that every reused
QEMU callback satisfies it. The whitelist for no-RAM service initially consists
of inspected controller/PM/RTC/PIT register and event work after all outward
MSI/event delivery is intercepted by the bridge. Any unexpected guest-RAM path
requires the granted path; silently entering the callback and then blocking on
an unowned grant is forbidden. Device-callback reachability remains a required
inventory. No generic C-stack suspension framework is assumed.

### Concrete interrupt transactions

| Operation | Host/shim ordering and effect ownership |
| --- | --- |
| Fixed-vector offer | Host source model admits the event under its mask/route rules and retains its pending ownership. The owner emits a typed vector/trigger/destination offer. Shim admits it to the appropriate LAPIC pending state; acknowledgement means queued in that controller, not that IF/PPR allowed guest delivery. The shim later performs normal priority, IRR→ISR and architectural-entry decisions. Repeated transport messages do not enqueue again; coalescing follows LAPIC semantics, not arbitrary queue dropping. |
| Legacy PIC/LINT ExtINT | Host reports the current line state and route identity without calling `pic_read_irq`. Shim considers it at a legal guest boundary using its actual IF, interrupt-shadow and local route state. Only when it commits to an INTA attempt does it reserve that vCPU's event transition and send a unique IACK transaction; guest execution/event selection for that vCPU pauses until the result. Owner executes `pic_read_irq` once, retains its result, and returns the vector including legitimate PIC spurious cases. Shim consumes it through external interrupt delivery, not fixed IRR/ISR insertion. No guest-visible local state may change during the reserved transition; native notifications only queue more work. |
| IOAPIC ExtINT | Pure parse checks the selected route/mask before creating a typed external-interrupt offer. The same external IACK ownership applies; do not reuse the eager vector-fetch parser or silently treat the result as a fixed interrupt. Once the routing attempt is admitted, later register changes affect future attempts; any cancel before IACK must prove the controller effect did not occur. IOAPIC-route bookkeeping for this adapted mode requires the explicit model seam, not an assumed fixed-mode remote-IRR rule. |
| LAPIC EOI for a fixed level interrupt | Shim applies its local ISR/TMR/EOI-suppression semantics exactly once and, when required, publishes a uniquely identified vector EOI to the host. Guest-visible EOI completion is ordered after host consumption for the initial conservative protocol. Owner applies `ioapic_eoi_broadcast` once, which may create another offer if a line remains asserted. It never waits for that new offer to reach the guest before acknowledging the EOI. |
| PIC EOI | Guest PIC port writes are ordinary exact-once host register transactions. They are not synthesized from a LAPIC EOI for ExtINT; auto-EOI and cascade behavior remain in the PIC model. |
| Poll/configuration read | Pure state observation cannot perform an IACK merely to discover a possible vector. Side-effecting device reads remain explicitly typed register accesses; do not relabel them as pure polling. |

The IACK reservation is not a host wait while holding a local APIC lock. The
vCPU publishes a retained boundary/service record and releases execution authority;
its host thread can service notifications. An actual guest exception during
subsequent vector delivery uses the inherited exception-priority/delivery path
without repeating IACK. IF-clear or interrupt-shadow-blocked ExtINT never starts
IACK. A native wake/GIC notification never itself authorizes guest delivery.

Destination routing must also be coherent. APIC ID/logical destination/mode and
arbitration-relevant state cannot be copied once at boot and then trusted forever.
The proposed initial bridge orders routing-relevant guest updates through the
same owner, with per-vCPU route epochs; the shim does not complete such a local
transition until the owner has published its new directory entry. Incoming offers
carry the ordered epoch and are consumed only after preceding local updates.
For fixed physical delivery the selected APIC-ID map suffices; logical/broadcast
and lowest-priority arbitration require their own complete state/selection rules.
The pinned `apic_bus_deliver` LOWPRI branch itself contains an arbitration TODO,
so copying it is not a complete lowest-priority architecture argument. Those
modes are **unresolved required coverage**, not excluded guest features.

### Wake, backpressure and adversarial traces

A halted/paused vCPU has a durable pending-event mailbox plus a monotonically
advancing notification sequence. The shim/host wait path publishes its sleeping
state, rereads pending state/sequence under the chosen release/acquire protocol,
and sleeps only when no work raced that publication. Arrival records pending
work before issuing wake. Repeated wake signals may coalesce; pending work cannot.
Masking may keep work pending without guest delivery. Queue capacity reserves
control/IACK/EOI replies separately from ordinary device payloads so a full data
queue cannot prevent an already admitted event transaction from completing.
An exact native memory-order/wait primitive is still a platform premise.

- A masked IOAPIC entry is inspected repeatedly: pure parse produces no IACK;
  PIC ISR/IRR is unchanged by observation. An eventual eligible external attempt
  obtains one IACK serial and cannot acknowledge twice after duplicate replies.
- A level source remains asserted while guest writes EOI: owner clears remote-IRR,
  forms a new offer and replies to the old EOI without waiting for acceptance of
  that offer. The guest can resume, so delivery does not depend on its own blocked
  EOI completing a second event first.
- A vCPU is paused by the memory coordinator while a device completion needs RAM:
  owner queues the grant request and continues controller/IACK/EOI control service.
  It does not sit inside an ungranted DMA callback waiting for the coordinator.
  Once granted, RAM commit precedes completion interrupt publication.
- A line arrives as HLT sleep is armed: either the final pending/sequence recheck
  sees it or the published sleeping state causes wake. The wake is a prompt to
  reconsider x86 eligibility, not an injected architectural vector.

**Stop and limits:** this supplies a conditional ownership/progress interface for
fixed physical delivery, normal single-source external acknowledgement, EOI and
owner/coordinator interactions. No healthy vCPU must run guest code merely to
let the host owner finish an admitted controller operation. The remaining
precise controller seam is adapting IOAPIC ExtINT's eager-ack parser/bookkeeping
and completing routing/arbitration for all enabled modes. Callback RAM-reachability,
local state/wait ordering, x86 exception delivery and shared-clock behavior remain
named premises; no full interrupt-controller or machine acceptance follows.

Source reading copies: `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-irq-protocol`, 5 files, 74,719 bytes at the selected pin; Intel revision 092 reading copy retained from the earlier audit. Completed within 45 minutes/4 MiB; no live handles or execution.

**Selection limit at the execution boundary:** host device reuse is a provisional
way to reduce new register/firmware semantics, not a final selection of QEMU as
the whole runtime. Captive in-VM execution is the candidate's performance
mechanism. Host register/EOI/IACK transactions, mirrored routing state and RAM
coordination are new costs and correctness boundaries absent from unchanged
QEMU's in-process calls. The bridge must justify both its semantics and eventual
cost against alternatives; upstream model reuse does not transfer that proof.

**Historical closure scope, completed in claims 29–30:** one bounded
60-minute architecture-contract pass consolidated the whole machine interface: boot/table/topology state, all
enabled interrupt delivery modes and acknowledgements, shared TSC/device/RTC
time (including MSR writes), lifecycle/shutdown, storage completion/durability,
and the macOS containment authority. It must distinguish selected normative
rules from unsatisfied platform/source premises and later implementation
conformance checks, and stop with a reviewable complete conditional contract or
the first load-bearing unresolved choice plus its smallest resolution. This is
not authorization to repeat unbounded device-helper samples or run a VM. The completed bounded pass and its stopping condition follow below.
