**CLM-03-03-29 — Source-backed clock seam and conditional selected time policy.**
SDK 26.4 `Hypervisor.framework/Headers/hv_vcpu.h`,
`hv_vcpu_set_vtimer_offset`, explicitly documents
`CNTVCT_EL0 = mach_absolute_time() - vtimer_offset`. Its
[Apple API page](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/hypervisor/hv_vcpu_set_vtimer_offset(_:_:))
identifies the offset with CNTVOFF_EL2; the stronger host-clock equality is the
SDK declaration, not inferred from that shorter web text. SDK
`usr/include/mach/mach_time.h` declares `mach_timebase_info` conversion and
contrasts `mach_continuous_time` as advancing during sleep. These are source
contracts on the SDK 26.4/M4 target, not new observations of all hosts.

**Selected revision of the earlier time hypothesis:** use active host time,
excluding host sleep, and freeze all machine clocks during an explicit completed
VM pause. This deliberately replaces the earlier elapsed-host-sleep proposal.
No physical-wallclock synchronization or real-time interrupt latency is promised.
An independently running vCPU, a HLT vCPU and a preempted vCPU observe the same
progressing VM time; stopping one vCPU is not a VM pause. An ordinary memory-coordination park is not an explicit VM pause and does not
freeze time, even if all vCPUs temporarily participate. Host sleep freezes this
virtual machine's elapsed time, rather than manufacturing missed sleep ticks.

Use one VM epoch with boot absolute tick `A0`, accumulated completed-pause ticks
`P`, and the SDK timebase rational. In RUNNING, virtual elapsed ticks are
`V = A - A0 - P`; in PAUSED they are the captured `Vpause`. Host owner reads
`A = mach_absolute_time()`. Each shim reads `A = CNTVCT_EL0 + O`, where `O` is
that vCPU's successfully installed, recorded HVF offset. Keep `O` fixed through
ordinary operation; do not use counter-offset changes to implement guest TSC
writes. Wide intermediate arithmetic and one defined rounding rule are shared,
not separately estimated per vCPU. Failed offset/timebase setup fails VM creation.
No assumption about scheduling affinity is needed by the documented equality.

| Interface | Normative behavior and interaction |
| --- | --- |
| TSC | One fixed VM-wide frequency, chosen explicitly with the CPU profile, maps `V` to a base TSC; per-vCPU architectural write offsets apply modulo 64 bits. RDTSC, RDTSCP and IA32_TSC RDMSR all use that same expression. WRMSR updates only the addressed architectural offset at its instruction commit boundary; it does not change PM/PIT/RTC time or another CPU's TSC. All advertised adjustment/deadline MSRs need corresponding profile semantics; no accidental extra feature is inferred. |
| Devices | PIT, PM timer, RTC progression and shim LAPIC countdown use rational conversions of this same `V`, with each model's divider, wrap, latch and pending/coalescing rules. Replace reused models' clock binding explicitly; stock QEMU virtual-clock globals do not establish this bridge. Deadline callbacks carry lifetime and timer-arm generation, recheck current virtual time, and never advance device time merely because a host callback fired. |
| RTC | Initialize its calendar from one host UTC sample at boot, then progress from `V`; guest RTC writes update calendar state according to the model. Host wallclock corrections and host sleep do not jump the guest calendar. The guest may independently synchronize via its network. This is an explicit virtual-clock policy, not a claim to track civil time through host sleep. |
| Pause | Request a whole-VM coordination boundary. Until every relevant participant is stopped, time still progresses. At the exclusive boundary capture `Vpause`, finish or retain admitted backend effects, and freeze device/time admission. On resume publish the updated `P` and timer generation/state before releasing any participant. Configuration is immutable during RUNNING; this avoids a torn epoch across vCPUs. Backend arrivals during pause may be retained, but do not mutate guest RAM or deliver guest events until admitted after resume. |
| HLT and delayed host scheduling | HLT does not stop clocks. Native timer wakeups only prompt rechecking virtual deadlines and x86 eligibility. A late callback processes model-defined expiry/coalescing, not an unbounded synthetic tick loop. Timers waking a paused VM cannot implicitly resume it. |
| Local deadline writes | If the CPU profile enables TSC-deadline mode, recompute eligibility when either deadline or relevant architectural TSC offset changes; past deadlines follow the selected APIC semantics. If filtered out, no TSC-deadline interface is advertised. The frequency and enabled MSR inventory remain owned with the CPU profile, not silently selected by this clock seam. |

The conditional equality argument is direct substitution of the documented
counter relation into the shared expression for `V`. It establishes a common
reference, not completion of every timer model, native timer programming,
wraparound implementation or interrupt-delivery rule. The reviewed legacy clock
substrate supplies calibration-visible device behavior; CPU-profile frequency
and device constants must describe this same conversion before boot acceptance.

**CLM-03-03-30 — Conditional whole-machine lifecycle and authority contract.**
The following defines the external obligations of the machine candidate. It is
not acceptance that a provider already implements them. It composes the reviewed
boot, memory, time and interrupt seams without coupling generator choice to a
particular device runtime.

| Boundary | Selected interface |
| --- | --- |
| Construction | One VM lifetime owns guest memory, vCPU/shim state, device owner, immutable translation publications, backend requests and bounded queues. Finish device decode configuration, no-SMM/SCI_EN initialization, finalized ACPI table relocations/checksums and declared topology before the BSP handoff. AP startup follows the separately reviewed mailbox/startup32 ABI. A failed construction never publishes a partly initialized runnable VM. |
| Execution | Guest CPU access completion, host DMA and page-table/code mutation use the shared coordination contract, including retained partial effects. The host device owner follows claim 28's nonblocking authority rules. Translation jobs operate on admitted immutable inputs, with no ambient guest-RAM write authority. Generator choice changes its adapter and semantic obligations, not the machine's authority rules. |
| Interrupts | Typed fixed, external-IACK and EOI operations retain claim 28's ownership and exactly-once effects. All enabled routing modes, IOAPIC ExtINT bookkeeping and bounded producer-state retention remain required coverage; this table does not close them by naming an interface. Neither native wakeup nor a transport reply is itself x86 delivery. |
| Block write | Snapshot/pin or otherwise admit the guest source through the memory protocol before an asynchronous host write. Completion status describes that backend operation, with exact partial/error behavior; write completion under negotiated writeback is not durability. Backend code cannot retain a freed guest pointer or write guest RAM outside an admitted completion. |
| Flush | Admit a barrier after preceding writes for the selected disk, await their backend completion, then request `F_FULLFSYNC` on the backing file. Return successful virtio flush only after that operation succeeds. Preserve failures; no fallback to weaker fdatasync/fsync while reporting the stronger success. Later requests may be serialized behind the barrier initially. No host owner waits synchronously for storage I/O. |
| Durable scope | The promise is successful completion of the documented host media-flush request for the already existing backing file, subject to the device compliance limit in claim 09. It is not atomic rollback of partial writes, survival of lying hardware, or persistence of newly created directory entries. Provisioning/publishing a new image is a separate host operation. Unsupported full-sync semantics must reject this writable-backend configuration or return truthful flush errors; they cannot silently weaken it. |
| S5 / orderly stop | ACPI S5 requests termination, not automatic proof that the guest flushed every filesystem. Stop new guest execution/device admissions, resolve admitted effects, perform the chosen backend flushes and report failure if they fail. Successful host stop may establish the host flush boundary, never guest filesystem consistency. External callbacks use lifetime IDs and are retired before mappings/descriptors are released. |
| Crash / forced stop | Stop admissions and terminate the failed VM's workers; retained host I/O must finish/cancel before its buffers/resources are reused. A supervisor can reclaim the crashed process's mappings, but cannot invent successful block completions or roll back external effects. Previously acknowledged flushes retain their promised scope. Peer VMs must not inherit this VM's writable memory, descriptors or device queues. |

The flush primitive and its limit are already reviewed in claim 09; this selects
an explicit backend contract instead of inheriting `qemu_fdatasync`. A storage
backend that cannot meet it is incompatible with this selection. Neither S5 nor
process exit upgrades durability. The implementation must preserve the declared
completion ordering, but implementation construction is not required merely to
state this architectural rule.

**First unresolved load-bearing authority choice — per-VM containment.**
Select one separately killable host worker per VM as the crash boundary, with
only that VM's guest memory, disk capabilities and network endpoint. The intended
rule is stronger than process separation: a compromised worker may corrupt its
own VM and authorized disk, but cannot open peer disks, signal/control peer
workers, obtain the supervisor's ambient file authority, or mutate peer RAM.
Parser/device/code-generator libraries inside the worker do not themselves
supply that restriction. An in-VM guarded EL1 boundary also does not constrain a
compromised host worker.

[Apple App Sandbox](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/app-sandbox)
states resource access is restricted through entitlements; SDK 26.4 `sandbox.h`
marks the older API deprecated and documents named profiles for `sandbox_init`.
This supports investigating an OS-enforced restriction, not the assertion that
one shared app entitlement/container already isolates individual VM workers.
The missing choice is the actual enforceable per-worker authority mechanism,
including HVF rights and the intended inherited disk/socket access. A shared
writable application container could expose peer images; unrestricted outbound
network entitlement may exceed a preauthorized endpoint. These are concrete
counterexamples to accepting process separation as the required containment.
Undocumented restriction mechanisms remain admissible, with an explicit behavior
contract, rather than being rejected simply for lacking a public API.

**Stop:** the time policy and lifecycle/storage interface above are reviewable
conditional choices. Whole-machine acceptance remains open at the concrete
per-VM authority mechanism, as well as the already identified full interrupt
coverage and callback/queue bounds. Source declarations cannot establish that a
particular entitlement/profile composition supplies those restrictions. No new
runtime evidence, hardware durability test or sandbox validation was performed.
The source-only pass stopped within 60 minutes/4 MiB; two Apple Markdown reads
(5,770 bytes) are in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-{vtimer-offset,app-sandbox}.md`.
No live download handles remain.

**Retained proposed follow-up (not started):** following Return 10 acceptance
of claims 29–30, one 45-minute source-only comparison of
per-VM App Sandbox helper authority versus an admissible narrower restriction
mechanism. Read the exact launch/inheritance, HVF entitlement and file/socket
capability contracts; stop on one enforceable authority recipe or the precise
behavior requiring a separately authorized bounded probe. Do not implement a
broker or run a sandbox experiment in this step. Preserve the already open IRQ
coverage as a separate required interface completion, not another unbounded
helper audit.

**Review disposition:** findings-review Return 10 accepts claims 29–30 in their
source/conditional scope without a substantive return. The ordinary
memory-coordination-park versus explicit VM-pause distinction is clarified above.
No complete machine/containment or physical durability acceptance follows. The
proposed 45-minute authority source comparison remains recorded in the current
summary and has not started. Current authorization is inventory cleanup only.

**Inventory-cleanup disposition, September 20:** findings Return 11 accepts the
charter/status/component-boundary cleanup. Claim 20's placement preference is
explicitly an unverified total-cost hypothesis; no-PIT and legacy-device options
remain unranked on captive-boundary adaptation cost. Source claims and prior
review scope are preserved. No new research or architecture acceptance follows.

## Per-VM worker authority — bounded source pass

**Method, September 20:** authorized 45 minutes, at most 8 MiB of official Apple
documentation/SDK declarations and primary sandbox-implementation sources, with
60 seconds/2 MiB per download. Establish launch/inheritance, HVF entitlement and
disk/socket authority for one VM worker; compare App Sandbox with a narrower
admissible profile. Stop at a conditional enforceable recipe or first precise
missing behavior plus the smallest resolution. Read-only source inspection, no
sandbox invocation, signing, executable build, VM or network experiment. Device
and generator selection remain independent.

**CLM-03-03-31 — Source-backed: ordinary App Sandbox inheritance is not a
per-VM authority recipe, and HVF permission is a separate requirement.**
Apple's [archived entitlement reference, “Enabling App Sandbox Inheritance”](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Miscellaneous/Reference/EntitlementKeyReference/Chapters/EnablingAppSandbox.html)
describes child inheritance of static parent rights, excluding rights acquired
later through Powerbox. Its current [helper embedding guidance](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode/embedding-a-helper-tool-in-a-sandboxed-app)
uses app-sandbox plus inherit entitlements and warns about extra entitlements
and get-task-allow. Separately, [HVF entitlement documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.security.hypervisor)
requires com.apple.security.hypervisor in every API-using process; the
[Hypervisor overview](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/hypervisor)
explicitly supports entitled sandboxed processes. These facts neither prohibit
HVF with sandboxing nor prove this particular helper-signing/inheritance
combination. Do not assume a parent's HVF entitlement grants a child the right.

Apple's [file access documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/accessing-files-from-the-macos-app-sandbox)
associates containers with app code identity, with unrestricted access inside
the app's own container. Repeated instances are not described as separate VM
containers. The [network-client entitlement](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.security.network.client)
permits outgoing connections, including to local servers; TCP connection
initiation and UDP data-flow restrictions differ. Thus neither “one child per
VM” nor a general network entitlement demonstrates the per-VM authority boundary
in claim 30. An independently sandboxed XPC service remains an alternative;
its service-specific rights do not by themselves specify instance-specific
files, memory or control capabilities.

**CLM-03-03-32 — Source-backed: custom Seatbelt has a concrete implementation
route, but preexisting capabilities and operation defaults matter.**
Inspected Chromium commit `5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880`, commit time
2026-09-20 07:16:41 UTC. This is primary evidence of Chromium's implementation,
not an Apple guarantee of our policy on macOS 26.6.2.

- [`sandbox/mac/seatbelt.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/seatbelt.cc)
  declares parameterized/custom compilation APIs outside the public SDK header.
  It calls sandbox_init, sandbox_init_with_parameters, or compile/apply wrappers.
  [`sandbox_serializer.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/sandbox_serializer.cc)
  applies source policy with flags zero or a compiled policy and propagates
  failures; [`seatbelt_exec.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/seatbelt_exec.cc)
  reads the serialized policy in the child before applying it. SDK 26.4
  `usr/include/sandbox.h` instead documents named profiles and says an already
  sandboxed process cannot initialize again through that API. The custom route
  is an admissible undocumented dependency, not a public named-profile contract
  or permission to stack it blindly onto App Sandbox inheritance.
- Chromium's [README](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/README.md)
  describes default-deny policies and continued use of preopened descriptors.
  The [`seatbelt_unittest.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/seatbelt_unittest.cc)
  source specifically checks write/ftruncate on an inherited writable descriptor
  and notes the historical version change. Another case explicitly denies
  process-info operations before allowing self inspection: default-deny alone
  does not mean every sensitive operation has become denied. These are inspected
  test expectations, not tests run or observations in this investigation.
- The [V2 design](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/mac/seatbelt_sandbox_design.md),
  updated January 30, 2025, applies restriction before loading the large runtime
  library because framework initializers can acquire persistent files and Mach
  service ports. Its compatibility risk discussion applies to changed OS
  framework dependencies. The inspected
  [`common.sb`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/5233c110e9d303cd8c144e8c4e0591147ec53880/sandbox/policy/mac/common.sb)
  grants specific self-signal, file and Mach-service operations. It is a Chromium
  policy, not a safe whole-profile import for this VM worker.

**CLM-03-03-33 — Conditional candidate and precise stopping point: a narrow
bootstrap can bound ambient worker authority, but its HVF composition is unproved.**
Prefer investigating a standalone entitled per-VM worker with a custom Seatbelt
restriction over interpreting shared inherited App Sandbox rights as per-VM
isolation. This is a mechanism hypothesis, not a measured cost winner or accepted
containment implementation. An independently configured XPC helper is not
rejected; it needs the same instance-authority argument. No generator or device
library is selected by this containment candidate.

The proposed authority sequence is deliberately small:

1. A trusted launcher selects the VM's already existing disk descriptors and a
   dedicated control/backend channel, creates that worker's private memory and
   finite resource limits, and launches only the trusted bootstrap. No guest
   image parsing, translation or device descriptor processing occurs before
   restriction. Guest-controlled paths, profiles or entitlement values never
   become launch authority.
2. Bootstrap retains only this VM's required capabilities. This inventory covers
   descriptors **and** Mach send/receive rights, bootstrap/service handles,
   shared-memory objects and resources acquired by library initializers. No peer
   VM disk, memory, process-control/task right or supervisor-wide service endpoint
   is inherited. A same-UID process boundary cannot replace that requirement.
   A preopened disk is intentionally full authority over that authorized object;
   sandbox path denial does not revoke an already granted descriptor.
3. Apply a reviewed custom restriction, check success, and fail closed before
   any guest work if it fails. Its intended policy denies new ambient file opens,
   peer process/task control and unauthorized Mach/network acquisition, with
   narrow runtime/framework exceptions and explicit treatment of default-allowed
   operations. Do not copy Chromium's whole allowlist or rely on the phrase
   default-deny as proof. Initially load nonessential runtime/device/generator
   libraries after restriction, keeping their initializers within the boundary.
4. The dedicated channel is a VM capability, not a generic privileged command
   interpreter. Existing block descriptors serve disk I/O without open-by-path
   requests. If a network backend needs host-created endpoints, its authority is
   independently limited to the configured VM network service; the worker cannot
   ask a supervisor to open arbitrary files, hand out peer handles or execute
   commands. Which network backend supplies that endpoint remains a device choice;
   this candidate does not invent or accept a new general-purpose broker.
5. Guest execution is admitted only after the worker acknowledges the installed
   restriction and complete capability inventory. During failure/teardown the
   supervisor can kill that worker, while resource reuse still obeys the existing
   callback/flush lifetime contract. The worker gains no corresponding authority
   to control the supervisor or peers.

**Conditional isolation argument:** if the selected OS restriction denies new
unauthorized acquisition/control, and every preexisting capability is limited as
above, a compromised worker can corrupt its own VM and explicitly authorized
backend objects but cannot derive peer/supervisor authority merely from a guest
pointer or requested pathname. This is an argument about authority, not memory
safety of the trusted kernel or a guarantee against unspecified kernel defects.
It requires the stated native policy and launch behavior; a successful sandbox
initialization return alone does not establish them.

**First missing platform behavior / stop:** the inspected sources do not supply
an exact macOS 26.6.2/SDK 26.4 restriction that both preserves required HVF
operations and denies peer authority while maintaining the disk/channel rights.
In particular, success creating a VM before restriction does not show that
post-restriction mapping, vCPU creation/run/exit/destruction or timer operations
work; creation after restriction may require a different allowlist. Nor does
Chromium's inherited-fd write test establish F_FULLFSYNC under this policy or
all socket operations. Source proves a credible API/launch seam, not the combined
native behavior. Stop here rather than broadening permissions until the VM works
or declaring a full containment recipe accepted.

**Smallest resolution:** prepare one bounded, separately reviewed platform
conformance method for the exact signed worker, resource manifest and candidate
profile. It must distinguish setup-before-restrict from create-after-restrict,
exercise the required post-restriction HVF lifecycle, own-file I/O/full-sync and
connected-channel operations, and check denial against a second worker's file,
process-control and memory/task capabilities. A matched unrestricted control is required for **each challenged operation**,
not only HVF/signing: same signing/identity, resource and target configuration,
with the custom restriction as the changed condition. A peer-file/control/task
operation already rejected by ordinary OS protections is not evidence that the
custom policy denied it; classify that result as a baseline denial, and use an
otherwise permitted controlled target or leave the specific policy claim
unestablished. Positive own-resource operations must likewise work in their
control before a restricted failure is attributed to the policy. Do not treat a sandbox-status bit,
hv_vm_create success or lack of crashes as the containment oracle. A VM-owning
worker requiring broad peer-capable rights would falsify this candidate and
motivate a narrower separately isolated service boundary. This pass neither
implements that method nor authorizes execution; the exact allowed-operation
profile is the first artifact it must make reviewable.

**Pass result and provenance:** source-only, completed within 45 minutes/8 MiB;
all downloads finished and no live handles remain. Apple documentation was read
as served September 20; SDK declarations are local SDK 26.4. A guessed Apple
command-line-tool URL returned 404; the actual helper-tool identifier was then
read, with no absence inference from that failure. Chromium sources and metadata
are reading copies `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-seatbelt-*`; Apple copies are
`natemu-track03-{helper-inherit,hvf-entitlement,network-entitlement,hypervisor-overview}.md`. Base64
responses were decoded solely as text; no downloaded code or test was executed.
Findings Return 12 independently accepts claims 31–33 in source/conditional
scope after clarifying that every challenged operation needs a matched control;
no substantive return remains. No actual containment enforcement, signing
combination, probe result or overall architecture acceptance is claimed.

**Review disposition and next boundary:** the source pass is complete. The next
step is the concrete capability manifest/profile and bounded platform-method
preparation stated in the current summary, not more general sandbox research.
It is unstarted; no new implementation, signing or runtime work has occurred.

## Containment conformance preparation — not executed

**Method preparation, September 20:** authorized 45 minutes to prepare one exact
SBPL profile, capability manifest and disposable source/runner under a new
Track 03 directory. Source may be compiled/syntax-checked, but no sandbox call,
signing, probe or VM execution before independent pre-execution review. Reuse
frozen Track 01 retention helpers by copying them into the eventual retained
snapshot, never editing frozen files. Proposed run cap: one target M4, three
modes (unrestricted, restriction before HVF load/setup, restriction after HVF
setup), two fresh worker repetitions per mode, one disposable peer, one vCPU
and 16-KiB guest backing per worker, 20 seconds per worker and 240 seconds for
the execution phase. Fixtures and outputs remain in one newly allocated external
raw run directory; no user disk/network endpoints. Each operation has a matched
unrestricted control, and an OS-baseline denial remains inconclusive about the
custom profile. Stop on unsupported profile/initialization, invalid controls,
resource/time cap or completed matrix; retain failures. Actual execution requires
the separate review and authorization recorded in this investigation.

### Concrete candidate and prepared fixtures

Prepared [disposable source/runner](../../../experiments/track03/containment/README.md)
in `experiments/track03/containment`, independent of the frozen Track 01 program.
The exact [`worker.sb`](../../../experiments/track03/containment/worker.sb) denies
by default, explicitly denies process-info, signal, Mach lookup, network and file
operations, then admits self process-info/signal and reads/executable mappings
only in the two named Hypervisor framework trees and `/usr/lib`. It deliberately
contains no invented HVF operation token and no dynamic disk/run-directory path
allowlist. Whether those rules parse and permit the required APIs is what the
probe tests. No automatic additions follow a denial.

The sole signing entitlement is com.apple.security.hypervisor; no App Sandbox
inheritance or get-task-allow entitlement is added. `probe.c` links only libSystem
and libsandbox and obtains HVF symbols through dlopen/dlsym. The control has the
same signed executable and resources. BEFORE installs the restriction before
loading HVF/creating the VM; AFTER loads/creates/maps/configures one vCPU before
restriction, then exercises required post-restriction operations. All three use
only the literal two-instruction native MOV/HVC guest. BEFORE/AFTER independently
matter: a rejected post-restriction HVF call is retained as a failed capability
case rather than aborting before the other fixed comparison can run.

| Operation | Positive/control oracle | Restricted case / interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Initial environment | `sandbox_check` says no inherited sandbox; retained host/SDK/signature context | An already-sandboxed worker stops with an environment result. It is not a kill of this standalone profile; any launch-environment adjustment needs explicit recorded context. |
| Profile | Exact retained bytes, initialization return and resulting status | Initialization failure stops; no fallback profile. A status bit alone proves no isolation. |
| Own disk | Fresh 4-KiB temporary disk FD; pwrite exact marker, pread exact bytes, F_FULLFSYNC success in matched control | Same operations must succeed through the inherited FD; full-sync success is API availability under policy, not a power-loss durability experiment. |
| Existing channel | Preconnected AF_UNIX socketpair; child reads A and parent receives B | Check byte values and counts, not just nonnegative return. This covers that channel only, not arbitrary socket families or new connections. |
| Peer file | Separate same-user temporary file; control can open read/write | Restricted open must fail with access-denial errno. Unexpected success is a negative containment finding; no user file is accessed. |
| Peer control | Dedicated peer handles SIGUSR1 without terminating, emits S through its own retained stdout; control syscall succeeds and marker is observed | Restricted signal must fail and produce no marker. `kill(pid,0)` or syscall return alone is not the oracle. Only this disposable peer PID is used. |
| Peer task capability | task_for_pid on that same signed peer; record Mach result/right | If control is denied by baseline OS policy, classify both restricted cells as inconclusive for custom-policy task/memory exclusion. No debug entitlement is silently added to force a positive. No peer memory is modified. |
| New connection | Control connects to a per-cell loopback-only listener | Restricted attempt must be access-denied; no external destination. It is separate from inherited socketpair use. |
| HVF lifecycle | Successful dlopen, create/map/vCPU configuration, MOV/HVC exit with expected syndrome and X0, post-boundary protect/unmap/remap, vCPU/VM destroy | Every reached API/result retained; before-versus-after comparison isolates setup timing. No success inferred from hv_vm_create alone. One vCPU and 16 KiB only. |
| Timer interface | Set/get virtual timer offset and mask, value agreement | Configuration API availability only. No timer expiry, interrupt delivery or clock accuracy claim. |

Mach-port names/types at entry are bounded diagnostics, not validation that all
inherited Mach/bootstrap/service rights are harmless. This minimal fixture
creates no peer capability transfer and loads no large device/generator runtime.
Consequently even a fully successful finite matrix would not close arbitrary
Mach-service access, production initializer capabilities, full peer-memory
exclusion or the entire production worker allowlist. Those limits prevent a
small positive result from becoming a false general containment guarantee.

### Budget, retention and stopping rules

The proposed run has exactly six fresh workers, one disposable peer and two
repetitions, with no performance sampling or exclusions. At most one VM worker
runs at a time. Per-worker deadline is 20 seconds, peer readiness 5 seconds and
execution phase 240 seconds. CPU limit is 10 seconds per process, open descriptors
64, file size 8 MiB and core dumps disabled. Finite source bounds diagnostic
output to 128 port entries plus fixed records. Parent kills/reaps owned process
groups in all exits; fixtures are retained under the raw run for inspection.
Unrestricted controls are checked immediately, including exact own-disk/channel
values, HVC result, full-sync and required peer-file/signal/loopback positives;
the task-capability control may remain baseline-denied/inconclusive. Invalid
unrestricted control, inherited sandbox, profile initialization error,
timeout or budget failure stops the matrix. A restricted HVF API denial/failure
is retained and the other predeclared setup order still runs; neither policy
widening nor unrecorded retry is permitted. A baseline-denied negative operation
never counts as custom-policy evidence.

`run.py` refuses missing/repository-contained raw roots and existing run names.
It snapshots its C/profile/entitlements/runner and copies the unchanged frozen
retention helper, verifies runner/helper snapshot equality, imports the retained
helper, then compiles from the retained C. It records environment, exact commands,
stdout/stderr, timeouts, peer markers, per-operation results and final checksums.
The prepared default is build-only. Only separately approved `--execute` signs
and starts processes. A new execution must have a fresh run identity; prep output
cannot be relabeled as decision evidence. The manifest's classifier reports
operation-level results and incomplete controls, never architecture acceptance.

**Preparation self-checks, not experimental evidence:** clang strict syntax and
link checks passed; `otool -L` confirmed only libSystem/libsandbox dependencies.
Python AST parsing and the pure synthetic `--self-check` passed, including a
missing signal marker, wrong channel reply, baseline task denial and incomplete
matrix. No sandbox_init, explicit entitlement signing, HVF load or guest execution was performed.
The temporary unentitled link-check binary is `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-containment-preflight`;
only the retained-snapshot build is eligible for any reviewed execution.

**Submission / next action:** independent pre-execution review of the exact
profile, C source, runner, operation oracles and bounds in this section. Stop
with acceptance of the finite method or a concrete defect/unsupported policy
rule. No probe execution has been performed or authorized by this submission.
The first runtime result must remain limited to its exact target, signed worker,
profile, inherited capabilities and fixture coverage; further generalization
needs its own evidence.

Each run has six sequential cells: two repeats each of unrestricted CONTROL,

positive oracles. Restricted cells recorded successful sandbox_init and active
sandbox status. Timer offset/mask set/get passed their configuration oracles;
no timer expiry/delivery was tested. Full-sync return success does not prove
physical power-loss durability.

For each run, both unrestricted controls successfully opened the peer fixture,
sent SIGUSR1 with an observed peer S marker, and connected to the disposable
loopback listener. Each of the four restricted cells returned EPERM for all
three operations; no restricted signal marker appeared. Thus each run supplies

The inherited socketpair remained usable despite denial of a new connection.
Task-port acquisition returned Mach error 5 with no right in controls and
restricted cells alike: all four restricted comparisons per run are

diagnostics, not proof that all inherited service or memory authority is absent.

**Decision consequence (conditional inference accepted in Return 15):** prefer applying the
candidate restriction before HVF loading and heavy VM initialization, after only
the explicit bootstrap inputs/handles have been acquired. This finite HVF path
does not require unsandboxed HVF warm-up on the tested host, so there is no
observed need to retain that additional unrestricted initialization phase.
This preference does not assert that every production library initializer or
backend operation works under this profile; those capabilities still need an
explicit manifest and applicable evidence. Device/code-generator selection is
unaffected.

**Remaining scope:** neither run proves general Mach-service/peer-memory
exclusion, a complete inherited capability inventory, all filesystem/network
operations, production signing/helper launch, arbitrary HVF APIs, SMP, timer
delivery, crash/exhaustion behavior, M2 parity or the whole machine containment
contract. Successful BEFORE and AFTER cells settle the finite ordering question,
not equivalence of all future initialization sequences. The failed CPU-limited
run remains retained as an invalid control and is not counted as a profile failure.

## Production authority boundary source/design pass — scoped acceptance (Return 16)

**Method and limit (September 20, 2026):** one 45-minute source/design pass,
starting from accepted claim 36. Inspect Apple Mach task-right, exec/inheritance
and memory-access contracts plus selected launcher source to distinguish
acquiring new authority from retaining already-held authority. Construct the
smallest explicit capability set for one VM worker and its supervisor channel.
No runtime, profile or probe changes. Stop at a conditional enforceable launch
recipe or the first unsupported load-bearing capability; record that precise
remaining requirement rather than widening the experiment matrix. Device reuse
and code-generator selection remain independent of this process boundary.

**CLM-03-03-37 — Source-backed: task-right acquisition, existing authority and
launch inheritance are distinct boundaries.** The pass inspected Apple's public
XNU **xnu-12377.1.9** tag. This is a versioned source contract, not a claim that
its private policy implementation or exact build is the kernel in claim 36.

- [`bsd/kern/kern_proc.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_proc.c),
  `task_for_pid` and `task_read_for_pid`, apply POSIX checks, separate MAC checks
  with CONTROL versus READ flavor, and applicable task-access-server checks.
  The latter entry point is not covered by the fixture's failed CONTROL query.
  The open source calls policy hooks; it does not disclose the complete shipping
  Seatbelt/AMFI policy or prove what `(deny process-info*)` does to each flavor.
- [`mach_vm.defs`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/mach_vm.defs)
  makes `mach_vm_read`/`mach_vm_read_overwrite` consume `vm_map_read_t`, while
  `mach_vm_write` consumes `vm_map_t`.
  [`mach_types.defs`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/mach_types.defs)
  maps these to `convert_port_to_map_read`/`convert_port_to_map`.
  [`ipc_tt.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_tt.c)
  resolves held ports with the requested flavor and `task_conversion_eval`.
  These paths are not a fresh `task_for_pid` call. Conversion has its own checks,
  including the inspected developer-mode CONTROL rule; do not assume every
  transferred right always works, or that denying acquisition revokes it.
- In that same `ipc_tt.c`, `ipc_task_init` creates a new IPC space but explicitly
  inherits registered ports, exception actions, host, bootstrap and task-access
  rights from a parent. Numeric port namespaces or ordinary descriptor closing
  therefore do not prove the absence of inherited Mach authority.
  `task_set_special_port_internal` can replace bootstrap, but refuses overwriting
  an existing task-access port. `task_set_special_port` also restricts replacing
  the task/host ports under normal SIP conditions. Blanket "clear every special
  port" is neither an available recipe nor a sound authorization policy.
- [`kern_exec.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c),
  `exec_handle_port_actions`, accepts NULL special-port actions and calls
  `task_set_special_port`; exception actions are applied through a separate
  path. The SDK 26.4 `usr/include/spawn.h` declares
  `posix_spawnattr_setspecialport_np` and `posix_spawnattr_setexceptionports_np`;
  `mach/task.h` declares `mach_ports_lookup`/`mach_ports_register`.
  These support explicit launch construction, not a proven current-host sanitized
  HVF launch. A NULL bootstrap action does not erase other already-acquired rights.
- Apple's [Mach overview](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Darwin/Conceptual/KernelProgramming/Mach/Mach.html)
  explains port-right transfer and named memory entries that permit mappings.
  Its current [Mach IPC security guidance](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode/conforming-to-mach-ipc-security-restrictions)
  specifically identifies task/thread control-right transfer and exception
  behaviors carrying those rights as authority hazards. This supports forbidding
  such transfers in this architecture; it does not require adding the optional
  enhanced-security entitlement or prove the candidate profile's enforcement.
- Apple's [debugger entitlement contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.security.cs.debugger)
  ties `task_for_pid` debugging access to target protection and Get Task Allow.
  [Hardened Runtime](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/hardened-runtime)
  describes memory-tampering protections and executable entitlements. These are
  a source-supported candidate target-protection layer, not a reason to claim
  the ad-hoc, sole-HVF-entitlement fixture tested production hardened signing,
  or to infer all READ-flavor behavior from the CONTROL documentation.

**CLM-03-03-38 — Conditional architecture choice: authority by construction at
launch, with no peer-memory capability protocol.** Retain one host worker per VM
for host translation, device ownership and captive-VM management. Translation jobs
inside that worker and native shims do not require another VM's task port or
shared guest RAM. Choose an explicit per-VM byte channel and resources below;
reject a general task-port/Mach-right rendezvous between workers and supervisor.
This is a concrete restriction on the architecture's interfaces, independent of
which code generator or device models are reused.

| Capability | Candidate holder and permitted use | Exclusion / evidence boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Own VM RAM, translation/native-code storage, vCPU handles | Worker allocates after restriction; all per-VM host threads participate in its coordinator | No cross-VM RAM mapping, task READ/CONTROL right, thread control right or named memory entry is passed. Claim 36 covers a small HVF path, not the full allocator or SMP. |
| Block backing | Supervisor opens the user-authorized VM-specific file; worker receives that file descriptor with declared read/write rights | No directory descriptor, arbitrary-path open RPC or peer disk descriptor. The existing fixture covers own-file I/O/full-sync return; descriptor lifetime/identity and actual backend features still need conformance. |
| Boot inputs and runtime libraries | Explicit immutable boot inputs and selected installed library paths; trusted tiny bootstrap precedes guest parsing | System library allowances are not arbitrary user-file access. Large device/generator libraries load only after restriction; no broad initialization warm-up exemption. |
| Lifecycle, serial and diagnostics | One bounded per-VM socketpair/pipe protocol carrying bytes and a fixed VM identity established by the supervisor endpoint | No worker-selected peer/PID/path, FD transfer, Mach descriptors, task-port request or generic privileged syscall proxy. Supervisor treats all received lengths/commands as untrusted; only this VM's lifecycle can be affected. Log/serial output uses its assigned endpoint. |
| Network | A VM-specific packet endpoint with fixed backend authority, supplied separately from lifecycle control | No arbitrary socket-creation broker on the control channel. The tested socketpair proves only a byte-channel mechanism; the actual network backend/endpoint and its required grants remain a device choice, not established by this pass. |
| Entropy and time | Own-process OS entropy and clock interfaces; no peer mapping or broker authority required by the architectural contract | Exact production API/profile composition remains applicable conformance work; no new entitlement inferred. |
| Mach/runtime facilities | Self task/thread/reply rights, ordinary host/runtime facilities, preserved system task-access authorization; no application peer-service rights | Do not clear task-access protection or pretend ordinary host rights can all be replaced. Every inherited non-self application right requires identified provenance and bounded authority; unidentified rights block guest admission. |

**Launch sequence and its narrow unresolved premise.** The supervisor supplies
only the explicit descriptor set and clean launch environment to a trusted minimal
worker executable. It does not put peer task/thread/memory rights in registered
ports, exception channels or a rendezvous table. The candidate supervisor launch
context has an empty registered-port collection, checked by the trusted launcher;
if a selected runtime requires registered ports, name and justify each instead of
silently inheriting it. Prefer a NULL bootstrap special-port spawn action and no
application exception-port action, preventing initializers from acquiring ambient
application services before restriction. Existing system exception handling must
be identified or explicitly replaced with a reviewed non-peer arrangement; this
pass does not assume all inherited exception ports are empty or harmless.
Ordinary system host/task-access facilities remain as specified above.

The worker applies the candidate restriction before HVF and large runtime loading,
then creates only its own VM resources. Guest bytes and guest-controlled device
work are admitted only after successful restriction and resource preparation.
The supervisor never sends peer capabilities later, including during crash
reporting or recovery. The channel is not used to receive ancillary capabilities;
trusted launch grants are fixed separately. These rules remove a need for
post-compromise capability filtering inside the worker: confinement must survive
arbitrary worker behavior, relying on OS enforcement and what the trusted
supervisor makes reachable, not the compromised worker following the protocol.
No claim is made that the source-derived launch actions have already run together.

**Decision and stop.** The pass closes the interface choice: no application Mach
capability exchange or cross-VM shared-memory authority is needed by the selected
worker boundary; existing-right containment is a launch/provenance obligation,
not a `task_for_pid` denial checkbox. It does **not** close platform enforcement.
The first unsupported composition is whether the minimal worker can initialize
libSystem/libsandbox and its required HVF path under the proposed sanitized
bootstrap/registered/exception context. Claim 36 used the ordinary launch context
and cannot answer that. Independently, new peer-memory acquisition needs a target
signing/policy contract covering READ as well as CONTROL; current baseline failure
and open XNU hooks do not supply that contract. A shared memory entry or a right
transferred after launch would falsify the exclusion argument even if both PID
queries fail. A required ambient bootstrap service would falsify the zero-service
candidate and require one named, authority-bounded exception, not default widening.

**Smallest next step (proposed, not executed):** one 45-minute method-preparation
pass for a launch-context-only extension of the retained fixture: exact NULL
bootstrap/registered-port and exception disposition with unchanged profile/guest,
plus a source-backed choice of peer target signing and separate READ/CONTROL
oracles. Any runtime must receive separate pre-execution review. If a positive
policy control cannot be arranged without changing the production premise, report
that component as inconclusive and state which documented OS target protection is
relied on instead; do not add debugging entitlements to the production worker.
Stop at the reviewable narrow method or a specific impossible launch prerequisite,
not a broad service-permission campaign. No new runtime occurred in this pass.

**Provenance and review status:** root findings Return 16 accepts claims 37–38
in source/conditional interface scope, with no substantive return.
Reading copies of the cited XNU tag files and Apple Markdown documentation are
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-authority-*`; SDK headers were inspected in the
installed 26.4 SDK. The GitHub API pin lookup returned HTTP 403, so this pass uses
the explicit upstream release tag rather than inventing a commit identity.
The source-only pass stopped after the concrete launch composition gap; no claim
of a sanitized production launch, additional peer-memory denial or complete
architecture is submitted.


## Sanitized-launch method preparation — submitted, no execution

**Method/budget:** September 20, one authorized 45-minute preparation pass.
Reuse the existing retention runner, guest instructions/register state, profile,
and sole HVF entitlement. Add one small launcher that applies the proposed port
context before exec and child-side diagnostics that validate the actual launch
postconditions. Proposed finite decision-evidence sequence is one ordinary
unrestricted control, one ordinary restricted-before control, then one sanitized
restricted-before worker. No after-HVF cells, retries or policy widening. Separate
READ and CONTROL task queries use the same non-debug-entitled peer; absent a
successful baseline, no custom-policy exclusion verdict is possible. Compile and
synthetic oracle checks may run now; signing/launch/sandbox/VM execution may not.

### Concrete submission — prepared, pending pre-execution review

Files: [launch.c](../../../experiments/track03/containment/launch.c),
[probe.c](../../../experiments/track03/containment/probe.c),
[run.py](../../../experiments/track03/containment/run.py). The new launcher uses
`posix_spawn` with SETEXEC, so the existing supervised PID/process group remains
the lifetime boundary. It makes only these sanitized-context changes: clear its
registered-port collection with `mach_ports_register`; clear its current-thread
exception actions; apply NULL TASK_BOOTSTRAP_PORT and NULL task exception spawn
actions. The worker checks actual bootstrap, registered, task-exception and
main-thread-exception state before applying the sandbox. A nonempty sanitized
result or failed query stops admission; setter success alone is insufficient.
The independent reviewer's early task-versus-thread observation motivated the
separate thread clearing/readback. System task-access/host special ports and CPU
limits are not changed. This is an explicit test of removing these four channels,
not a proof that every inherited capability or future thread is harmless.

The ordinary cells use the same SETEXEC adapter without sanitization. Thus both
normal and changed launch paths include the adapter; the restricted ordinary
control tests that adding the adapter/readback itself preserves the fixture.
The unchanged profile remains loaded by the worker before restriction; no new
permission is added. Guest instructions, registers and HVF operations are
unchanged. The C additions are launch diagnostics and the independent
`task_read_for_pid` query. That symbol is exported by SDK 26.4
`usr/lib/system/libsystem_kernel.tbd`; its argument shape follows the inspected
XNU syscall. CONTROL remains the existing Mach-returning API; READ records the
BSD return and errno. Both record whether an actual right was returned and
release any acquired right; neither reads or writes peer memory.

**Exact proposed sequence:** `--launch-audit --execute` selects just three cells,
all repeat index 0: ordinary CONTROL, ordinary BEFORE, sanitized BEFORE. One
fresh harmless peer serves those cells. CONTROL must pass the existing positive
operations plus peer open, observed SIGUSR1 and loopback controls before anything
restricted starts. Ordinary BEFORE must pass all positive operations and launch
queries before the sanitized cell starts. A sanitized HVF failure is a retained
compatibility limitation; missing/failed child launch postconditions, profile
failure, outer sandbox or timeout stop the run. There is no profile fallback,
AFTER cell or retry. All per-operation denial verdicts still use the unrestricted
positive control. An unexpected successful restricted operation is reported as
such, never relabeled as compatibility success or a baseline failure.

**Task-right oracle and signing:** retain the same ad-hoc sole-HVF-entitlement
peer and worker, with no Get Task Allow, debugger or hardened-runtime changes.
READ and CONTROL are separately classified; acquisition denied in CONTROL is
inconclusive for the custom profile regardless of restricted outcomes. A positive
CONTROL acquisition followed by a restricted permission denial supports only that
flavor and API under these signing conditions. This method deliberately does not
manufacture a positive baseline by transferring a right or granting debugging
privileges. If no positive task-right baseline exists, production READ/CONTROL
target protection remains open and needs a separate source-backed signing choice;
this small launch comparison cannot close it. No generalized memory-isolation
claim can be made from a successful launch postcondition.

**Budget/retention:** three workers maximum, one worker plus one peer concurrently;
one 16-KiB MOV/HVC VM with one vCPU per worker. Worker 20-second wall timeout,
peer readiness 5 seconds, execution phase 100 seconds; unchanged core/file/FD
limits, peer CPU-10 and build CPU-60. VM workers and their SETEXEC adapter inherit
unlimited CPU limits and never call the CPU-limit setter. Producing snapshot now
includes launch.c along with the five prior inputs; both binaries build from that
snapshot. The adapter has no HVF entitlement; only the probe receives the unchanged
entitlement file. Existing process-group timeout/kill/reap and raw failure capture
apply. Proposed fresh runtime identity `containment-launch-reviewed-01` under the
existing external root; no such runtime was performed by this preparation.

**Preparation evidence:** strict warning-as-error compile/link and synthetic
assessor checks passed. The latter cover nonempty bootstrap/registered/task/thread
exception channels, incomplete comparison, positive and baseline-denied READ
controls, and the prior guest/resource/control failures. Build-only preparation

## Production task-access protection source/design pass — scoped acceptance (Return 18)

**Method/budget:** September 20, one authorized 45-minute source/design pass.
Inspect exact installed Apple policy/source declarations and primary signing
contracts for CONTROL and READ acquisition, then determine the least additional
worker authority required by the existing HVF/component design. No runtime,
sandbox query, probe or signing change. Stop at a supported conditional target
protection choice or the precise missing policy mapping/indispensable grant.
Repeated baseline-denied task queries are not a method for closing this question.

**CLM-03-03-40 — Source-backed: the target-signing description does not close
READ, but Apple exposes a target-independent syscall admission boundary.**
The installed macOS 26.6.2 (25G83) Apple policies provide concrete primary input:

- [com.apple.gputoolsserviced.sb](/System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/com.apple.gputoolsserviced.sb)
  explicitly permits `mach-task-read` and separately permits BSD
  `SYS_task_read_for_pid` through `syscall-unix`/`syscall-number`.
  [com.apple.Photos.CPLDiagnose.sb](/System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/com.apple.Photos.CPLDiagnose.sb)
  permits `mach-task-read mach-task-name` for `target others`.
  These establish separate named operations in the shipped policy, not the
  complete private implementation or an equivalence with `process-info*`.
- [airlock.sb](/System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/airlock.sb) has a `deny syscall-mach`
  rule followed by a `machtrap-number` allowlist; it also uses BSD syscall-number
  filtering. Its BSD denial carries telemetry modifiers, which this proposal does
  not copy or interpret as enforcement evidence. The installed
  [system.sb](/System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/system.sb) identifies these files
  as changing Apple private interfaces. They are exact local policy sources,
  not a stable public SBPL specification, and none is imported wholesale here.
- Apple XNU **xnu-12377.1.9**
  [`osfmk/kern/syscall_sw.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/syscall_sw.c)
  maps Mach trap **45** to `task_for_pid`;
  [`bsd/kern/syscalls.master`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/syscalls.master)
  maps BSD syscall **539** to `task_read_for_pid` (538 is INSPECT, not READ).
  [`osfmk/arm64/bsd_arm64.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/arm64/bsd_arm64.c),
  `mach_syscall`, checks the task's trap filter mask and calls
  `mac_task_mach_trap_evaluate(proc, call_number)` before invoking the trap.
  A non-success policy result skips the trap. This is distinct from the later
  target/flavor authorization inside `task_for_pid`.
- [`bsd/dev/arm/systemcalls.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/dev/arm/systemcalls.c),
  `unix_syscall`, similarly checks `proc_syscall_filter_mask` and calls
  `mac_proc_check_syscall_unix(proc, syscode)` before `sy_call`; an error skips
  the syscall. [`security/mac_process.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/security/mac_process.c)
  dispatches the corresponding policy hook. These interfaces supply process and
  call number, not a queried target PID. Enforcement remains conditional on
  active filtering/MAC policy and a denied evaluation; merely writing an SBPL
  string has not established that condition. The public tag is not claimed to
  be the exact source of the host kernel binary.

The previously cited Apple debugger entitlement contract and installed
[taskgated(8)](/usr/share/man/man8/taskgated.8) describe debugging/task-port
authorization; they do not specify an exhaustive current READ-flavor policy.
No source in this pass justifies promoting the observed baseline denials into
that missing target-signing contract. Differing unprivileged UIDs would trigger
the inspected POSIX check for both calls, but that alternative adds privileged
identity/launch management and is not selected merely to avoid this narrow gap.

**CLM-03-03-41 — Conditional design choice: remove PID-based task acquisition
from the worker's authority, rather than depend on a debuggable peer control.**
The worker has no architectural need to acquire another process's READ/CONTROL
port. It already uses its own task/thread facilities and owns its VM. Select,
subject to exact profile binding/conformance, unconditional denial of the two
PID-acquisition entries in addition to the existing no-capability-transfer and
sanitized-launch rules. Unlike target-specific checks, this denies self-target
uses of those two acquisition APIs too; normal `mach_task_self` is a different
operation and must continue to work.

A concrete **uncompiled, unexecuted** profile amendment to assess is:

```scheme
; Arm64 XNU source mapping inspected above; verify binding on the actual OS.
(deny syscall-mach (machtrap-number 45))
(deny syscall-unix (syscall-number 539))
```

No source/probe profile was changed in this pass. Numeric entry identities avoid
inventing an unverified SBPL symbol; they remain explicitly version/architecture
qualified. This is not a proposed broad syscall allowlist, an added runtime
framework, or a grant of `syscall*`. Nor does it replace the lifetime/provenance
requirements for rights already held or obtained over a different channel.

**Conditional exclusion argument:** if the installed policy binds these rules to
the inspected pre-dispatch checks, neither PID-acquisition implementation runs
in the restricted worker, regardless of target credentials, debugger consent or
Get Task Allow. Thus those two routes cannot supply a peer-memory capability.
A user-space reimplementation of the same syscall still reaches the kernel
entry; changing the PID cannot evade a call-number denial. This proves only the
specified routes under the binding premise. Other right transfer, named memory
entry, exception and supervisor paths retain claim 38's separate no-peer-authority
contract. It does not prove absence of every possible acquisition API.

**Other routes remain explicit:** this two-entry choice is not a complete
peer-right acquisition proof. The same inspected `kern_proc.c` also contains
`debug_control_port_for_pid`, with its own CONTROL MAC/entitlement path; absence
of the private debug-port entitlement is not by itself evidence that every
fallback path denies access. `task_name_for_pid` and `task_inspect_for_pid` are
different flavors; the previously inspected map converters reject NAME/INSPECT
as direct memory-map substitutes, but any rights derivable through their methods
still require applicable reachability accounting. Task-identity tokens, exception
messages and already-held special/named-memory rights are governed by the
no-transfer/launch contract, not these syscall rules. `ptrace` is a separate BSD
entry (26 in the inspected table), so debugger absence or a blocked task query
must not be called proof of its denial. No indispensable worker operation in the
current architecture requires these peer routes; their actual grant/denial and
derivation closure remains part of the production authority proof. This bounded
pass does not silently mark them covered or widen the proposed two-entry test.

**Production grants and target signing:** choose the release worker/supervisor
without Get Task Allow, debugger, task-port private entitlements, or an arbitrary
Mach/FD-transfer service. Hardened Runtime with normal library validation is the
candidate release-signing layer, not a claimed proof of READ denial or a new
observed launch condition. Apple's
[library-validation contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation)
allows Apple libraries and libraries signed with the main executable's Team ID;
build selected device/generator libraries into that signed dependency set rather
than grant arbitrary plug-in loading. The exact installed code paths remain in
the capability manifest and must be readable under the selected profile.

The fixed architecture generates code as **host data** to execute in the captive
VM. Apple's [allow-JIT contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit)
concerns writable/executable host mappings using MAP_JIT. SDK 26.4 `hv_vm.h`
separately specifies mapping current-process memory into guest physical space
with guest permissions. The accepted finite fixture already uses a host RW
anonymous mapping with guest EXEC permission, without a host-JIT entitlement.
Therefore host `allow-jit`, unsigned-host-executable-memory and disabled library
validation are not intrinsic requirements of our code-generation boundary.
An imported allocator that demands host executable memory is an adaptation choice
to examine, not a reason to silently grant that authority or choose a generator.
This does not establish hardened-signing compatibility for the finished worker.

The minimum demonstrated platform grant remains the HVF entitlement plus the
explicit own-file/channel resources and selected read-only library paths.
Actual network backend access must resolve to claim 38's VM-specific endpoint;
there is no demonstrated need for worker peer-task access to implement block,
serial, time, entropy, translation or such an endpoint. This architectural
statement does not claim that every selected third-party initializer already
works with that grant set. A real backend needing an additional service must
name its authority and show how it remains confined; widening to ambient services
would reopen the containment choice.

**Stop and exact missing premise:** source/design work settles a narrower
protection direction but does not accept the profile amendment as enforced.
The remaining premise is the actual SBPL-to-pre-dispatch binding and compatibility
of denying these entries with the required worker calls. This is smaller than
reconstructing all private READ signing policy. A future bounded method can use
**self-target** CONTROL and READ acquisitions before restriction as positive
controls, then require both entry calls to be denied after the amendment while
`mach_task_self` and the unchanged HVF/resource fixture still work. Self-target
controls avoid adding a debugger entitlement, privileged user or transferred
peer task right; if they do not succeed, no denial inference is available.
Successful self denial would support call-level enforcement only together with
the inspected target-independent filter argument, not by guessing why a peer
query fails. Kernel filter failure, a needed self-acquisition in HVF/initializers,
or a rule accepted but not enforced would falsify this candidate.

**Proposed next action:** one 30-minute method-preparation pass for exactly that
two-entry filter distinction, preserving the accepted sanitized launch, native
guest and resources. Declare before/after self oracles and direct-entry coverage;
stop at a reviewable finite method or a named binding obstacle. Any sandbox/VM
execution needs new independent pre-execution review and explicit bounded scope.
No task query, sandbox call, signing change or runtime occurred in this source
pass, and no repeated baseline-denied peer-query campaign is proposed.

**Review/provenance:** root findings Return 18 accepts claims 40–41 in scoped
source/conditional-choice scope. It requires attribution controls: the original
profile must permit the measured self acquisitions before an added filter can be
credited with denying them. Downloaded
Apple files are `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-*`, including the exact
XNU tag files and Apple entitlement Markdown. Installed policy snapshots are
`...-protection-installed-{system,airlock,com.apple.gputoolsserviced,com.apple.Photos.CPLDiagnose}.sb`.
Current installed files and SDK are primary material; copied policy examples
establish vocabulary/use only, with the explicit binding premise above.


## Two-entry filter method preparation — submitted, no execution

**Method/budget:** September 20, one authorized 30-minute preparation pass.
Reuse the three-cell runner and accepted sanitized launch. Proposed sequence:
sanitized unrestricted CONTROL, sanitized original-profile BEFORE, sanitized
amended-profile BEFORE, once each. Preserve original profile/guest/entitlements;
the sole new policy is a separate original-profile copy plus the two numeric
entry denials in claim 41. Add self-target CONTROL/READ observations. Both
unrestricted and original-profile cells must show successful acquisition before
admitting the amended cell; otherwise stop with an explicit attribution failure,
not a filter verdict. No baseline widening, retry or debug entitlement. Strict
compile/link and synthetic checks are allowed; no worker execution is authorized.

### Concrete filter submission — prepared, pending independent pre-execution review

`--task-filter --execute` would select exactly three sanitized-launch workers:
CONTROL with no sandbox applied, BEFORE with unchanged `worker.sb`, then BEFORE
with separate [worker-task-filter.sb](../../../experiments/track03/containment/worker-task-filter.sb).
The amendment is byte-for-byte the base profile followed by the two numeric
syscall-denial rules and comments. Neither base profile nor guest/HVF setup and
exercise functions, entitlement file or SETEXEC launcher changed from the
accepted launch replication. This mode is exclusive with other diagnostic modes.

[probe.c](../../../experiments/track03/containment/probe.c) adds four self-target
acquisitions after resources and restriction (if any): API CONTROL, API READ,
direct Mach trap 45 and direct BSD syscall 539. A returned valid right is required
for success, and every returned right is deallocated. The direct arm64 calls use
x0=self task, x1=own PID, x2=output address and x16=-45 or 539, then SVC 0x80.
Inputs are computed before binding fixed registers, addressing an early reviewer
concern about getpid clobbering x0. BSD carry/error handling follows inspected
`arm_prepare_u64_syscall_return`; Mach returns its kernel result directly.
This tests kernel entry as well as the public/private library wrappers, not a
user-space wrapper check masquerading as confinement. The independent
`self_task_facility` oracle uses `mach_port_type` on `mach_task_self` and requires
a send right; a nonzero cached port number alone is not sufficient.

**Attribution and admission:** both first cells must pass prior launch/HVF/file/
channel controls and **all four** self-acquisition successes. Unrestricted peer
open/signal/loopback controls remain required. If any self acquisition or self
facility check fails in the unrestricted or original-profile cell, the runner
records `task_filter_inconclusive_before_amendment`, retains that output and stops
before the amended cell. It does not widen the base profile, try a different
signing identity, add debugger rights or credit the untested filter. This
intentional inconclusive stop returns normally so its manifest status, not the
runner exit code, determines the scientific outcome. It may stop after only one
or two workers.

If admitted, the amended cell must retain the ordinary positive operations and
usable self task facility. Each acquisition must return no valid right and a
failure; READ additionally requires EPERM/EACCES rather than an unrelated BSD
error. Mach failures are recorded exactly and interpreted only with matched
controls/source ordering, not as a universal meaning for every error code.
Missing output, process death, invalid context, policy initialization failure,
outer sandbox and timeout are not successful denials. Unexpected acquisition
success is reported as `entry_not_denied`. The final assessment names only the
four measured self calls; it cannot certify all peer acquisition routes.

**Question/decision consequence:** does adding these two entry-specific rules
actually deny entry where the original profile allowed it, while preserving the
required finite worker behavior? If so, the observation plus claim 40's dispatch
argument supports these two unconditional route exclusions. If base self access
is already denied, this method supplies no binding attribution and stops. It
neither proves full peer-memory exclusion nor resolves the independently listed
other acquisition/held-right routes. No production target-signing guarantee is
inferred from self controls.

**Resource/retention scope:** at most three workers, one at a time with one
harmless peer; inherited unlimited VM CPU limits, no CPU setter, unchanged
20-second worker and 100-second phase caps, 16-KiB/one-vCPU native guest, fixed
own-file/socketpair/loopback fixtures and kill/reap behavior. Seven producing
inputs are now retained, adding the amended profile to the previous six. Builds
still use the snapshot; sandbox/profile parsing occurs only in an independently
reviewed future execution. Proposed fresh identity
`containment-task-filter-reviewed-01`; no such run has occurred in preparation.

**Preparation checks:** synthetic cases cover successful matched self controls,
base-profile direct-READ failure that forbids attribution, unexpected direct
CONTROL success under the amendment, incomplete comparison and all earlier
launch/resource controls. Strict warning-as-error builds pass. Build-only
[preparation 03](/private/tmp/natemu-track03-raw/containment-task-filter-preparation-03/manifest.json)
and its [checksums](/private/tmp/natemu-track03-raw/containment-task-filter-preparation-03/SHA256SUMS)
retain the current corrected sources and binaries; preparation 01 retains the
pre-review fixed-register arrangement, superseded without executing it. Neither
preparation signed for HVF or launched a worker, sandbox or VM. The SBPL amendment
has not been compiled/applied and remains a candidate.

**Stop:** current method/code are submitted to the independent memory reviewer
in the existing pre-execution thread. Preparation authorization does not authorize
runtime. Any source changes after review require affected checks/review again.


**Metadata correction before runtime:** new manifests explicitly identify schema
`natemu.track03.containment.v2`, covering the added cell policy/context and
assessment shapes. Earlier unversioned manifests, including preparation 02, are
historical retained formats and are not rewritten. Root requested this metadata-

03 retains the amended runner and requires memory's changed-input recheck before

## Capability guard coverage — bounded source stop at alternate CONTROL acquisition

**Method:** September 20, authorized 30-minute source/design pass. Inspect the
