### CLM-03-03-44 — An alternate CONTROL entry needs an explicit guard

**Source finding and conditional choice accepted in root Return 21.**
The remaining gap is concrete: Mach trap **96** dispatches
`debug_control_port_for_pid`, independently of trap 45 `task_for_pid` and BSD
539 `task_read_for_pid`. This is the first alternate acquisition boundary examined;
the pass stops here rather than assert complete coverage.

In the pinned [Mach trap table](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/syscall_sw.c),
trap 96 names that handler. The [handler](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_proc.c)
resolves the supplied task port and target PID, applies its POSIX check, and—when
the caller lacks `DEBUG_PORT_ENTITLEMENT`—runs a CONTROL-flavor MAC check and the
applicable task-access-server upcall. A successful path obtains the debug control
port and copies a send right into the caller's IPC space. A worker without the
private entitlement therefore still has an ordinary authorization path; absence
of that entitlement alone does not remove the entry. This does **not** establish
that the current sandbox permits it. The original-profile trap-45 denial does
not independently demonstrate every predicate or result of trap 96 either.

The demonstrated rule in claim 43 covers only BSD 539. The historical, unexecuted
two-entry profile adds Mach 45, not Mach 96. Consequently neither the measured
READ rule nor that proposed two-entry list establishes unconditional exclusion
of this alternate CONTROL acquisition. The selected default-deny policy may
already deny it through the CONTROL MAC hook; its applicable binding is the
missing proof, not an observed exploit or assertion that peer access succeeds.

**Smallest concrete correction:** make the candidate acquisition boundary
explicitly include `(deny syscall-mach (machtrap-number 96))`, alongside the
separately proposed trap-45 exclusion and demonstrated READ-entry rule. No
indispensable worker operation currently calls for acquiring another task's
debug-control port; ordinary `mach_task_self` use is not that acquisition.
The pinned [arm64 pre-dispatch path](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/arm64/bsd_arm64.c)
consults the active task trap mask and `mac_task_mach_trap_evaluate(proc,
call_number)` before dispatch, skipping the handler on non-success. If the
explicit rule is bound to that filter for trap 96, the exclusion is independent
of PID and prevents this handler from reaching its entitlement/upcall branches.
The source argument is concrete; **SBPL-to-Mach-filter binding and compatibility
are not established by the BSD-only observation.** No candidate profile bytes
were changed or executed in this pass.

### Acquisition is separate from use of existing rights

The correction prevents one acquisition entry, not use or derivation from an
already-held right. The retained [task-port conversion code](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_tt.c)
`task_port_kotype_valid_for_flavor` accepts stronger types for weaker operations:
READ conversion accepts READ or CONTROL, whereas an INSPECT or NAME right alone
is not accepted there as READ. Thus merely obtaining NAME/INSPECT is not an
automatic upgrade through this conversion routine; this is not a claim that
all identity-token or special-port derivations have been audited. Conversely,
a held CONTROL/READ right can authorize its corresponding map operations without
repeating the PID acquisition syscall. Entry filtering cannot revoke it.

The selected no-right-transfer byte-channel protocol and sanitized launch remain
required premises. Their finite tests do not establish every inherited capability,
exception channel or broker. Identity-token derivation, other process-control
routes and the complete held-right inventory remain explicitly unclosed; none
is silently approved because trap 96 has a proposed fix. No blanket production
peer-isolation decision follows.

**Inspectability:** exact retained inputs are
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-osfmk-kern-syscall_sw.c` (trap 96),
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-authority-bsd-kern-kern_proc.c` (handler 6282–6400),
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-osfmk-arm64-bsd_arm64.c` (filter 249–262),
and `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-authority-osfmk-kern-ipc_tt.c` (conversion
2398–2434). Existing source identities and primary release links are preserved;
no fresh platform execution evidence was added.

**Stop and decision consequence:** unconditional alternate-CONTROL acquisition
exclusion is not yet closed. Prefer an explicit entry guard over inferring
protection from private-entitlement absence or unrelated self denial. Root
Return 21 accepts this narrow source/conditional argument after independent exact-
source inspection, with no substantive return. The smallest subsequent action is to establish the
actual Mach-filter binding for this exact guard using applicable primary source
or a separately reviewed discriminating method; an already-denied baseline is
not attribution, and unchanged self-query reruns are not proposed. This binding question does not make an artificial dependency for the remaining
route argument: independent acquisition/derivation and held-right audits can
proceed separately. Full coverage still requires each applicable boundary to be
resolved; this pass does not claim that closure.

## Mach-96 filter attribution — handler-entry discriminator

September 20: authorized 30-minute source/discriminating-method pass. Inspect the
retained XNU dispatch/handler branches and installed Apple policy syntax; stop
at applicable binding evidence or a concrete finite method. No profile/code
changes or runtime. The current macOS policy copies demonstrate syntax use, not
their compiler's private implementation; the public XNU tag is not asserted
binary-identical to this host.

### CLM-03-03-45 — Handler copyout distinguishes two identical failure returns

**Source inference and proposed method accepted in root Return 22.**
The direct CONTROL MAC-hook shortcut is not established by the available source:
[`mac_proc_check_get_task`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/security/mac_process.c)
passes credentials, target identity and flavor to registered policy hooks. Both
ordinary and debug CONTROL handlers invoke that facility, but the private policy
and the cause of the observed trap-45 failure remain unidentified. Common hook
names alone do not prove the observed rejection applies to every debug-control
request. Claim 43's BSD-only result still does not prove Mach-rule binding.

There is a smaller discriminator than obtaining a positive CONTROL right. In
[`debug_control_port_for_pid`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_proc.c),
using a nonzero own PID with `target_tport = MACH_PORT_NULL` fails its initial
`port_name_to_task` conversion. The handler copies its initialized null right to
the supplied writable output word and returns `KERN_FAILURE` (5), before the
CONTROL MAC hook or target-task authorization. The direct call cannot acquire a
right on that source path. A seeded nonzero output therefore becomes zero if
that handler branch executes.

The inspected [arm64 dispatch](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/arm64/bsd_arm64.c)
marshals arguments, then evaluates an applicable Mach filter before calling the
handler. Its rejection jumps past the handler; it does not perform the handler's
null-right copyout. Thus identical `KERN_FAILURE` return codes can have distinct
output-word behavior. This is an inspected source prediction to test against the
actual host, not a current measured fact or a universal undocumented ABI promise.
The retained installed `airlock.sb` explicitly uses `deny syscall-mach` and
`machtrap-number` allow rules, which supports the candidate syntax but does not
resolve its active binding for our profile.

### Minimal method proposal — no producing code or run yet

Use the same sanitized launcher, HVF/resource positives, signing and finite
worker lifecycle. Three sequential workers: unrestricted; original profile;
original profile with **only** `(deny syscall-mach (machtrap-number 96))` appended.
Preserve the READ-only and two-entry profiles as historical inputs. This method
adds neither a peer debug-CONTROL query nor a valid-target debug-control acquisition.
The common probe retains its previously scoped peer diagnostics; those do not
become new peer-authority evidence from this discriminator.

In each cell, execute two direct Mach-96 calls with own PID, null target port and
a valid writable `mach_port_name_t` output, freshly seeded with two distinct
nonzero constants. Compute PID/pointer before binding arm64 syscall registers;
retain exact native call assembly and raw input/output words. The words are
sentinels, never treated as actual rights or deallocated. The adjacent user-space
write/read checks and the common unchanged call site guard against an invalid
output pointer or constant-output oracle. All required launch, self-task-facility,
HVF and own-resource positives must hold in every accepted cell.

| Cell | Required result for both sentinels |
| --- | --- |
| Unrestricted | Exactly Mach 5 and output zero: actual handler-copyout control. Otherwise stop invalid/inapplicable. |
| Original profile | Exactly Mach 5 and output zero: the same handler branch remains observable. A preserved sentinel, missing output, other error or failed positive stops before amendment; no widening. |
| Mach-96-only amendment | Exactly Mach 5 with the original sentinel unchanged, with every positive intact. Zero output means the handler still ran; any other output/return, crash or failed positive is not accepted attribution. |

Exact Mach 5 is a deliberately conservative success criterion. A different
legitimate denial may exist, but this method records it as inconclusive rather
than interpreting arbitrary errors as filter evidence or changing its oracle
after the run. Requiring the original-profile copyout avoids the old mistake of
crediting an already-denied baseline. If the original profile already suppresses
the copyout, stop with that observation; this does not independently identify
which private rejection mechanism caused it.

A successful difference would attribute suppression of this exact handler branch
to the sole added Mach-96 rule, consistent with pre-dispatch denial. The inspected
filter hook receives process and call number, not target PID; generalizing the
rule to valid targets remains a **source-conditioned inference**, not a measured
peer operation. It neither proves native instruction filtering for all entries
nor revokes held rights or closes the other acquisition/derivation routes.

Preparation, if independently accepted, must add a distinct mode/profile and
pure oracle with synthetic wrong-output/return/identity/positive-control cases,
retain a fresh explicit-schema snapshot, and obtain pre-execution review. Keep
at most three workers, one peer plus one worker concurrently, one 16-KiB/one-vCPU
native guest per worker, 20 seconds per worker and 100 seconds total; inherited
unlimited VM CPU, finite file/descriptor limits and process-group kill/reap remain.
No retry, automatic replication, profile widening or signing changes. No runtime
budget or executable-method readiness is granted here.

Exact inspected copies remain
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-authority-bsd-kern-kern_proc.c` (initial debug handler),
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-osfmk-arm64-bsd_arm64.c` (argument/filter/
dispatch ordering), `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-security-mac_process.c`
(hook forwarding), and `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-protection-installed-airlock.sb`
(Mach policy syntax). This bounded pass stops at root review of that concrete
method. Independent work on other capability routes need not wait for it.

## Mach return 53 — primary-source explanation, original oracle preserved

September 20: authorized 20-minute source-only explanation. Inspect the current
SDK constant, the matching public XNU constant and trap-initializer definitions,
and the already retained filter/return path. Stop at concrete applicability and
the smallest next architectural action. No binary execution, profile change,
or alteration of the original exact-5 oracle/retained run.

### CLM-03-03-47 — Return 53 names policy denial and can bypass handler copyout

**Source finding and bounded interpretation accepted in root Return 24.**
The installed SDK 26.4 `usr/include/mach/kern_return.h` defines `KERN_DENIED` as
53 with the description “Denied by security policy.” The pinned public
[XNU kern_return.h](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/kern_return.h#L326)
has the same definition. Its `KERN_FAILURE` 5 is a separate catch-all code. Thus
claim 46's raw 53 is a named Mach policy-denial result, not BSD errno 53, nor
synonymous with the method's required 5. The native call reports x0 directly and
does not translate it through the BSD carry/errno convention.

In the inspected [arm64 dispatch](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/arm64/bsd_arm64.c#L249),
`mac_task_mach_trap_evaluate` returns a `retval`; any non-success skips the handler.
Only traps marked `mach_trap_returns_port` replace that value with a null port.
The [MACH_TRAP initializer](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/syscall_sw.h#L110)
uses designated fields, leaving unspecified fields zero; the [trap-96 entry](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/syscall_sw.c#L206)
does not set that flag. A callback-returned 53 can therefore reach user x0
unchanged while skipping this handler's output-word copyout. The interface does
not require every policy denial to become KERN_FAILURE 5.

The same dispatch also has a separate `CONFIG_DEBUG_SYSCALL_REJECTION` branch
that explicitly produces `KERN_DENIED` before handler dispatch for ordinary
return-valued traps. Its actual enablement on this host is not established.
Therefore the return constant alone cannot identify which private/current kernel
branch produced it. Published XNU is still a release-source reference, not an
assertion that the running private policy callback has been inspected.

**Interpretation of existing evidence:** exact 53 plus preserved output words
is consistent with policy rejection before the inspected handler's null copyout.
The original/base cells' zeroed outputs and identical call site distinguish this
from a missing call in those controls. That is a source-conditioned explanation
of the finite observation, not accepted attribution of all Mach-96 valid-target
requests or a newly accepted filter mechanism. Claim 46 remains the accepted
negative/inconclusive report under its original exact-5 method. Neither its
assessment nor producing code is changed; there was no new run or replication.
The method mismatch is not evidence that the guard failed to deny an operation.

Exact new retained primary files:

| File | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-mach53-kern_return.h` | 10008 | `4577d58f74dfbb8ef07ce460f82cb7a45999ad2a0479486df8d22658d4847f50` |
| `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-mach53-syscall_sw.h` | 4407 | `c8d8476d174b9a8ff8f0df865281ccc3b12d7a3b7f81ff1015afb76728b17691` |

Current-SDK definition was read at
`/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk/usr/include/mach/kern_return.h`;
`xcrun --show-sdk-version` reports 26.4. Other dispatch/table inputs are the
unchanged retained files cited in claim 45. Fetches were bounded public header
reads only.

and grants no execution. Other capability-route closure remains independent.

## Production entry authority — descriptor choice and token boundary

September 20: authorized 30-minute source/design pass after claim 48. Trace
consequential held-right derivation and actual descriptor inheritance, then choose
a concrete production entry/grant boundary. Stop at a reviewable choice and the
first remaining exclusion premise. No runtime, policy modification or repeated
Mach test. Existing source claims apply only to their release/host scope.

### CLM-03-03-49 — Select explicit descriptor inheritance and byte-only broker transport

**Source-backed conditional design, accepted in root Return 29.** Production
launch must not inherit the research harness's ambient standard streams as a
policy. The tested `close_fds/pass_fds` wrapper and sanitized Mach diagnostics
establish finite fixtures; they do not identify every production handle. Select
one explicit launch manifest with only the resources below, prepared by the
trusted supervisor before worker entry:

| Resource | Selected authority and binding |
| --- | --- |
| fd 0 | Dedicated read-only `/dev/null`, not the parent's terminal/input descriptor. |
| fd 1 and fd 2 | Write ends of bounded per-VM logging pipes. The reader tags bytes with the originating VM and never treats text as broker commands, paths or capability requests. No shared writable log-file descriptor enters a worker. |
| Owned block image | Preopened regular-file descriptor with access mode matching the selected VM disk. No directory descriptor or other VM's writable backing. Supervisor validates object identity/type and ownership before grant; a user-facing path string is not an enduring capability check. |
| Control/backend transport | Two unidirectional byte pipes between this worker and its trusted supervisor/broker. The manifest identifies direction, owner and finite protocol capacity. No Mach endpoint, Unix ancillary-right transport, listener or general-purpose network socket is granted through this channel. |
| Local runtime resources | Own task/thread/HVF facilities and private allocations required by the already scoped runtime; no peer task/READ/CONTROL/identity token grant. Library loading stays inside the selected profile, not an arbitrary file-open broker. |

Use `POSIX_SPAWN_CLOEXEC_DEFAULT` plus explicit file actions to bind 0/1/2 and
inherit only manifest descriptors. The current SDK 26.4 declares the flag and
`posix_spawn_file_actions_addinherit_np`. In pinned
[XNU kern_exec.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c#L3178),
spawn marks only applicable OPEN/DUP2/INHERIT destinations `UF_INHERIT`; in
[kern_descrip.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_descrip.c#L789),
the exec descriptor pass closes close-on-exec files and, under that flag, files
not explicitly marked to inherit. A failed action/identity check stops launch.
This is an explicit source-supported recipe, not implemented by the existing
research launcher. Do not assume descriptors 0–2 are safe because their numbers
are conventional. Descriptor numbers, object identity, type, access mode and
purpose all belong in the launch manifest.

The byte-pipe choice makes descriptor/Mach-right transfer absent from the
transport format itself rather than relying on a Unix socket receiver to ignore
SCM_RIGHTS. The SDK identifies SCM_RIGHTS as a socket ancillary rights operation;
ordinary pipe payloads are bytes. Broker messages contain bounded operation IDs,
lengths, VM/incarnation identity and payload/status, with no arbitrary PID/path,
FD-number duplication, Mach right, launch command or memory-address operation.
Network packets and entropy can arrive as bounded broker bytes; selecting actual
network endpoints/policy remains a separate backend choice. This does not promise
zero copies or accept network performance. The initial baseline grants are static;
adding a resource requires a separately admitted manifest transition or restart,
not interpretation of worker-supplied numbers as authority.

Kernel conversion of an allowed file to a fileport does not erase its provenance:
`sys_fileport_makeport` looks up an existing descriptor/fileglob before producing
a send right. It can repackage a permitted file capability; it does not select
an arbitrary peer file from a numeric request. This narrows the consequence of
such derivation, without claiming every file operation is automatically safe.
No log/control pipe is a generic capability broker.

**Memory composition:** a disk/pipe descriptor grants I/O authority, not guest-RAM
commit authority. Returned bytes remain host-owned until the per-VM coordinator
admits the exact destination/route/policy/lifetime operation. An accepted direct-
host RAM window is within that same VM worker and ends before guest release;
backend completion/interrupt follows committed bytes and tracked invalidation.
Compiler inputs are immutable admitted copies, not unrestricted live RAM reads.
This preserves claim 30's owner boundary and the reviewed finite handoff evidence;
memory's production direct-window proposal is independently reviewed in INV-03-04.
Neither transport nor memory grants access to a peer worker's state.

### CLM-03-03-50 — Token conversion is a separate held-authority boundary

**Scoped source finding and explicit remaining premise, accepted in root Return 29.** Pinned
[XNU task_ident.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/task_ident.c)
implements `task_identity_token_get_task_port` independently of the PID-acquisition
entries tested in claims 43/48. A held token resolves an exact task identity; a
requested READ/CONTROL flavor undergoes `task_conversion_eval`, and non-self
conversion additionally calls `mac_task_check_task_id_token_get_task`. Successful
conversion produces a task port. A self CONTROL conversion has a distinct fast
path. This is not an unconditional token-to-peer-CONTROL escalation, but denying
PID traps does not remove the token conversion mechanism or prove its private
policy denies every peer target.

The current SDK `mach/task.defs` declares `task_create_identity_token(task_t,...)`,
requiring a task capability rather than accepting a PID or NAME-only request.
The already inspected flavor conversion and `special_port_allowed_with_task_flavor`
reject simple INSPECT/NAME-to-READ/CONTROL upgrades; READ can request only READ,
INSPECT or NAME special ports, INSPECT only INSPECT or NAME. These close those
specific naive upgrades, not all possible token producers or exceptions.

**Selected conditional entry invariant:** no peer task right, peer identity token,
peer exception capability, privileged broker endpoint or other peer resource may
be present at worker entry; none is transferred later through the selected byte
pipes. Own-task token/right derivations remain own-task authority. Null bootstrap,
empty registered ports and task/thread exception actions are retained as explicit
launch premises, not an exhaustive proof that every inherited right is absent.
Normal host/task-access facilities are not silently cleared or presumed harmless
by a diagnostic port count.

**Concrete remaining exclusion:** the source and existing finite launch records
do not yet establish that the entire production Mach entry space lacks a peer
identity token or a service endpoint that can supply one. Nor do they establish
that every other process-control route is denied. The callback used for token
conversion is different from the observed PID rule; it cannot inherit empirical
acceptance by name. Therefore full peer isolation remains open even with explicit
FD closure. This is a provenance/admission gap, not an observed exploit.

The next smallest source/design action is to trace the actual process-creation
Mach-space inheritance/reset boundary and enumerate only the capabilities that
survive the selected exec/launch, including system-created special rights. If
that establishes absence plus the no-transfer invariant, no repeated token/PID
probe is required merely to prove an unreachable peer-token use. If it exposes
an indispensable reachable peer-authority endpoint, name and restrict that exact
endpoint or its operation rather than declare default-deny universally sufficient.
Other independent route checks can proceed without waiting on this one.

New primary files are pinned to `xnu-12377.1.9`:

- `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-capability-task_ident.c`, SHA-256
  `f23c85a60448b1c75409bb9fb70cf939b79a84376ee2d9069d781711335f1656`.
- `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-capability-kern_descrip.c`, SHA-256
  `480cfed4e987be874bd71fb6933c254adf9fb1f36de8496dee8f351b18da13b1`.

Existing retained `kern_exec.c` and `ipc_tt.c` supply the cited spawn/flavor
branches. SDK26.4 headers `spawn.h`, `sys/spawn.h`, `sys/socket.h` and
`mach/task.defs` provide the current declarations. Public directory lookup was
unavailable (403); a proposed alternate identity filename returned 404, while
`task_ident.c` was retrieved successfully. No claim depends on failed retrievals.

Stop at root review of the explicit descriptor/transport choice and token
provenance gap. The choice is conditional production design, not a claim that
the current probe implements its full manifest. No code, profile, signing or VM
execution changed, and no further runtime budget is requested by this pass.

## Mach entry provenance — bounded source follow-up

Root source pass, September 20, following Return 29: trace ordinary new-process
`posix_spawn` through task/IPC initialization and identify retained special
capabilities. Budget 25 minutes, no native execution, policy change or new probe.
The expected distinction is a fresh ordinary IPC namespace versus explicitly
inherited special rights; falsifier is a source path copying arbitrary parent
rights or granting peer authority outside the proposed manifest. Stop at a
reviewable scoped source result and exact unresolved service boundary. This
cannot establish private policy behavior merely from the public kernel pin.

### CLM-03-03-51 — New-task IPC space narrows inheritance; special endpoints remain explicit

**Source-backed and inferred, independently accepted in machine Return 30.** Select
ordinary new-process `posix_spawn` (without `POSIX_SPAWN_SETEXEC`) for this entry
argument. In pinned [kern_exec.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c),
the new-process branch calls `fork1(... PROC_CREATE_SPAWN ...)`.
[kern_fork.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_fork.c)
passes `CLONEPROC_SPAWN` through `cloneproc`/`fork_create_child` to
`task_create_internal`, with memory inheritance false. Main-thread register
copying through `thread_dup` occurs only in the non-spawn branch.
[task.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/task.c)
calls `ipc_task_init(new_task, parent_task)`.

In [ipc_tt.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_tt.c),
`ipc_task_init` creates a fresh IPC space and fresh own-task CONTROL/NAME ports;
READ/INSPECT are allocated on demand. Debug-control, dyld notification and
resource-notification state start empty. This initializer does not copy the
parent's arbitrary IPC name table. Consequently a peer identity token merely
held in that table is not automatically inherited through this path. This is a
narrow source inference, not a complete census of ports later acquired by
startup libraries, explicit spawn actions or broker interactions.

The initializer **does explicitly inherit** registered ports, task exception
actions, host, bootstrap and task-access special ports. `exec_handle_port_actions`
can additionally install supplied special/exception/registered/watch ports.
The production launch must therefore admit its port actions explicitly, alongside
claim 49's FD manifest. Clearing bootstrap/registered/exception channels addresses
those channels only; it is not a general empty-namespace proof. Own task/thread
ports constructed by the kernel confer own-task authority, not peer authority.

One concrete correction to a possible cleanup assumption matters:
`task_set_special_port_internal(TASK_ACCESS_PORT, ...)` refuses to overwrite an
already valid task-access port, including with null. `TASK_HOST_PORT` replacement
also has a SIP-related access restriction in the public setter. Thus the design
must not promise that worker startup can simply clear every inherited special
port. `task_get_special_port_from_user` reaches the task-access copied-send branch
subject to its MAC check and accepted task flavor; user retrieval is not
unconditionally permitted. The remaining endpoint must be
classified, or its absence established as an actual launcher premise.

This is **not evidence that task-access grants arbitrary peer rights**.
[kern_proc.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_proc.c)
uses a target's task-access endpoint for a taskgated authorization upcall after
other checks; the inspected upcall returns an authorization result, and the
kernel separately produces the task right. Its name or presence alone proves
neither a peer capability nor an exploitable bypass. The next source action is
to classify that endpoint's exposed message contract and the inherited host-port
flavor, then bind admission to the actual launcher context. Private service
policy and runtime presence remain unestablished.

Finally, `ipc_task_reset` is a security-transition reset of task-port flavors and
selected exception/debug state. The exec caller invokes it conditionally for
credential/MAC changes. Neither its name nor routine exec establishes a universal
right purge. This finding makes no claim about every exec/SETEXEC variant; they
are outside the selected creation path here.

**Decision consequence:** the residual inheritance obligation is a finite set of
special/startup-granted endpoints, rather than arbitrary copying of every parent
Mach right. Claim 50's complete entry-provenance gap narrows but does not close.
The repeated PID/trap tests remain unnecessary for this source question. No
profile, launcher code or native execution changed. Public XNU pin evidence is
not an assertion that every private installed-kernel policy matches that source.

New retained primary files are `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-capability-kern_fork.c`
(SHA-256 `73b578159deebc71434185d3638ab1468f995a19c882e1902d001994bb0391c3`)
and `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-capability-task.c`
(`f5ea4822b15aca636c4d940679e878904729b88acf312821d2ebacbf69312f71`). Existing
retained `kern_exec.c`, `ipc_tt.c` and `kern_proc.c` supply the other cited paths.
The first network fetch failed DNS resolution inside the sandbox; the authorized
network fetch succeeded. No conclusion relies on that failed retrieval.

**Stopping point:** independent machine source review of this scoped inheritance
result, followed by the exact special-endpoint classification above. No runtime
budget is requested or implied.

## Inherited task-access and host endpoints — source/design continuation

**Method, September 20:** bounded 25-minute primary-source pass after accepted
claim 51. Inspect the pinned XNU endpoint messages, host-port construction and
startup acquisition paths. Distinguish an authorization reply from transfer of
peer rights, and an ordinary host send right from a privileged host capability.
Stop at a concrete launch admission rule or the first missing service-policy
premise. No policy probe, native execution, profile change or repeated PID test.
The public release pin does not identify all private installed service behavior.

### CLM-03-03-52 — Scalar task-access replies and ordinary host grants require different admission rules

**Source-backed and conditional design, accepted in root Return 31.** The
public XNU `xnu-12377.1.9` message contract narrows the task-access concern, but
ordinary host authority exposes a concrete additional service-acquisition route.
This finding does not establish the installed private service's complete message
dispatch or sandbox policy.

**Task-access is not a task-right reply in the inspected interface.**
[`task_access.defs`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/task_access.defs)
defines `check_task_access`, `find_code_signature` and
`check_task_access_with_flavor`. Their declared requests carry PID/GID/flavor
values as applicable; none declares an output task port, identity token or other
capability. The access-check requests carry a server audit token separately from
the claimed numeric caller fields. This establishes the message shape, not that
an arbitrary receiver correctly authenticates or implements only these messages.
In the already retained `kern_proc.c`, the kernel sends the authorization upcall
after its earlier checks and subsequently constructs/copies out the requested
task right itself. A successful direct service reply is not itself that right
and is not a transferable kernel authorization ticket. No taskgated bypass is
inferred. [Kernel caller](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_proc.c#L5671).

There is also a startup use: `kern_exec.c` calls `find_code_signature` for its
externally attached-signature path. A signature supplied through image activation
with `CS_SIGNED` skips that path in the inspected routine. The result determines
whether exec continues; it does not return a peer capability to the worker.
Consequently removing a target's task-access endpoint is not a justified generic
cleanup step, even apart from the valid-port overwrite restriction in claim 51.
The production executable must have its intended signed activation path; this
source branch does not prove the full installed signing/service behavior.
[Exec caller](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c#L7985).

**Host flavor has an explicit credential-dependent construction.** `ipc_task_init`
initially copies the parent's host special right without attenuation:
`host_port_copy_send` preserves ordinary versus privileged host type. However,
the selected ordinary spawn path subsequently calls `set_security_token`.
`kern_prot.c` selects `HOST_PRIV_NULL` for a nonzero credential UID; UID zero can
receive privileged host authority unless the MAC check removes it.
`task_set_security_tokens` installs the corresponding ordinary or privileged host
port through the internal setter. Thus a concrete production premise is an
unprivileged non-root launcher/worker, with no credential-changing executable or
explicit port action that defeats this construction. Merely calling the process
“sandboxed” is not that premise. [Spawn](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_fork.c#L296),
[credential selection](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/kern_prot.c#L1668),
[installation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/task.c#L5311).

`host_self_trap` returns the task's stored host right. The kernel's ordinary
host converter accepts `IKOT_HOST` or `IKOT_HOST_PRIV`; privileged conversion
requires both the privileged type and equality with the current task's stored
host port. An ordinary host right does not pass that privileged conversion.
This is source support for a flavor boundary, not proof that ordinary host
operations cannot grant further capabilities.
[Host conversion and copying](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_host.c#L244).

**Concrete remaining route: ordinary host to IOKit main.**
`mach_host.defs` declares `host_get_io_main(host_t, out io_main_t)`; its compatibility
name is `host_get_io_master`. The implementation checks for a non-null ordinary
host and obtains a send right to `main_device_port` through the kernel host
special-port helper. The inspected body requires no bootstrap lookup. Therefore
null bootstrap, no arbitrary inherited IPC table and a no-right-transfer private
broker do not by themselves prevent this service acquisition. This is not a
claim that the IOKit main port grants peer memory, nor that current sandbox
policy permits the call: kernel message filtering and downstream service/user-
client policy remain separate.
[Ordinary-host interface](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/mach_host.defs#L145),
[implementation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/host.c#L1429).

The inspected public `mach_init_doit` acquires own-task and reply ports and reads
page parameters from the commpage; it is not evidence of an IOKit dependency.
It is also only one initializer. Dyld, other libraries and Hypervisor framework
initialization cannot inherit a no-service-acquisition conclusion from it.
[Mach startup](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/libsyscall/mach/mach_init.c#L133).

**Conditional admission choice:** retain normal non-root host facilities, but
classify every further capability-returning service operation actually admitted.
Do not grant a blanket exemption for “host” or “task-access.” No worker-originated
task-access RPC is required by the inspected kernel-upcall path; its user-message
access should be denied unless separately justified, without pretending this
removes the kernel's retained endpoint. For `host_get_io_main`, prefer denial in
the worker unless indispensable initialization requires it. If required, admit
that acquisition only with a named downstream service/user-client contract and
its exclusion of peer/resource authority. Both are proposed enforcement rules,
not claims that the current profile implements them or that a message-ID filter
has already been bound correctly. Own-task/reply facilities remain necessary;
ordinary host queries and kernel-mediated signing are not interchangeable with
arbitrary service messages.

**Stopping point and smallest next action:** the inherited public task-access
protocol is no longer an unexplained peer-right output. The first concrete
unclosed derived endpoint is ordinary-host IOKit acquisition, not an observed
exploit. After independent review, a proposed bounded 25-minute source/policy
binding pass should identify the exact host-get-IO-main dispatch/filter hook and
whether the selected HVF/startup path requires that acquisition. Resolve deny
versus explicitly admitted downstream authority; do not repeat unchanged PID
queries or launch a broad policy campaign. Other authority routes can be audited
independently. Complete private-service dispatch and production startup provenance
remain open.

**Retention/applicability:** eight successful primary downloads are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-special-endpoints-01/`; `sources.json` records their
exact original URLs, sizes and SHA-256 identities. The operative new files are
`osfmk-mach-task_access.defs`, `osfmk-kern-ipc_host.c`, `osfmk-kern-host.c`,
`osfmk-mach-mach_host.defs`, `bsd-kern-kern_prot.c` and
`libsyscall-mach-mach_init.c`. `host_priv.defs`/`host_security.defs` were inspected
for interface distinction only; no obsolete host-security routine is assumed
implemented. Existing retained claim-51 fork/task/IPC/exec/proc files complete the
chains. Sandbox DNS initially failed; authorized source retrieval succeeded.
Two guessed paths (`osfmk/mach/host.defs`, `iokit/Kernel/IOKitServer.cpp`) returned
404 and supply no evidence. No native execution, profile edit or runtime budget
was used. The public release pin is not an identification of the installed binary
or all private service implementations.


## Ordinary-host IOKit dispatch — bounded policy binding

**Method, September 20:** 25-minute source/design continuation after accepted
claim 52. Trace the exact ordinary-host IOKit MIG dispatch and filter interface;
inspect actual primary startup/HVF information for a required service acquisition.
Stop at an enforceable conditional exclusion or a precise private-platform
premise. No native execution, policy probe, signing or profile edit. Existing
finite HVF success does not by itself identify all framework service requests.

### CLM-03-03-53 — Host IOKit acquisition has a distinct kernel-MIG filter boundary

**Source-backed and conditional policy choice, accepted in root Return 32.**
SDK 26.4 `mach/mach_host.h` maps `host_get_io_main` to message **205**. The pinned
`mach_host.defs` preserves the subsystem position while selecting the older
`host_get_io_master` spelling for a sandbox compiler without
`SANDBOX_SUPPORTS_HOST_IOMAIN`. This is a kernel-object MIG message, not a bootstrap
name lookup and not Mach trap 96 or BSD syscall 539.
[Interface](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/mach_host.defs#L145).

In pinned `ipc_kobject.c`, the server resolves the message ID to a MIG routine.
Before invoking that routine, it obtains the current task's kobject filter mask
and the routine's registered filter index. If a mask exists, the index is absent
or not allowed, and the policy callback exists, it calls
`mac_task_kobj_msg_evaluate(proc, message_id, index)`. A non-success result becomes
the reply error and skips the kernel routine. A mask bit that permits the call,
an absent mask, or an absent callback does not follow this rejection path. The
public code therefore exposes an appropriate pre-handler enforcement boundary;
it does not prove that any particular SBPL text installs the required mask and
policy. `ipc_kobject_set_kobjidx` associates a message ID with its filter index;
`task.c` provides mask installation and callback registration. The actual private
policy's naming/index association cannot be inferred solely from this API.
[Dispatch](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_kobject.c#L560),
[index registration](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/ipc_kobject.c#L365),
[policy installation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/task.c#L9926).

There is concrete current-system policy syntax rather than a guessed operation
name. The installed Apple `/System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/airlock.sb`, on the
recorded macOS 26.6.2 host, uses `syscall-mig` with a `kernel-mig-routine` list
containing `host_get_io_master`. That file's surrounding policy is permissive
with telemetry; its list is evidence of the shipped spelling, **not an observed
exclusion**. This supports preparing the following isolated candidate rule:

```scheme
(deny syscall-mig (kernel-mig-routine host_get_io_master))
```

The rule is recorded here only; no profile has been edited or compiled in this
pass. It must bind message 205 on the installed compiler/kernel before it can
establish acquisition denial. The message hook is separate from the already
observed trap filter, so claims 43/48 cannot establish this binding. Denying
message 205 also does not revoke an already held IOKit port, cover all possible
IOKit acquisition paths, or deny all messages on a service port. Claim 49–52's
entry/provenance obligations remain necessary. Task-access messages sent to a
user-space receiver are not automatically covered by this kernel-object hook.

**Startup/HVF requirement remains precisely unproved.** Apple documents HVF as
an entitled user-space API and describes process-local VM creation, mapping and
per-thread vCPU execution; it does not specify an IOKit-main acquisition contract.
SDK `hv_vm.h` exposes no caller-supplied IOKit port, while its export stub is not
an implementation or an import/call graph. The public Mach initializer inspected
in claim 52 does not establish all dyld/library initialization. Public
`hv_support_kext.c` has task/thread trap dispatch, but that alone neither
identifies every Apple-Silicon framework operation nor excludes an earlier
IOKit service setup. No inference from an Intel/private historical backend is
substituted for the current M4 path.
[Apple HVF overview](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/hypervisor),
[public trap layer](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/kern/hv_support_kext.c#L224).

The retained successful restricted fixture loads HVF after applying its base
profile in the before-initialization cells, but that base profile has no explicit
`syscall-mig`/host-get-IO-main denial. Its bootstrap lookup restriction cannot be
used to prove absence of this different acquisition route. Existing results stay
valid in their original scope; they neither prove a dependency nor exclude one.
The private framework executable is not a standalone file at the inspected
framework path on this host, and the SDK stub supplies no implementation proof.
No cache extraction, instrumentation or native experiment was attempted.

**Decision:** provisionally exclude this unnecessary-until-demonstrated worker
acquisition via the narrow message-205 policy boundary. Do not add a general
IOKit grant merely because ordinary host authority can obtain its main port.
A required acquisition would instead reopen the named downstream service/user-
client authority contract before admission. The unresolved load-bearing premise
is now exact: the installed sandbox must bind that rule to pre-handler rejection
of message 205, while the required restricted HVF startup/run/map path must still
work. The source supports the boundary and shipped syntax, not those combined
runtime facts or complete containment.

**Proposed next bounded action, unstarted:** prepare one narrow discriminating
method, using a known-positive `host_get_io_main` control and unchanged HVF
startup/map/run operations, with the single added rule separated from the base
profile. A base denial is not evidence of the added rule; no acquired port may
silently be carried from a control into the restricted worker. Fix exact reply/
right-presence and required-API outcomes before review; do not guess a universal
error code from the callback source. Any native decision-evidence run needs its
own reviewed method and budget. No run is authorized by this finding.

**Retention:** `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-iomain-dispatch-01/sources.json` records
six newly downloaded public XNU files and four exact local Apple primary copies,
with origin, byte count and SHA-256. The local copies include the installed
`airlock.sb`, SDK `mach_host.h`, `hv_vm.h` and `Hypervisor.tbd`; their precise
platform scope differs from the public XNU release pin. The existing retained
`task.c` and `hv_support_kext.c` supply the cited installation/trap paths. No claim
rests on absence of a search result or on assuming the private platform equals
the public release source.

## MIG205 acquisition fixture — preparation only

### CLM-03-03-54 — Proposed discriminating method; no execution evidence

**Method declared before code changes, September 20; 30-minute preparation bound.**
Reuse the existing disposable supervisor, sanitized launcher, native MOV/HVC
fixture, signing inputs and base profile. Add only one separate profile containing
the base bytes plus claim 53's `syscall-mig`/`host_get_io_master` denial, and an
explicit `--iomain-filter` mode with manifest schema v6. This authorizes source,

sandbox invocation, native execution or a runtime budget.

The proposed later matrix has three fresh sanitized workers, in exact order:
unrestricted/base, restriction-before-HVF/base, restriction-before-HVF/amended.
Each calls SDK `host_get_io_main(mach_host_self(), ...)` once after its policy
point and before loading HVF. It starts the output at null, records the return,
returned name and whether a live send right exists, and releases acquired send
rights before HVF initialization. It never sends an IOKit service request or
opens a user client. No IOKit right passes between cells. The fixture cannot
assert that unrelated startup code never acquired another right.

Both first cells must acquire a live send right and deallocate it successfully,
with the existing host/own-task, launch, HVF, disk/channel and unrestricted peer
positive checks, before the amendment is admitted. Base denial, missing output,
invalid launch or failed positive operations stops without amendment attribution.
The amended cell records success, failure and exact return separately. Nonzero
return with null/no-right is **not automatically a permission-denial PASS**: its
meaning needs independent findings review against that actual return path.
Success with a send right falsifies this candidate exclusion. A non-null invalid
or unclassifiable returned name, cleanup failure, transport error or absent row
cannot be turned into policy acceptance. Required HVF setup/map/run/destroy
positives must still complete; an acquisition failure alone does not establish
compatibility. Do not revise the oracle after observing a return.

If separately reviewed and authorized, retain the existing maximum three workers
and one harmless peer, 100-second execution phase, 20-second worker bound, one

a CPU setter, confined temporary file/socket fixtures and no retries/widening.
Current task performs no such run. Synthetic cases must reject changed cell

Cleanup failure stops before HVF. Each cell is a new process, not a reset of a

## Entropy device and admitted completion (claim 56; source/design)

**Method, 2026-09-20:** bounded 30-minute primary-source/design pass, begun
at approximately 11:39 UTC. Inspect the selected QEMU pin's entropy queue and
backend, Linux v6.12 consumer, Virtio 1.3 entropy requirements and Apple SDK
26.4 native entropy contract. Compose a finite request/reset/commit interface
with INV-03-04 claims 26–29; stop at a reviewable entropy subchoice or precise
missing architectural premise. No native call, VM run or implementation is
authorized by this pass. Primary copies and hashes are retained under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-entropy-01/sources.json`. An attempted
`bsd/kern/kern_getentropy.c` URL at the public XNU pin returned 404; it supplies
no evidence. The installed Apple SDK manual and declaration below supply the
host API contract instead.

**CLM-03-03-56 — scoped choice independently accepted in root Return35.** Select
virtio entropy device ID 4 on the existing modern MMIO transport, queue 0,
split ring with maximum size **8**, and only the common `VERSION_1` feature.
No entropy-specific features/configuration, indirect descriptors, packed ring,
`EVENT_IDX`, `IN_ORDER`, access-platform or optional ring-reset feature are
exposed. Ordinary queue disable/full-device reset remain implemented. This
closes the entropy interface choice conditionally on the existing transport,
RAM-service and interrupt contracts; it neither selects a code generator nor
asserts a tested device implementation or whole-machine completion.

### Source basis and exact reuse boundary

[Virtio 1.3 §5.4](https://docs.oasis-open.org/virtio/virtio/v1.3/virtio-v1.3.html)
identifies the device and sole request queue and defines no device-specific
features/configuration. Its normative §5.4.6.2 explicitly permits a positive
short fill, despite the preceding descriptive sentence about completely filling
a buffer; the driver must use returned length and supply only writable buffers.
Consequently a capped positive prefix is sufficient: no new size-limit feature
or promise to fill an arbitrarily large request is needed.

At QEMU `c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`,
[`virtio-rng.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/virtio/virtio-rng.c)
creates an eight-entry queue, passes through common feature bits, gates service
on queue readiness/`DRIVER_OK`, and copies entropy through `in_sg` before pushing
actual length and notifying. It also has a virtual-time quota timer. Reuse the
queue/device interface and inspected split-ring machinery; **do not invoke its
unchanged `chr_read` against guest-backed IOVs outside an admitted RAM window**.
The owner/readiness tests are not a replacement for the incarnation, source
invalidation and all-observer transaction below. The inspected
[`rng-random.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/backends/rng-random.c)
opens a device path and uses an FD callback/read/assertion policy. That backend
is not selected, and the device's default builtin backend is not evidence for
our native backend's behavior.

[Linux v6.12 `virtio-rng.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/char/hw_random/virtio-rng.c)
posts one writable buffer of `max(32, SMP_CACHE_BYTES)` bytes, publishes returned
length with release ordering, consumes only those bytes, and posts a new buffer
when depleted. Its callback tolerates spurious interrupts. Removal marks the
consumer removed, wakes waiters, resets the device and deletes queues. These
paths support short positive completions and this small queue; they do not
establish vendor boot, entropy credit policy or throughput.

Apple's retained [SDK 26.4 getentropy manual](/private/tmp/natemu-track03-entropy-01/sdk26.4-getentropy.2)
and [declaration](/private/tmp/natemu-track03-entropy-01/sdk26.4-random.h)
provide `getentropy(void *, size_t)`, available since macOS 10.12. The manual
limits requests to 256 bytes, describes kernel random bytes without a device
FD, and explicitly describes availability in a sandbox/chroot. It reports
failure for invalid output memory, oversized requests or a fatal error. The
manual generally recommends higher-level random APIs for ordinary clients;
here the deliberate small-volume interface exports kernel-generated entropy
without constructing a new userspace generator. This source contract does not
prove a particular private production filter permits the syscall, or promise a
hard host-call latency bound. A filter denial is a backend failure below, not a
reason to broaden process authority silently.

### Finite backend and consumption contract

One entropy service lane in the per-VM worker performs native acquisition; the
single device owner continues processing reset/control events. It has **one**
in-flight job, one private **256-byte** staging buffer, one bounded result slot
and one captured chain of at most eight direct descriptors. No entropy FD,
IOKit right, broker-delivered seed, guest-controlled path or custom PRNG is
needed. Other available heads remain in the guest queue until that slot retires;
there is no unbounded host request list. The owner handles at most one completion
per dispatch turn and requeues work so other device/control events can progress.
No extra entropy quota clock is selected; this chooses bounded storage and fair
service, not a throughput guarantee or measured optimal thread count.

After `DRIVER_OK` and queue readiness, snapshot/validate the next chain under the
existing admitted read interface. Capture incarnation, head, a monotonically
unique operation ID and exact writable prefix routes; reject loops, illegal
flags, overflow, invalid routes and zero total capacity through the existing
malformed-device/fail-contained path. Set `n = min(writable capacity, 256)`,
with `n >= 1`; address/range validation must not allocate proportional to the
advertised total byte count. Reserve the result and completion capacity before
launching exactly one `getentropy(staging, n)` call. No host call or wait holds a
RAM grant. Success retains those exact bytes until commit or cancellation;
notifications, retransmitted requests and coordinator retries do not resample
or create another operation for the same dequeued head.

A native error discards private bytes and stops the affected VM through the
existing contained host-failure path. There is no entropy request status field:
we do not invent `IOERR`, zero-fill, publish a zero-length success, spin-retry,
or keep a running guest indefinitely waiting on an unreported backend error.
Unexpectedly stalled native acquisition leaves the device owner responsive;
a management stop may contain/terminate that worker, but cannot report a
successful drained reset or reuse its live slot. This is an explicit availability
cost of a fatal host entropy failure, not a normal guest entropy result.

### RAM publication, reset and late results

A ready result obtains the same resident-authorized finite service window as
INV-03-04 claims 26–29. Revalidate incarnation/admission and retained routes;
commit the `n` entropy bytes over the captured writable prefix, then the used
entry with exact length `n`, then used index with the split-ring ordering. All
payload and ring writes, including aliases of executable bytes or paging
structures, belong to the tracked write union. There is no status byte for this
device. Retire affected source/shadow generations and reject stale translation
publication before releasing guest observers or publishing the completion's
interrupt offer. Apply the negotiated basic split-ring notification suppression
and the existing typed IRQ transaction, not an independent direct LAPIC write.
An unexpected partial host-store failure retains the contained-VM rule; it does
not create an ordinary entropy completion. Byte-span copy rules and overlapping
physical spans follow the already selected ordered RAM-service semantics.

Completion state changes once from retained result to committed completion.
Duplicate callbacks are discarded by operation ID; they cannot write the ring
again. Exactly-once here means one guest completion and no replay of its sampled
bytes, not a guarantee that the host generator was never advanced by a canceled
operation. A reset first closes the old incarnation to new admissions, lets an
already granted finite commit settle, and cancels every ungranted old result.
The pending native call may return into its still-live private slot; canceled
bytes are discarded, never reassigned to a new request or VM. Reset/queue-disable
acknowledgement waits outside the RAM window until old calls/results, possible
writes and interrupt offers are retired; only then may queue storage/incarnation
be reused. No asynchronous thread cancellation or reset-time waiting on the
device-owner thread is introduced. This also covers a result arriving after
queue readiness was cleared: readiness alone must not relabel it as new work.

**Decision consequence and limits.** The entropy subchoice now has a concrete
profile, native source, finite admission and guest-visible completion/failure
contract. Remaining implementation conformance consists of obeying the shared
transport/ownership contracts and ensuring the final production policy permits
the declared native call; no new architecture choice about entropy status,
capacity, replay or reset is left implicit. No randomness measurement, native
API invocation, guest run or private-policy acceptance occurred. Root independent
review is the next action for this claim; no experiment budget is created.

**Claim56 closure:** root Return35 accepts the entropy profile/backend/completion
choice under its named shared premises. Implementation and final-policy
conformance go to planning; no new entropy experiment is required for this choice.

## Network profile and native backend authority (claim 57)

**Method:** machine-authored source/design pass, September 20, 2026, bounded to
30 minutes. Inspect Linux v6.12, Virtio 1.3, selected QEMU
`c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294` network paths and installed Apple SDK
26.4 vmnet contract. Compose with accepted RAM-window, reset and capability
interfaces. Stop at a concrete subchoice and the first unresolved authority
premise; no native execution, profile changes or implementation. Four primary
files and hashes are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-network-01/sources.json`. A guessed QEMU
`net/vmnet-shared.m` URL returned 404; the actual inspected implementation is
`net/vmnet-common.m`, and mode semantics below come from Apple's SDK.

**CLM-03-03-57 — proposed network interface and conditional backend choice;
pending independent review.** Choose a modern virtio-MMIO network device, ID 1,
one split RX/TX queue pair (indices 0/1), maximum 256 entries per queue. Offer
`VERSION_1`, `MAC`, `MTU` and `STATUS`; MAC is stable per configured VM, MTU is
1500. No checksum/segmentation offloads, merged receive buffers, control queue,
MQ/RSS, packed ring, indirect descriptors, event index or optional ring reset.
Declining optional MAC/MTU/STATUS does not invent another transport: retain
1500-byte baseline service; permit guest-selected source MAC rather than relying
on a fixed MAC as an authorization boundary. Status reports backend readiness
when negotiated; unrecoverable backend failure contains the VM even when the
guest declined STATUS. Full-device and QueueReady disable remain supported.
This is a device profile, not a closed production network authority claim.

### Guest interface evidence and completion

[Virtio 1.3 §§5.1.6.1–5.1.6.4](https://docs.oasis-open.org/virtio/virtio/v1.3/virtio-v1.3.html)
uses the modern 12-byte header even without merged buffers. Without the selected
offloads, packets have ordinary checksums and no segmentation request; RX has
zero flags, GSO_NONE and `num_buffers=1`. The whole packet occupies one available
buffer chain, which may have multiple descriptor elements. TX's num_buffers is
zero. Do not confuse the absent merge feature with a ten-byte legacy header.

[Linux v6.12 virtio_net](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/net/virtio_net.c)
selects that 12-byte header for VERSION_1, obtains MAC/MTU when offered, otherwise
can choose a random MAC, and has a non-mergeable small-packet receive path.
Its `GOOD_PACKET_LEN` includes Ethernet/VLAN overhead. This supports the chosen
non-offload path without requiring a control queue; it is not a boot or workload
measurement. Keep ordinary IPv4/IPv6 packets and guest-computed checksums; no
protocol-specific optimization is required in the device adapter.

Select at most **one TX and one RX operation** in flight, with one private
2048-byte packet slot each and finite metadata bounded by the queue size.
Advertised guest lengths never cause proportional allocation. Accept ordinary
Ethernet frames up to 1518 bytes (1500-byte payload plus single VLAN overhead),
and validate descriptor directions, bounded chain traversal, total lengths and
routes before touching guest RAM. Ignore unknown header flag bits as required;
do not use inactive checksum offsets or trust hdr_len. Unsupported segmentation
requests/malformed chains follow the established malformed-device policy.

TX snapshots header and frame under an admitted RAM read window, releases that
window, strips the virtio header, and submits immutable packet bytes to the
backend. TX used completion has length zero and releases the guest buffer; it
is not a remote delivery acknowledgment. Each operation ID is sent at most once;
an ambiguous backend send failure is never replayed as though nothing happened.
The network can lose packets. For transient no-capacity, this baseline drops the
packet and completes its buffer; it does not build an unbounded retry queue.
A persistent backend failure contains the VM rather than pretending link health.

RX acquires a private complete frame before requesting a RAM commit grant. If
there is no guest buffer or private capacity, drop the frame. If the available
chain cannot contain header plus frame, contain the malformed/unsupported queue
rather than writing a truncated packet or borrowing a second available head.
A valid RX commit writes the initialized virtio header and frame, used element
with actual byte count, then used index. TX ring writes and every RX header,
payload and ring write participate in the same physical write union, including
source/PTE aliases, as INV-03-04 claims 26–29. Source/shadow retirement completes
before observer release and the typed IRQ offer; no host network callback writes
guest backing directly. Normal split-ring notification suppression applies.

The pinned [QEMU virtio-net](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/net/virtio-net.c)
uses guest IOVs in TX `qemu_sendv_packet_async` and retains an element for deferred
completion; it pushes zero-length TX completions. Its RX path copies into guest
IOVs. These are reusable queue semantics, not authority to preserve guest-backed
IOVs across the new execution boundary. Our staged interface replaces that
lifetime. Existing QEMU model reuse remains independent of code-generator choice.

### Native backend candidate and rights boundary

Select **vmnet shared/NAT as the native backend candidate**, owned by a separate
per-VM packet broker, not by the captive execution worker. The worker retains
only the existing deliberate byte-pipe grants. Framed messages carry a bounded
packet length, VM/incarnation and operation ID; they carry no file descriptors,
Mach rights, paths, interface-selection commands or RAM pointers. The broker
owns one supervisor-configured interface; its startup parameters are immutable
from the guest channel. The broker never receives guest RAM authority. This is
an actual capability split, not a claim that packet parsing or all broker process
rights are already contained by a tested production policy.

The [Apple SDK vmnet header](/private/tmp/natemu-track03-network-01/sdk-vmnet.h)
defines shared-mode NAT, requested MTU, returned maximum packet size and packet
counts for read/write. Match the inspected QEMU startup by setting
`vmnet_allocate_mac_address_key=false`, so backend-generated MAC selection does
not compete with the configured guest MAC (or a driver choosing its own MAC).
Request MTU 1500, disable TSO/checksum offload and virtio
headers, and require reported MTU 1500 and maximum packet size at most 2048 before
making the interface ready. A read slot is at least the reported maximum;
use one-packet calls, validate counts/lengths, then apply the 1518-byte selected
frame limit. QEMU's [vmnet-common implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qemu/qemu/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/net/vmnet-common.m)
provides concrete start/read/write/stop call sites and private RX buffers. Its
blocking start/stop waits and direct callbacks are not selected as device-owner
behavior. Broker callbacks run independently; they send typed results only.
[Apple vmnet documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/vmnet)
requires `com.apple.vm.networking` for a sandboxed process. No entitlement is
silently added to the previously tested execution-worker profile.

For a one-packet write, success with count one is backend acceptance; success
with count zero is a drop/no-capacity outcome in this baseline. Error or invalid
count is a backend failure with no retry of uncertain external effects. Read
success/count zero is no packet, not a fabricated frame. Reset closes the old
queue incarnation, retires already granted RAM commits, and cancels unsent and
uncommitted work. An already accepted outgoing packet cannot be recalled.
Drain broker/result/channel references outside RAM grants and device-owner
callbacks before acknowledging reset or reusing operation slots; discard late
old RX frames/completions by incarnation. A new incarnation must not inherit a
buffered old packet. Interface stop completion and local callback/message drain
are separate obligations; the SDK's stop description alone does not prove every
queued application callback has disappeared. Failed/stalled drain contains the
VM/broker, never acknowledges successful resource reuse.

**Explicit network grant and remaining applicability premise.** Select ordinary
shared/NAT network reachability, including vmnet's documented host-service
reachability, as the candidate's deliberate guest packet capability. That does
not grant host files, task ports, peer VM memory, arbitrary broker commands or
process-control authority. Services reached through the network retain their
own authentication. The charter's prohibition of unauthorized host/peer state
access does not itself prohibit every host-addressed packet. Earlier direct
worker socket-denial probes established a process-operation boundary; they did
not select an air-gapped guest network or forbid a separately granted packet
endpoint. This paragraph proposes that endpoint's actual reachability explicitly,
rather than extending worker socket rights silently.

Set the isolation key for participating per-VM interfaces as defense within its
documented interface scope, **not** as proof of host-address denial or complete
peer-network exclusion. A deployment requiring no host-service reachability
cannot use this choice unchanged; that is a stricter network policy requiring
an enforceable filter/backend contract. No such broader requirement is inferred
from the charter here. Production entitlement/provisioning and the broker's
actual minimum service/filter grants remain unestablished platform premises;
giving the worker arbitrary outbound sockets or granting root to make vmnet
work is not the selected remedy.

Compared with the alternatives, QEMU's documented
[user-mode network backend](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/system/devices/net.html)
provides networking without root through a userspace stack, but documents ICMP
limitations; placing such a stack in a separate broker is an alternative if
vmnet provisioning fails. It is not proven cheaper or fully equivalent here.
Bridging exposes a physical-network attachment and needs an explicit interface
grant, whereas host-only mode omits outside connectivity. Prefer the inspected
native shared interface for the initial ordinary-NAT contract, without claiming
measured performance or that isolation-key semantics justify that preference.

**Decision and next action.** The guest feature/buffer/ownership/reset choice and
ordinary shared-network grant are concrete. The separate vmnet broker is the
conditional native backend selection; its remaining load-bearing platform
premise is authorized non-root launch with the required production entitlement
and minimum service grants, not an invented blanket prohibition on host packets.
The smallest next source/design action identifies that broker launch/grant
recipe or chooses the userspace alternative if the native authority cannot be
obtained within the deployment contract. Root independent review comes first;
no extra packet mock or unchanged self-test is proposed. No networking runtime
acceptance or complete charter-11 closure follows from this pass.


**Claim57 closure:** root Return36 accepts the scoped guest profile, staged
packet/queue/reset interface and conditional native broker selection. Production
broker authority remains the next concrete source/design question.

## Non-root network broker signing and service boundary (claim 58)

**Method:** bounded 30-minute source/design continuation of accepted claim 57,
September 20, 2026. Inspect Apple's entitlement/signing documentation, shipped
macOS 26.6.2 application policy/service declaration and the selected QEMU
userspace backend dependency. No signing, profile mutation, account inspection,
network traffic, native framework invocation or implementation. Retained primary
artifacts and hashes: `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-vmnet-launch-01/sources.json`.

**CLM-03-03-58 — proposed conditional non-root broker recipe and specific
remaining service premise; pending independent review.** Keep claim 57's separate
per-VM packet broker and vmnet shared interface as the native candidate. Require
an independently signed broker bundle whose restricted entitlement is actually
authorized; do not equate adding a key to an ad-hoc signature with obtaining the
capability. Do not move this requirement into the execution worker or run either
process as root. The project has not established whether an appropriate profile
is available; source research does not establish its absence either.

### Actual entitlement and service evidence

Apple's [com.apple.vm.networking documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.vm.networking?language=obj)
describes non-root virtual-interface management and states that this entitlement
is restricted to virtualization developers, with requests directed to an Apple
representative. The actual Apple documentation JSON is retained, not inferred
from a third-party report. Apple's
[restricted-entitlement packaging guidance](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode/signing-a-daemon-with-a-restricted-entitlement)
explains why a standalone executable cannot embed its authorizing provisioning
profile and gives an app-like bundle structure containing the executable,
Info.plist, signature and `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile`. Its sample uses
other restricted entitlements and a daemon deployment; reuse the packaging
principle, not those unrelated entitlements, root launchd installation or the
sample's unrestricted runtime as our policy.

The retained installed `application.sb`, lines 796–797, gates this exact rule
on `com.apple.vm.networking`:

```scheme
(allow mach-lookup (global-name "com.apple.NetworkSharing"))
```

The installed `com.apple.NetworkSharing.plist` declares that Mach service and
`/usr/libexec/InternetSharing` as its program. These primary OS artifacts locate
an actual named service boundary. They do **not** reveal its private request
protocol or prove that this one allow rule is a complete custom sandbox profile;
the system application policy contains other common grants. The public vmnet
API remains the client interface—no hand-built private service protocol is
selected. A Mach service send right may convey further service-specific rights;
calling the endpoint a name alone does not prove its whole authority.

A separate Apple page lists
[`com.apple.developer.networking.vmnet`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/entitlements/com.apple.developer.networking.vmnet)
with boolean metadata, but the inspected page supplies no statement making it
an interchangeable unrestricted authorization for the above API. Its reported
default is not evidence that an arbitrary binary may bypass the documented
restricted key. The current candidate therefore does not substitute this key
or combine speculative entitlement sets.

### Reviewable launch/grant recipe

1. Package the fixed packet-broker executable in its own app-like bundle with a
   bundle identity and signature/profile authorizing `com.apple.vm.networking`.
   Use the appropriate distribution signing/notarization process; no HVF, JIT,
   debugger, arbitrary device or execution-worker authority is needed merely to
   move packet bytes. Treat signing/profile validation failure as failure to
   launch this backend, not permission to fall back to root.
2. Supervisor launches one broker for this VM as a non-root user, with deliberate
   stdin/stdout/stderr binding, fixed pipe endpoints and immutable interface
   parameters. Close other descriptors and sanitize inherited task/registered/
   exception rights using the existing launch rules. The broker's service
   acquisition differs deliberately from the worker's NULL-bootstrap launch:
   it needs a bootstrap lookup context constrained by its policy to the named
   NetworkSharing service (plus independently justified runtime startup needs).
   Do not reuse the worker's launch fixture and falsely assume vmnet works with
   no way to acquire its required service.
3. Before accepting guest packets, apply the broker's process policy: no task
   acquisition/process control, host file access beyond executable/runtime
   dependencies and deliberate diagnostics, or guest-requested service lookup.
   Permit the explicit vmnet service use needed by the chosen interface. The
   shipped conditional rule is source evidence for the named grant, not proof
   that a custom profile can be copied from one line. Entitlement authorization
   and sandbox permission are distinct prerequisites.
4. The broker starts one fixed shared interface and validates the claim-57
   parameters. It keeps its interface/service resources within its own lifetime;
   only framed bytes/status and fixed incarnation IDs cross to the worker.
   The worker cannot command a new interface, receive a service/task right or
   choose a broker executable. Existing bounded packet slots, reset drain and
   late-message rules remain unchanged. The broker's continuing bootstrap/service
   use must stay restricted; no inference that clearing bootstrap after start
   is safe for framework reconnect/stop callbacks is made.

**Precise remaining native premise:** the public sources do not enumerate the
complete vmnet client startup/callback/stop service and returned-right set under
this narrowed non-root broker policy. Thus the named grant is a concrete lower
bound, not an accepted minimum complete profile. Actual authorization of the
broker's signing profile and the framework's required continuing service rights
must be established before claiming a deployable native backend. No additional
packet correctness mock can settle either fact. This does not reopen the worker's
reviewed limited authority or imply all host-addressed traffic is prohibited:
claim 57's ordinary shared/NAT packet grant remains explicit.

### Real userspace alternative without silently shrinking networking

The selected QEMU pin's
[`net/slirp.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/net/slirp.c)
provides an actual replacement boundary: Ethernet packet input through
`slirp_input`, packet-output callback, socket polling and timers around
`slirp_new`. Its configuration supplies IPv4/IPv6, DHCP/DNS and optional
forwarding/file-service fields. The pinned
[`slirp.wrap`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/subprojects/slirp.wrap)
selects libslirp `26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d`. This is a reusable
packet-to-socket engine for the same separate broker; it does not require moving
the QEMU main loop or guest RAM into that process. The alternative would use
ordinary outbound socket authority, with IPv4/IPv6 enabled and `restricted=0`
for Internet access, and no TFTP/SMB/exec forwarding or unsolicited host-forward
listeners in the initial manifest. Host reachability is still an explicit
network grant, not a task/file capability.

Do not turn the generic
[QEMU user-network ICMP warning](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/system/devices/net.html)
into a false unconditional Darwin failure. At the actual dependency pin,
[`src/ip_icmp.c`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip_icmp.c)
contains explicit Darwin receive-layout handling, tries an ICMP datagram socket,
and on selected unsupported/denied errors tries a raw socket. If sending ICMP
fails, the echo path can fall back to UDP echo. The latter is not equivalent to
working ordinary ICMP. The source therefore supports a serious Darwin-capable
alternative but does not prove every required protocol works under an unprivileged
custom broker policy. Socket permissions, IPv6/ICMP behavior, resource ceilings
and DNS configuration access need the alternative's own precise authority and
protocol contract. It is not an automatic reduced TCP-only fallback.

**Decision consequence:** native deployment now has a concrete authorized-bundle
and named-service recipe, with exact remaining private-framework applicability
instead of an abstract request to “allow needed services.” Preserve vmnet as the
conditional native choice; if its restricted entitlement cannot be obtained,
libslirp is the concrete packet-compatible candidate to evaluate at the same
broker boundary without changing the virtio profile or weakening networking
silently. Root independent review is next. Any subsequent bounded work should
resolve the actual signing/service premise or the alternative's protocol/grant
