closure; this source pass authorizes no runtime campaign and asks for no user
approval based on an assumed missing entitlement.


**Claim58 closure:** root Return37 independently accepts the conditional bundle/
named-service recipe and alternative-source findings. Actual signing permission
and the complete narrowed framework service/callback authority remain open.

## Userspace backend protocol and authority closure (claim 59)

**Method:** machine-authored 30-minute bounded primary-source pass, September20,
following accepted claim58. Inspect the exact libslirp dependency
`26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d` selected by QEMU's retained wrap file.
Trace IPv4/IPv6, ICMP, DNS, callback lifetime and resource authority. Eight fetched
primary source files and SHA-256 records are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-slirp-01/sources.json`; claim58 retains IPv4 ICMP and
QEMU wrapper sources. No native networking, code/profile changes or implementation.
Stop at an actual protocol gap or reviewable backend choice, not a TCP-only
substitution disguised as equivalent networking.

**CLM-03-03-59 — reject unchanged pinned libslirp as a complete network fallback;
retain its concrete reusable broker boundary. Pending independent review.**
This pin supplies IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, local network control and real IPv4 ICMP
paths, but its external ICMPv6 echo path is explicitly unimplemented. Selecting
it unchanged would silently narrow the ordinary network contract being assessed
in claims57–58. This does not mean every later libslirp release or userspace
network engine has the same limitation, nor establish that vmnet authorization
is obtainable. It makes the exact decision gap concrete.

### Protocol paths, including the decisive counterexample

At this pin,
[`tcp_subr.c::tcp_fconnect`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/tcp_subr.c)
opens a stream socket for the supplied address family and connects it;
[`udp.c`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/udp.c)
and [`udp6.c`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/udp6.c)
provide the datagram/IPv6 flow path. The public configuration independently
enables IPv4/IPv6 and includes DHCP/DNS controls. These are actual protocol
components, not evidence for arbitrary Ethernet/IP protocol passthrough.

Claim58's inspected IPv4 ICMP code tries a datagram ICMP socket, handles Darwin's
receive layout, and may try raw sockets or UDP echo after failure. Retain that
conditional behavior; do not call UDP echo equivalent to genuine external ICMP.
The new decisive source is
[`ip6_icmp.c::icmp6_input`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip6_icmp.c#L415):
local virtual-host echo is answered, but an external echo request reaches an
explicit TODO/error log and is not forwarded. The file does implement neighbor
discovery/router advertisement machinery; its incoming ICMPv6 error-type cases
in this switch fall through without forwarding work. Thus “IPv6 enabled” is
not proof of external ICMPv6 coverage. A successful TCP or local gateway ping
would not distinguish this missing path.

**Concrete falsifier:** a valid guest ICMPv6 echo to an external IPv6 destination
cannot reach a host ICMPv6 socket through this inspected branch, independently
of whether the sandbox would permit such a socket. No runtime test or entitlement
experiment is needed to establish that source gap. Do not relabel it as
ordinary packet loss while claiming the path is supported.

### DNS and host authority are specific, not a generic socket entitlement

On Darwin,
[`slirp.c::get_dns_addr_libresolv`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/slirp.c#L266)
uses `res_ninit`, `res_getservers` and `res_ndestroy`. Its comment intends to
choose the first server matching the requested address family, but the actual
copy is guarded only by `found == 1`, outside the matching-family condition.
After the first match, a later nonmatching-family entry can overwrite the cached
address using the union member for the requested family while `found` remains
one. A mixed-family resolver list is therefore a concrete source-level correction
boundary; first-matching-server correctness is not established. Its inspected
scope-ID handling writes zero. This is not a simple `/etc/resolv.conf` read,
nor proof of Apple's full split-DNS/scoped-interface resolution semantics.
[`socket.c`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/socket.c)
redirects virtual DNS port53 traffic through those resolver-address functions.
The broker needs the actual resolver configuration service/read authority or an
explicit equivalent numeric server provider; guessing a public DNS server would
change deployment behavior and is not selected here.

The same ordinary-network grant as claim57 would authorize IPv4/IPv6 stream and
datagram traffic including deliberate host reachability. Genuine ICMP needs its
own allowed Darwin socket forms; a raw fallback must not silently escalate the
broker to root. No vmnet entitlement, guest RAM grant, unrestricted file access,
exec/TFTP/SMB feature, host listener or descriptor-transfer channel follows from
using this stack. Existing pipe/incarnation framing stays the worker boundary.
Libresolv's required process-service accesses and the socket-policy binding are
concrete outstanding platform facts, separate from the proven missing ICMPv6
implementation. Source inspection here grants no additional live authority.

### Capacity, scheduling and teardown adaptation

[`libslirp.h`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/libslirp.h)
provides packet, clock, timer and poll callbacks. Its packet-output contract
allows dropping when the guest is not ready. Bind these callbacks to the same
per-VM broker, finite byte channel and private packet slots from claim57; no
callback gets guest backing or writes a ring. The virtual clock/timer callbacks
must use the selected VM active-time domain, and ordinary memory coordination
must not freeze that clock. Guest queue reset does not make an already emitted
host packet reversible.

A real resource distinction remains: `socket.c::socreate` allocates socket
state, `tcp_subr.c` allocates TCP control state, and UDP/UDP6 input creates flows.
The inspected configuration has MTU/MRU but no aggregate flow/heap quota field.
Claim57's two packet staging slots bound the channel, **not the whole stack**.
Any selected adapter must also admit flow/socket, packet-reassembly and buffer
allocations against the per-VM resource envelope before allocation, with a
specified drop/error or contained-process failure on exhaustion. A poll-FD
registration callback occurs too late to be treated as proof that all preceding
allocations were bounded. Ordinary descriptor limits alone do not cap heap
state; no complete allocator audit or numerical safe ceiling is claimed here.

Run stack entry, socket polling and timer callbacks under one broker event
owner with finite dispatch batches and reserved stop/control progress. On
teardown, stop new packet/flow admission, retire callbacks/timers/poll references,
close the stack's sockets through its cleanup, and drain old framed results
before slot/incarnation reuse. Packet consumption, transmission and late-message
rules remain claim57's; do not call cleanup while a concurrent stack callback
still holds its pointers. This is a required adapter/lifetime construction,
not a claim that the QEMU host main loop or this C library directly implements
our broker process contract.

**Decision consequence and next action:** retain libslirp's packet-to-socket
component as a real alternative, but do not promote this unmodified pin to the
complete backend. The smallest source/design continuation is to identify a
concrete external ICMPv6 implementation usable without new privilege and bind
its event/resource/error semantics (or a later pinned backend already providing
it), while preserving IPv4/IPv6/DNS scope. Resource/authority adaptation remains
part of that selection. The native vmnet route still has its separate restricted
signing/service premise; neither uncertainty justifies weakening networking or
pretending one is solved by the other. Root independent review comes next.
No repetitive packet mock or runtime campaign is authorized by this record.


**Claim59 closure:** root Return38 accepts the scoped protocol/resource findings
after correction of the Darwin mixed-family DNS loop interpretation. Unchanged
libslirp is not the complete selected backend; the adapter family remains open.

## COM1 serial interface and lifecycle composition

**Method:** machine-authored30-minute primary-source/design pass, September20;
source-only, no implementation, guest execution or console experiment. Read the
selected device-owner/port-I/O/IRQ contracts (claims27–30,49), accepted ACPI
shutdown22 and Linux console/16550 sources. Eight exact inputs and SHA256 values
are retained in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-serial-01/sources.json`. QEMU is the
same device pin `c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`; Linux is v6.12.
No change to generator choice, network backend gaps or network action ownership.

### CLM-03-03-60 — Select one host-owned COM1 UART with bounded byte-stream backend

**Source/interface decision independently accepted by memory, findings Return39.** Select one QEMU
16550A UART at COM1, with the register model on the single host device owner and
all guest port accesses arriving through the existing exact-once typed service.
This closes the serial subchoice by the construction below; it does not establish
stock boot, every firmware table or actual console output.

**Register, discovery and IRQ contract.**
[serial-isa.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/char/serial-isa.c)
selects index0 at ports `0x3f8–0x3ff`, IRQ4 and an eight-byte region. Its AML
provider supplies `PNP0501`, UID1, present/enabled status and the port/IRQ resource.
[serial.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/char/serial.c)
and [serial.h](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/hw/char/serial.h)
supply the16-byte FIFOs, divisor-latch multiplexing, scratch register, line/modem
status, loopback, interrupt priority and receive timeout. Keep baudbase115200
(the1.8432MHz input divided by16); do not replace those transitions with a
write-only console port or invent numerical UART timing. Serial timing callbacks
use claim29's VM-active clock, including its explicit-pause/sleep policy; an
ordinary memory coordination park does not freeze that clock.

Select dedicated legacy IRQ4, **edge/high** in the machine's COM1 resource and
ISA routing, with no IRQ4 override or second device sharing it. This is a small
explicit AML adaptation: the inspected QEMU serial AML emits level/low/shared,
so that line is not copied unchanged while claiming edge/high agreement. The
UART's logical asserted/deasserted output feeds the existing PIC/IOAPIC ISA fanout;
controller masks and guest routing determine the admitted destination. It is not
a second independent shim interrupt injection. The model's `serial_update_irq`
raises while an enabled condition is pending and lowers when cleared. Linux's
[8250_core.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/tty/serial/8250/8250_core.c)
explicitly explains that its ISA handler drains the line before return because
ISA interrupts are edge-triggered. Thus a held pending UART condition must not
be converted into repeated rising edges by the bridge. Ordered line transitions
and controller state stay on the one owner; reserve/coalesce owner wakeups without
losing the actual low→high sequence. IACK and EOI retain claims27–28 ownership;
reading UART IIR is not PIC acknowledgement, and PIC/LAPIC EOI does not clear UART
status. This closes this source's routing interface without claiming every IOAPIC
mode or ExtINT case complete.

Linux's [x86 serial defaults](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/include/asm/serial.h)
already name0x3f8/IRQ4 as ttyS0 with matching base baud, and its
[PNP driver](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/tty/serial/8250/8250_pnp.c)
recognizes PNP0501, reads resources and registers an8250 port. Describe this same
single UART in the selected DSDT, not a second resource. The direct-boot command
line selects `earlycon=uart8250,io,0x3f8,115200n8 console=ttyS0,115200n8`.
[8250_early.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/tty/serial/8250/8250_early.c)
uses inb/outb for this transport, configures8n1/divisor with supplied baud, and
polls transmitter-empty after writing each console character. The normal
[8250_port.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/tty/serial/8250/8250_port.c)
provides interrupt-driven RX/TX and console setup. These source routes require
the target image's8250/console configuration; they are not evidence that every
inherited initrd/kernel combination has been booted. No BIOS/real-mode UART
initialization or SPCR discovery is required for the explicit earlycon path.

**Effects and backend contract.** Scalar port permission/guest-fault checks
precede the device request. The host transaction carries VM/device incarnation
and access serial; the response owns the value and side-effect completion.
RBR reads pop bytes, IIR can clear THRE pending, LSR clears break/overrun, and MSR
clears delta bits in the model. Retrying transport must return the recorded
result, not repeat any of these reads. Writes and loopback similarly execute
once. The model declares one-byte implementation accesses; wider x86 port
transactions retain the selected bus's byte-lane decomposition and a completion
record for the whole request, rather than passing size2/4 into callbacks that
assert size1. String I/O uses the semantic owner's per-element effect record;
completed elements are not replayed after an event. No UART operation directly
writes guest RAM. An INS destination write, if present, remains the memory
owner's separately admitted effect.

Use the already authorized per-VM supervisor byte channel: no terminal device
opening, socket destination, FD transfer or privileged proxy in the worker.
The backend holds one64KiB TX byte ring and one4KiB RX byte ring, with finite
channel buffers and one readiness registration per direction. These capacities
are selected resource limits, not measured requirements. Worker and supervisor
must preserve byte order; external terminal rendering/log storage is beyond UART
transmitter completion and is not a durability acknowledgement.

The UART-facing TX operation accepts one byte into available private TX storage
and returns1. When full, it returns0 and installs one readiness continuation;
that continuation fires only after space has been reserved for its pending byte,
not on repeated spurious writable notifications. This binds the actual source:
`serial_xmit` retains the shift-register byte on zero/EAGAIN only when watch
registration succeeds, has a maximum of four retries, and otherwise advances
without delivering it. The selected backend therefore must not exhaust that
retry path merely by issuing callbacks while still full. A readiness publication
failure is a contained backend failure, not a fabricated successful watch or
silent claim of lossless output. The device owner never blocks on pipe write or
terminal consumption. Native I/O readiness work may enqueue a tagged owner event;
it cannot enter the UART concurrently.

Positive OS writes retire only their reported prefix; zero/EAGAIN retains the
suffix until readiness. A channel failure/disconnection changes to an explicit
**detached console** disposition: discard remaining queued output with a bounded
lost-byte counter/host lifecycle diagnostic, and accept/discard subsequent TX
bytes so a disconnected console cannot deadlock guest boot or shutdown. This is
normal serial-sink loss, not guest disk failure or successful host logging.
No automatic replay on reconnection is selected. While connected but slow,
bounded backpressure can legitimately stall the guest's polled console; other
host service, coordination, cancellation and supervisor detach must remain
responsive. Unlimited lossless logging and independence of guest code from its
own blocking console writes are not promised.

RX reads stop when the4KiB ring has no space. The owner queries `serial_can_receive`
and supplies no more than the returned positive count, then retains unconsumed
bytes. A guest RBR read invokes the model's input-accept callback and permits
another bounded transfer. In FIFO mode, the model can advertise zero at its
trigger threshold even before the FIFO is full; do not override that result with
physical FIFO space. The model's four-character timeout and guest interrupt/read
path permit draining. EOF means no further input; it does not synthesize NUL,
break or a guest shutdown request. This is a byte-console backend, not a physical
modem: return `-ENOTSUP` (negative) for modem/serial hardware ioctls, retaining the model's
reset DCD/DSR/CTS state and its guest loopback behavior. No untrusted in-band byte
sequence becomes a host command. Explicit BREAK injection is not part of the
selected byte protocol; guest TX break control remains a modeled register effect.

**Reset, pause and shutdown.** Timer, readiness and receive callbacks carry the
current device incarnation. Cold machine reset closes UART requests, drains any
already executing owner transaction, detaches readiness callbacks, cancels both
UART timers and retires old events before destroying/recreating the UART and
emptying private rings. Recreating avoids assuming the model's `serial_reset`
initializes every field: it resets many registers/FIFOs and lowers IRQ, but does
not explicitly zero `fcr`. A guest FCR write instead uses its actual model FIFO
clear behavior and is not this lifecycle reset. Retain old callback storage until
cancellation acknowledgement; a stale queued event cannot write the new UART or
raise IRQ4. No wait for a guest interrupt handler is part of reset drain.

During explicit VM pause, stop injecting RX/timer effects into the UART and keep
only bounded pending bytes; output already accepted may drain to its host sink.
On resume, claim29's clock and the owner serialize due timers/input before later
transactions. ACPI S5 remains claims22/30's shutdown trigger. First close new
guest device transactions, finish any admitted UART transaction, then allow at
most100ms host-monotonic best-effort TX draining before discard/close. This serial
sink deadline cannot downgrade a disk full-sync barrier or manufacture clean
storage shutdown; those independent lifecycle outcomes remain truthful. No UART
magic byte/port is a substitute for guest ACPI shutdown.

**Disposition:** select this COM1 register/discovery/IRQ/stream/lifecycle contract
as the serial subchoice, independently accepted in memory Return39. The adapted byte backend,
edge/high resource declaration and reset callback drain are actual construction
obligations; source reuse alone does not satisfy them. They do not require a new
architecture mock to choose the interface. Target driver configuration, complete
firmware publication and the existing interrupt/memory platform premises remain
in their owning questions. Network claims57–59 and their next bounded action
remain unchanged. Serial architecture review is complete in its scoped interface; implementation
conformance belongs to planning, not a new UART research or runtime pass.

## Clock programming, expiry authority and delayed-service boundary

**Method:** machine-authored bounded30-minute source/design pass, September20.
Compose accepted claim29 and INV-03-01 claim13 rather than redo clock discovery.
Reinspect Intel Vol3A092 §13.5.4 and retained selected-pin LAPIC/PIT/RTC/ACPI
sources, plus Linux v6.12 LAPIC programming. Exact seven reading-file paths and
hashes are retained in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-clock-composition-01/sources.json`;
Intel text remains `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-split-cmpxchg-01/intel-vol3a.txt`.
No new downloads, native execution, code changes or runtime budget. Stop at the
first unresolved consequence of replacing the old timer runtime.

### CLM-03-03-61 — Select serialized timer-arm authority; delayed legacy expiry reconciliation remains a concrete closure gap

**Source/interface finding accepted in root Return40.** Keep the
accepted active-time epoch, fixed1GHz virtual TSC and24MHz crystal/LAPIC input.
Do not expose TSC-deadline or TSC_ADJUST in this candidate. The following selects
a common programming/cancellation interface, but does **not** close charter
question9: the final subsection identifies a concrete unresolved legacy-timer
adapter rule, rather than classifying it as mere implementation conformance.

**Architectural time and writes.** All RUNNING reads use claim29's same exact
host-tick/timebase rational and pause accumulator. Only the final visible counter
conversion is rounded down; native wake deadlines round up and are rechecked.
RDTSC, RDTSCP and IA32_TSC reads share per-vCPU `base(V)+offset modulo2^64`.
An admitted IA32_TSC write computes `offset = requested_value - base(Vcommit)`
at that instruction's commit, so a later read advances from the written value.
It does not modify the VM epoch, PIT/PM/RTC, LAPIC phase, another CPU's offset or
HVF counter offset. Required privilege, serialization, TSC_AUX and unsupported-
MSR behavior remain in the selected CPU semantic contract; neither leaf15 nor
this rule silently advertises extra MSRs. A cold new VM establishes a fresh
epoch and initializes its CPU/device state before admission. Local architectural
CPU reset/INIT is not an implicit whole-VM time reset.

**Timer ownership and linearization.** Each source has one owner and one current
arm record `(VM lifetime, device/vCPU lifetime, arm generation, virtual deadline)`.
Host device timers remain on the single device owner; LAPIC programming/current
count and pending timer events remain resident per-vCPU state. All guest writes
are typed instruction effects. Before applying a write or returning a register
read, its owner accounts for an expiry already due at the admitted virtual time;
then the write replaces the arm/state and advances its generation. An expiry
exactly at that time is ordered before the write by this selected virtual-device
rule. This avoids host scheduling order deciding whether a due event disappears.
It does not mean every overdue hardware tick creates a separate guest interrupt.

A native/host callback is only a wake prompt carrying the arm identity. On wake,
the owner compares identity, samples current V, and changes architectural timer
state only if due. Early wakes rearm; stale canceled/replaced wakes have no guest
effect. A cancellation cannot erase a vector already admitted to LAPIC IRR or a
latched device status: it prevents future expiry effects, while existing pending
state follows normal acknowledgement/clear rules. Conversely a queued wake for
an old arm is not itself a pending x86 interrupt. Arm/cancel/native-wake changes
publish through the existing sequence/recheck sleep protocol, so a newly earlier
deadline cannot be lost between last-check and HLT/host sleep admission. Use one
replaceable wake registration and a coalesced wake flag per timer source, not an
unbounded queue of host callbacks. Expiration processing may publish an IRQ offer
but never waits for guest IF, EOI, RAM coordination or a guest handler to run.

During explicit whole-VM pause, the exclusive pause point captures V, closes
state-changing timer admission and retires native wakes by epoch generation.
Resume publishes the updated pause accumulator and fresh wake identities before
releasing CPUs/device service. Due architectural status already committed before
pause stays pending; backend arrivals remain bounded until resumed. HLT, native
preemption and ordinary coordinator parks keep V advancing. Host sleep does not
advance V by accepted policy, so it creates no synthetic slept-through ticks.
Timer teardown retires callback references before freeing owner records; lifetime
mismatch is not permission to dereference an already freed callback target.

**LAPIC source boundary.** Intel's
[Vol3A092 §13.5.4](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
states that writing initial count starts/restarts the countdown, zero stops it,
current count is read-only, periodic mode reloads at zero, and merely changing
one-shot/periodic mode does not start the timer. Its divider encoding selects
2/4/8/16/32/64/128/1. Bind that state to the accepted24MHz input; LVT masking
inhibits reception, not the source clock. HLT/preemption cannot change the count
rate. Keep the phase/count state separate from native wake registration: masking
may remove a wake optimization while reads still report an advancing countdown.
Initial-count replacement invalidates the old arm; changing LVT vector/mask cannot
rewrite a previously admitted IRR vector. Reconcile elapsed count before applying
divider/mode programming instead of retroactively applying a new divider to the
entire interval since the original load. The exact sub-tick divider transition
policy must be declared by the resident APIC model; this pass does not infer one
from the source's integer shift.

The pinned QEMU
[apic_common.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/apic_common.c)
uses elapsed virtual **nanoseconds** shifted by the divider and a periodic
`initial_count+1` arithmetic, while
[apic.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/apic.c)
changes divider fields without calling timer-update in that branch. These are
observed implementation facts, not a proof that the whole function supplies our
24MHz contract or every dynamic reprogramming case. Linux's
[v6.12 APIC source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/apic/apic.c)
programs LVTT/divider/initial-count and reads count for calibration; accepted
claim13 already requires those visible rates to agree with CPUID15. Thus selected
resident count arithmetic needs the explicit rate/phase adaptation; changing a
frequency constant in discovery is insufficient. Reserved deadline-mode exposure
is not acquired as a shortcut.

**Legacy sources and exact stopping condition.** Select the existing PIT,
24-bit PM timer and RTC register models with their clocks rebound to V. The
[PM implementation](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/acpi/core.c)
uses3,579,545Hz, returns low24 bits, ignores timer writes, latches timer status on
its bit23 transition boundary, and schedules a new overflow after status clear.
The FADT must describe this width. PM status/enable/SCI service remains the same
owner transaction; no TSC offset write can move its overflow. RTC calendar starts
from one host UTC sample and thereafter follows V and guest calendar writes.
Select the model's DISCARD lost-tick policy, not its KVM-dependent SLEW/reinjection
path. This is not yet an assertion that unchanged callbacks implement bounded
late service correctly.

There is a concrete mismatch with claim29's prohibition on an unbounded synthetic
catch-up loop. The pinned
[PIT callback](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/timer/i8254.c)
passes the **previous scheduled transition** to `pit_irq_timer_update`; the
[RTC periodic callback](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/rtc/mc146818rtc.c)
similarly calls periodic-update with `next_periodic_time`, not present V.
Repeated callbacks after a long runnable-but-descheduled interval can therefore
remain overdue through many periods. DISCARD does not by itself change those
arguments. Simply rebinding the clock or naming a finite event queue does not
resolve the catch-up work.

Nor is replacing every callback argument with present V a complete argument:
[PIT output/transition formulas](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/timer/i8254_common.c)
can yield the same output level before and after an interval containing a rising
edge. Sampling only the final level can lose the PIC's pending edge. Replaying
all edges invents potentially unbounded work and can interact with IACK/EOI
interleaving. RTC periodic/status/alarm state similarly distinguishes elapsed
calendar state from a latched interrupt indication, and register-C reads clear
that indication. This is a missing **delayed-expiry transition contract**, not a
need to observe whether a loop is slow.

**Smallest next resolution:** one bounded source/design pass should derive an
O(1)-per-source advance-to-V transition that preserves whether any relevant edge
occurred, final output/count, pending PIC/IOAPIC state, RTC PF/UF/AF latches and
future deadline, serialized against intervening register reads/writes/IACK/EOI.
It should also finish the resident LAPIC divider/mode phase rule against Intel's
permitted behavior rather than inherit QEMU nanosecond arithmetic. If finite
pending-controller state suffices, select that construction and close this seam;
if not, name the precise permitted loss/backpressure choice. No new runtime,
probe, timer framework or unconditional row9 acceptance follows from this pass.
Network next-action ownership and accepted serial60 are unchanged.

## Advance-to-time without a tick backlog

**Method:** machine-authored30-minute source/design continuation after accepted61.
Read the retained PIC/IOAPIC, PIT/RTC sources and Intel092 §§13.5.4/13.8.4.
Add Intel's8254 datasheet231164-005, September1993, pp11–17, from the
[Stanford primary-document mirror](https://www.scs.stanford.edu/25sp-cs212/pintos/specs/8254.pdf).
Its PDF/text and provenance/hash are retained under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-clock-fold-01`; other exact inputs remain in61's
manifest. Intel authorship is distinct from mirror hosting. No runtime, model
prototype or product implementation. The selected contract need not reproduce
QEMU's incidental nanosecond rounding artifacts.

### CLM-03-03-62 — Select exact source phases and finite pending-state folding across serialized observation cuts

**Architectural contract accepted in root Return41 after TMR correction.** Replace
old-deadline callback replay with the following advance-to-V contract. This
resolves the specific delayed-service gap in61 for the selected fixed timer
routes, subject to the explicit observation/receiver contract below. It does not
silently accept every interrupt routing mode in question8 or complete question9
by naming a clock function.

**The observation cut is essential.** An interval eligible for folding contains
no intervening timer programming/read, clear-on-read status, PIC IACK/poll/EOI/
mask operation, IOAPIC routing/EOI operation, local IRR→ISR delivery/EOI,
IRR/ISR/TMR query, or an incoming non-timer interrupt offer. Before committing
a non-timer offer, advance timer sources through its admitted V, commit their
receiver effects, then accept that offer as the next ordered operation. The same
rule applies to software/IPI offers, not only external device pins. Thus no
unrecorded offer may overwrite TMR inside an interval claimed to be foldable. Before such an operation commits, advance
its relevant timer sources to the sampled V and commit resulting controller
state/receiver offers, then execute that operation. These cuts are serialized by
the existing owner/typed-controller protocol. The operation's time is sampled
at actual admission, not an old request timestamp that could move time backward.
TSC-only reads and ordinary instructions do not require this service.

A local LAPIC consumer cannot acknowledge/deliver a vector while a folded interval
is being applied to that receiver. It participates through the existing reserved
controller transaction; it may continue unrelated execution, but pending-state
consumption waits for that finite commit. Offers are admitted into bounded
receiver state before releasing the cut, not left in a per-tick transport queue.
No host owner waits for a guest handler or memory grant; no participating vCPU
holds an authority needed by the owner while waiting. An implementation may
avoid a host round trip when a published frontier proves the relevant sources
already current. This is a real cross-boundary synchronization cost, not free
reuse of QEMU's in-process interrupt calls. If that frontier/transaction is
absent, independently running receivers invalidate the fold argument.

**Why a finite fold preserves pending state.**
The selected-pin [PIC source](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/i8259.c)
sets edge-triggered IRR on a low→high transition, leaves it set on a falling
edge, and records final input in `last_irr`; IACK clears an edge request. With
no IACK during the interval, the exact result is old IRR OR any rising edge,
plus the final input level. Repeating an already-pending edge adds no state.
For a level PIC input the final IRR follows final level; a temporary high is not
an acknowledged interrupt when no IACK occurred. Cascade propagation stays in
the same cut; claim28's no-speculative-IACK rule remains mandatory.

The [IOAPIC source](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/ioapic.c)
discards masked edge requests, admits unmasked fixed edge offers, and suppresses
repeated level delivery while remote-IRR is set. A level pulse may already have
admitted an offer even when its final level is low: retain that receiver effect
and remote-IRR rather than merely writing the final pin. With no intervening EOI,
a level route with clear remote-IRR admits its first eligible assertion; later
assertions do not create new offers until that gate is cleared. Existing set
remote-IRR is preserved and cannot be bypassed by folding.

**Receiver reduction includes TMR, not only IRR.** Intel
[Vol3A092 §13.8.4](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
describes same-vector IRR collapse and then explicitly says each accepted edge
clears TMR while each accepted level sets it; the bit controls EOI broadcast.
The pinned [apic_set_irq/apic_eoi](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/apic.c)
perform those updates even if IRR was already set. The original draft's
per-source one-offer reduction was therefore insufficient. For example edge at
t1, level at t2, edge at t3 leaves TMR clear; retaining only the first edge then
the level incorrectly leaves it set. Shared guest-programmed vectors remain
supported, including an already in-service instance of the same vector.

For each unchanged fixed source→receiver route, summarize the **first and last
eligible accepted edge offer times**, or the **first eligible level offer time**
if its remote-IRR gate permits one. Equal first/last is one record. Retain actual
virtual occurrence times and a deterministic stable source/route order for
simultaneous events; this is the selected tie rule, not host callback arrival
order. From periodic phase, the first/last occurrence can be calculated with
integer quotient/remainder; no per-tick list is retained. Merge these bounded
records across all relevant timer sources in that order, and apply their normal
receiver transitions before releasing the observation cut. A routing change,
receiver consumption or non-timer offer ends the interval, so it cannot change
which destination, trigger or gate those summaries denote halfway through it.

For each receiver/vector, the resulting IRR is its original bit OR any admitted
offer; ISR is unchanged because delivery/EOI is excluded from the interval. TMR
is unchanged if there were no offers, otherwise it is the trigger of the **last
accepted offer across all sources**. Keeping each edge route's last record and
each level route's only eligible record guarantees that global last is retained.
Keeping the first records also preserves first-pending/remote-IRR admission and
bounded wake publication. Intermediate identical edge offers cannot change any
other state in this selected fixed receiver path. Level remote-IRR stays set
after its offer even if the source deasserts; pin IRR/final input are reconciled
separately. PIC edge IRR/cascade and IOAPIC remote-IRR are not replaced by the
local IRR formula. No architectural delivery or TMR query observes the temporary
compressed replay because the receiver remains inside its observation cut.

For each timer output, the source summary additionally retains initial/final
level and whether rise/fall occurred. Apply a representative ordered pin
transition subsequence containing the consequential first assertion and final
deassertion; associate receiver offers with their retained occurrence times,
not the arbitrary order in which source summaries are computed. For example,
initial=final=low with a real intervening rise cannot disappear as an unchanged
level; initial=final=high with an intervening fall/rise retains that pair. Do not
feed this representative pin sequence through an unconstrained eager callback
that immediately commits receiver offers in per-source iteration order: collect
its bounded offer records and commit the merged order at the cut instead.
Repeated cycles after saturation need no replay, but their **last edge time**
can still determine TMR and is never discarded merely because IRR is already set.

There are at most two edge records or one level record per fixed route, plus
finite pin/controller state; the bound depends on configured sources and
receivers, not elapsed time. This proves a reduction for all shared-vector
combinations of the selected fixed routes. ExtINT acknowledgement remains an
IACK cut under claim28, not a speculative fixed-vector offer; other delivery
modes require their own question8 receiver state. That external dependency does
not defer or excuse this now-explicit IRR/TMR/remote-IRR ordering obligation.

**PIT source waveform, explicitly adapted.** Select the documented8254 clocked
state machine, driven by exact rational1,193,182Hz from V. Preserve control/read-
back/latch/byte sequencing and channel resources, but adapt numerical waveform,
gate/load and delayed-reload helpers where the inspected QEMU code differs.
Do not require equivalence to its floor-nanosecond conversion or `current_time+1`
repair. This costs more than merely rebinding `qemu_clock_get_ns`; no unchanged
PIT-helper correctness is asserted.

The Intel datasheet specifies clock-sampled loads/gates, periodic modes2/3,
mode2's one-clock low pulse, mode3's even/odd duty cycles, and mode-specific
count replacement. It also specifies nonperiodic modes, post-terminal counter
wrap, binary/BCD counts, and minimum legal counts. These are the selected
semantics, including pending reload timing; not only the commonly used Linux
mode3. Source state is finite: active count/phase, pending programmed count,
clock-sampled trigger/load, gate, output and independent read/status latches.
A register write establishes a cut, so the interval to the next cut contains at
most that finite set of pending changes, not an unbounded history of writes.

Advance across the next pending load/reload or terminal boundary directly. Once
the state is periodic with unchanged gate/count, calculate complete periods and
remainder by integer division, retaining whether an edge occurred and the final
half-cycle/count. Mode3 uses its documented unequal halves for odd counts;
mode2 retains the low pulse even if both sampled endpoints are high. Nonperiodic
modes have finitely many output transitions; their counter wrap is computed
modulo the binary/BCD range without inventing another output interrupt. Paused
gate phases retain the correct count; triggers and control-word output changes
are immediate/sampled according to the same selected state machine, not
retrospective elapsed-time subtraction. Undefined/illegal input combinations do
not justify terminating an otherwise valid guest or omitting a documented mode;
the implementation may choose a deterministic unspecified outcome for them.
The next native wake is the ceiling of the exact next transition time; guest
count/output reads use exact source phase, never rounded host wake time.

This bounded construction needs only a finite prefix of pending state changes
and one periodic quotient/remainder calculation per source. There is no iteration
per elapsed PIT cycle. It eliminates the61 quantization obstacle by selecting a
primary-documented device contract instead of reverse-engineering a bug-compatible
callback schedule. Numeric formulas and register helpers must conform later;
that is not a new architectural uncertainty about which waveform is intended.

**RTC and PM status are latches, not tick queues.** For RTC DISCARD, retain the
next periodic occurrence, update occurrence and alarm match in source time.
Within an unchanged configuration interval, if any occurrence is due, OR its
PF/UF/AF status once and apply enable/IRQF transitions. Calculate the next future
period directly; update the calendar to V once. An alarm already scheduled before
the interval cannot be forgotten merely because today's calendar no longer
matches it. After its occurrence is latched, the existing bounded next-alarm
calculation supplies the next match; no loop over elapsed days is needed.
SET/divider/enable/calendar/alarm writes and register-C reads split intervals.
Preserve existing source rules for whether a source is active under SET/divider
state; no discarded callback may skip a required flag. The retained
[RTC implementation](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/rtc/mc146818rtc.c)
ORs flags, lowers/clears on C reads and already computes future alarm times;
replace periodic scheduling from old expiry with this phase-based advance.
No SLEW queue or KVM reinjection counter is selected.

PM timer remains the accepted24-bit counter and bit23-transition status latch.
Advance tests whether its next transition was crossed, ORs status and recomputes
SCI from status/enables; W1C is a cut and establishes the next future transition.
Multiple wraps with no intervening clear remain one pending status. Counter
reads still report the correct wrapped value. Serial receive timeouts are
one-shot arm records; repeated wake prompts do not multiply that pending UART
condition. Device owners retain responsibility for non-clock backend completion.

**LAPIC phase choice.** Use the accepted24MHz crystal and divisor set. Maintain
current integer count plus fractional progress to its next decrement. Initial-
count write first settles old due effects, then loads N and begins a new divider
phase; N=0 stops. One-shot expiry leaves zero. Periodic expiry reloads N at zero;
large elapsed intervals use quotient/remainder and one pending IRR offer when
unmasked. No `N+1` nanosecond approximation is inherited. Masking blocks new
reception while phase/count continue; unmasking does not replay masked expiries.
An already admitted vector is not canceled by masking or count replacement.

Select this explicit deterministic dynamic-programming rule: a divider write
settles progress under the old divisor, preserves the resulting integer count,
and starts a full tick under the new divisor at the write boundary. A mode write
preserves count and divider phase, changes what happens at its next zero, and
cannot start a stopped/expired timer. It does not load N merely by changing LVT.
A running periodic→one-shot transition completes its current countdown once;
a running one-shot→periodic transition reloads at its next zero. Vector/mask
changes affect subsequent expiries only. Intel §13.5.4 specifies countdown,
reload, zero stop and mode-write-not-start, but does not specify the retained
fraction of a divider tick during a divider write. Choosing to restart that
fraction is a **virtual-model inference for an unspecified sub-tick phase**, not
a newly discovered physical-Haswell guarantee. If another applicable architectural
rule constrains that phase, it reopens this narrow choice. It does not change
rate, visible integer count, TSC, peer clocks or already pending interrupts.

**Disposition and remaining ownership.** After correcting root Return41's
IRR-only counterexample, this supplies a bounded phase/occurrence/first-last-offer
summary and an IRR/TMR/remote-IRR receiver-state argument for delayed timer service; finite pending
bits replace an unbounded callback backlog without losing unconsumed occurrences.
The admission cuts and source adaptations are mandatory parts of the selected
construction, not implementation details that can be omitted for performance.
The architecture does not promise real-time interrupt latency or queue every
missed periodic interrupt. It does promise accurate source state and the pending
state produced between actual architectural observations.

Question9’s scoped contract is independently accepted in root Return41 against
29/61/62 and INV03-01 claim13; the
unresolved question8 all-enabled-routing contract remains an explicit dependency,
not proved by this fixed-route argument. The accepted review includes the PIT semantic selection, observation-cut costs
and LAPIC phase inference. No runtime/conformance/performance acceptance or
network work change is made. Next bind the question8 routing contract to these
receiver cuts; no callback replay experiment is implied.

## Routing, core events and wait composition

**Method:** machine-authored bounded30-minute source/design pass, September20.
Read claims23/27–28 and accepted clock62/code-lifetime01-14, the submitted CPU
profile01-16, and retained Intel092 §§7.7–7.9/13.6/13.8/13.11/13.12 with pinned
PIC/IOAPIC/LAPIC sources. Add Intel82093AA datasheet290566-001, May1996, pp12–13,
from the [MIT primary-document mirror](https://pdos.csail.mit.edu/6.828/2018/readings/ia32/ioapic.pdf).
PDF/text/provenance hash are retained under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-event-composition-01`. The older IOAPIC document
supplies the specific delivery-class rule, not modern x2APIC destination width.
No runtime, code change, arbitrary new routing restriction or source campaign.

### CLM-03-03-63 — Select coherent destination and typed event admission; core-event retention remains a bounded closure gap

**Source/interface choice accepted by independent root Return42.** Keep one
host controller/device owner and resident per-vCPU LAPIC/core-event owners.
Register interfaces, vector selection and x86 delivery are separate effects;
none is replaced by a native wakeup. This pass settles a concrete lowest-priority
and IOAPIC ExtINT adaptation, composes event boundaries with the accepted lifetime
and timer contracts, and identifies the remaining core-event state contract.
Question8 is not yet marked complete.

**Coherent destination directory.** The directory records each live vCPU's
physical APIC ID, APIC mode/enable state, xAPIC LDR/DFR or derived x2APIC logical
ID, and architectural TPR. APIC-ID/mode/LDR/DFR/TPR mutations—including CR8's
TPR alias—publish through the same ordered routing transaction before subsequent
ICR/IOAPIC offers. The local instruction does not complete with a host directory
still advertising its old state. ISR/PPR remain resident delivery state; a host
routing copy never becomes a second authoritative ISR.

Use Intel
[Vol3A092 §13.6.2 and §13.12.10](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
physical matching, xAPIC flat/cluster masks, and x2APIC cluster/logical-bit
matching. In x2APIC, LDR is derived from ID (`cluster=ID>>4`, logical bit
`1<<(ID&15)`), not writable xAPIC flat state. Self/all/all-excluding-self ICR
shorthands override the destination field as specified. The candidate's small
unique IDs fit the selected IOAPIC destination field, while x2APIC ICR still has
its own32-bit destination semantics. Do not reinterpret the older82093AA's
four-bit destination description as a new truncation of the CPU profile.

For lowest-priority delivery select a modern chipset-style **minimum task
priority** among addressed eligible destinations, with lowest physical APIC ID
as a deterministic tie rule. Intel §13.6.2.4 describes chipset use of task
priority and explicitly allows equal TPR information to keep delivering to one
processor; it distinguishes the older P6 APR/focus scheme. This selection does
not claim physical Haswell arbitration timing/fairness. It is not the pinned
[apic_bus_deliver](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/hw/intc/apic.c)
LOWPRI branch, which chooses the first bitmap destination and carries an
arbitration TODO without consulting task priority. A lower-ID high-TPR CPU must
not win over an addressed higher-ID low-TPR CPU merely because it comes first.

TPR and other route changes are observation cuts under62. Thus during a folded
timer interval the selected lowest-priority destination is stable, including
ties; it can be resolved to the same finite fixed receiver route used by62.
IRR admission does not change the routing TPR. Subsequent resident delivery still
uses actual PPR/IF/shadows; choosing the routing target is not permission to
interrupt its current handler. Lowest-priority level interrupts retain their
source remote-IRR and eventual matching EOI behavior, not a second broadcast.

**Delivery-class state is explicit.** Fixed and lowest-priority offers preserve
vector/trigger/source/route incarnation. Receiver acceptance updates IRR and TMR
in the global accepted-offer order established in62, even for shared vectors.
IRR→ISR consumes the highest eligible vector under local priority; EOI affects
ISR/TMR and only then produces the required typed controller EOI. Host masks
or route changes do not roll back an already admitted receiver vector. PIC ports,
including poll/IACK/auto-EOI behavior, stay exact-once host transactions; a pure
pending query cannot consume them.

For IOAPIC ExtINT, the Intel82093AA delivery table explicitly requires **edge
trigger mode** and describes external-controller vector acknowledgement. Select
edge-request ownership with no fixed-level remote-IRR/EOI wait. Its delivery
status may remain pending while the receiver holds the typed INTA reservation,
then returns idle when that transfer finishes. The source edge is owned exactly
once; neither an eager route parse nor a fixed-vector IRR insertion substitutes
for IACK. LINT ExtINT remains level-sensitive under the separate SDM rule. The
existing claim28 protocol supplies IF/shadow eligibility, once-only PIC IACK,
spurious result and no fixed LAPIC EOI. This resolves the valid IOAPIC ExtINT
bookkeeping seam without requiring a PIC acknowledgement before checking masks.
An ExtINT+level programming combination is outside this source's defined valid
combination; it is not grounds to reject valid edge ExtINT or reinterpret all
ExtINT paths as fixed interrupts. No synthetic #GP is assigned to an ordinary
IOAPIC MMIO write merely because its programmed combination is unsupported.

NMI, INIT and SIPI bypass fixed IRR/ISR. NMI delivers event class NMI/vector2;
a fixed interrupt whose vector happens to be2 is still an external interrupt,
not an NMI. INIT is a typed CPU-initialization transition, and SIPI is a typed
startup transition applicable to the target's wait-for-SIPI state. The accepted
mailbox/startup32 mechanism remains their realization; a host callback may not
jump into a new guest entry while old native code/effects are still live. Close
ordinary execution admission, retain/drain the relevant continuation and callback
references, and apply the CPU owner's reset/start tuple before new code entry.
Committed MMIO/backend effects are not rolled back or replayed when the old
instruction ceases to resume. This is an event/lifetime requirement, not a new
claim that the complete INIT architectural reset table has been supplied here.

The no-SMM firmware direction suppresses firmware-triggered SMI, but does not
make the architecturally legal ICR SMI encoding reserved. CPU16's disabled-feature
list alone is therefore not an SMI reception contract. Preserve that explicit
profile-boundary obligation; do not silently route SMI as NMI, ignore it based on
an assumed feature bit, or copy QEMU's `CPU_INTERRUPT_SMI` into an absent resident
SMM handler. Required accepted scope remains unchanged.

**Instruction and event boundaries.** The resident event owner observes the
same committed architectural tuple as dispatch/recovery. A native repair,
translation wait or coordination checkpoint does not consume an STI/SS shadow,
unblock NMI or advance guest RIP. Legal REP iteration interruption uses its
instruction-specific phase; a shadow associated with the following architectural
instruction is not cleared merely because one internal iteration completed.
An actual guest event abandons/retains native continuation according to01-14,
not by resuming a stale block suffix after handler entry.

Intel §§7.7.2/7.8/7.9 distinguish NMI blocking, IF and instruction shadows.
Select the permitted STI behavior that blocks maskable interrupts after a0→1 IF
transition through the following instruction, without additionally inhibiting
NMI solely because of STI. SS-load/debug suppression retains02's separately
reviewed pending-cause rules; its unresolved debug fusion case is not answered
by treating all reasons as one boolean shadow. NMI blocking starts **before**
NMI delivery, so a fault during delivery does not erase it; execution of IRET
unblocks NMI even if that IRET faults. Merely completing a native repair does
neither. Gate/stack/descriptor faults use the precise-delivery owner and keep
already committed IACK/IRR-consumption effects; a nested fault must not repeat
external acknowledgement.

The event selector uses SDM Table7-2 for events pending at instruction boundaries:
applicable reset/initialization and prior-instruction traps precede NMI, which
precedes maskable hardware delivery; next-instruction fetch/decode faults cannot
be chosen prematurely over an already eligible higher class. That table expressly
does not define faults during instruction execution or event delivery and does
not classify INT/INT3/INTO/INT1 as ordinary between-instruction offers. Those
instruction-semantic transitions remain distinct. Code metadata must expose the
needed actual boundary, not only a native PC label.

**Bounded queues and wake progress.** Existing request slots are reserved before
host device work or a vCPU's ICR/IACK/EOI transaction; record committed results
until transport acknowledgement. One vCPU cannot have unbounded completed guest
ICR writes queued behind an unadmitted receiver. For fixed classes, receiver
IRR/TMR plus finite source/route state absorb admitted offers with62's exact
coalescing; no per-tick queue or guest-handler acknowledgement is required.
Reserve completion/control capacity independently from compiler-output and RAM
jobs. The owner awaiting RAM authority or an external backend keeps servicing
controller/cancellation traffic. A paused vCPU's host service can finish an
IACK/EOI reservation without running guest code or reacquiring a second memory
epoch. Source callbacks cannot keep a raw old-vCPU/code pointer after reset.

HLT and host waits publish a sleep generation, recheck pending requests/deadlines
and x86 eligibility, then sleep only if unchanged. Producers update retained
pending state before incrementing/waking that generation. Arrival either wins
the recheck or sees the published sleeper. A native wake is only a prompt:
IF-clear pending fixed interrupts must not be delivered; NMI/INIT and native
coordination have separate reasons to wake. Compiler waits remain serviceable,
and late compile replies fail identity validation rather than revive an event-
abandoned continuation. No event protocol waits for a guest instruction that
is itself blocked on that protocol's resource.

**Precise stop.** The above does not establish a complete bounded core-event
reception machine for repeated NMI/INIT/SIPI/SMI while delivery is blocked or
already in progress. In particular, Intel's cited NMI-blocking text establishes
when delivery is inhibited, but this pass has not established the legal retained
multiplicity/overwrite rule for two NMI requests while NMI-blocked. Copying
QEMU's one `CPU_INTERRUPT_NMI` bit is not independently sufficient evidence for
our acceptance/transport contract. Backpressuring a guest NMI-send transaction
until the target executes IRET is not an acceptable substitute: target code can
be waiting for the sender, creating a new cycle. Unlimited event descriptors
are also excluded. INIT/SIPI precedence and the SMI profile boundary need the
same explicit core-event table, not fixed-vector coalescing.

The smallest next architectural resolution is a bounded primary-source pass on
that **core-event reception/retention table**, selecting permitted saturation,
state replacement and completion acknowledgement for each class and binding
INIT to its actual CPU reset tuple. Preserve SS/debug and nested-delivery work in
their existing semantic owner. This is not a new routing survey, a reason to
remove legal NMI/INIT/SIPI support, or a demand for a runtime mock. The routing,
ExtINT, observation-cut and wait arguments above are reviewable now; whole
question8 remains open until the retained-class contract is justified.


### External ICMPv6 source continuation — root

Source-only exploration, September20: at most30minutes and five small primary
source files (8MiB total) to determine whether Darwin exposes unprivileged
ICMPv6 echo and what a libslirp adapter must preserve. Compare Apple socket
contract/implementation with claim59's missing external branch. Stop at a
concrete admission/reply/error/resource contract or exact unsupported premise.
No sockets, packets, native execution or new runtime authority are exercised.
This does not promote the unchanged libslirp pin or select a complete backend.

## Core-event retention and INIT state — scoped source continuation

**Method:** machine-authored bounded30-minute source/design continuation after
accepted63, September20. Read existing Intel3A092 chapters7/11/12/13 first,
then retain Linux v6.12 `arch/x86/kernel/nmi.c` and `arch/x86/kvm/x86.c`, and
Intel3C081 (326019-081US) §§32.3/32.8. Exact URLs, hashes and sizes are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-core-events-01/sources.json`; the new Intel PDF/text
are there, while the0923A reset table remains in the retained split-CMPXCHG
source directory. Different revisions are explicit; no claim that081 is the
latest manual. No native execution, implementation, scope relaxation or new
user-facing permission gate. Only main inventory row8 is reconciled to accepted63.

### CLM-03-03-64 — Select bounded NMI ownership and ordered INIT/SIPI; no-SMM reception remains an actual compatibility decision

**Source/design finding accepted by independent root Return44.** The repeated blocked-
NMI case now has independent primary consumer/implementation evidence beyond the
old QEMU pending bit. The INIT tuple has concrete retained/reset fields rather
than a synonym for fresh VM creation. The legal SMI reception conflict remains
unresolved; consequently this is not full question8 acceptance.

**NMI saturation and acknowledgement.** Linux v6.12's
[native NMI handler commentary](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/nmi.c)
explicitly describes the hardware latch as binary while one NMI is running.
Its [KVM process_nmi](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kvm/x86.c)
limits retention to one when NMI-blocked or an injection is in flight. Its
allowance of two when unblocked is expressly a software scheduling accommodation,
not evidence of an unbounded physical queue. These are primary Linux sources,
not a claim that the Linux implementation defines all Intel architecture.
Intel3A092 §7.7.2 supplies NMI blocking/unblocking; Intel
[3C081 §32.8](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/789585/326019-sdm-vol-3c.pdf)
independently specifies one latched NMI during SMM. That SMM passage is not used
alone to prove all ordinary blocked cases.

Select one pending NMI bit plus the already bounded active delivery record per
vCPU. An admitted NMI edge sets the pending bit; repeated edges while it is set
saturate it. A transport duplicate is discarded by request identity before this
architectural edge admission. Sender acknowledgement follows durable reception/
saturation, never waits for the target to execute IRET. When the semantic owner
begins an eligible NMI delivery, it transfers the pending bit into the active
record and sets NMI blocking **before** descriptor/stack delivery. A later NMI
can occupy the one pending bit while delivery or its handler is active. The
active delivery is not a second unconsumed pending edge. IRET unblocks according
to63 even if it faults; a native repair does not. On reset/INIT, pending state
is reconciled with the selected reset transition rather than replayed into the
new incarnation. This gives a bounded event mechanism and avoids a sender↔NMI-
handler deadlock. It is a selected compatibility construction supported by these
sources, not an observed captive-NMI result.

**INIT admission is not cold-VM creation.** Serialize core-transition requests
with a per-vCPU transition record and the existing device-owner ordering.
Before accepting INIT into architectural state, stop native execution at a
recoverable boundary, retain committed effects and old native references, and
obtain the reset owner's exclusive state authority. This may wait for the native
participant to stop/service its control path, but never for guest code or IRET.
Then commit the initialization tuple and route epoch together. The send result
can complete after that durable transition; old transport/callback objects may
finish draining independently without executing their stale guest continuation.
No second INIT/SIPI is reordered across this commit merely because its callback
arrived on another host thread. An admitted later INIT begins another transition;
a descriptor queue is bounded by the pre-reserved per-sender/per-target slots.

The actual selected-mode INIT state is derived from
[Intel3A092 §12.1/Table12-1](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf),
including its notes, not from a general assertion that every MSR survives:

| State at committed INIT | Required treatment for the current CPU candidate |
| --- | --- |
| Integer/control entry | EFLAGS2, EIP0xfff0; EAX/EBX/ECX/ESI/EDI/EBP/ESP and R8–R15 zero; EDX follows the selected family/model signature. CR0 retains CD/NW, sets bit4 and clears its other architectural bits. CR2/CR3/CR4 zero; EFER zero. |
| Segments/descriptors | CS selector0xf000/base0xffff0000/limit0xffff; other segment selectors/bases zero and limits0xffff; descriptor-table bases zero/limits0xffff, LDTR/TR initialization and hidden attributes follow the table's specified reset state. Do not reuse previous protected-mode descriptor caches. FS_BASE/GS_BASE reset to zero despite the general MSR-retention statement. |
| Debug | DR0–DR3 zero, DR6's defined reset value0xffff0ff0, DR7=0x400; the selected profile's reserved-bit read rules still apply. Previous breakpoints cannot remain armed accidentally. |
| Retained semantic state | x87/MMX, MXCSR, XMM0–15, YMM-high and XCR0 remain unchanged. The table's x87 entries mentioning FINIT/FNINIT are **not** a request to execute FINIT on INIT; note5 explicitly says INIT leaves x87/MMX unchanged. SYSENTER and STAR/LSTAR state, TSC/TSC_AUX and MTRRs retain their stated INIT behavior. PAT retains its separately accepted INIT policy. |
| Memory/translation | Keep guest RAM and cache contents; invalidate the TLB/derived shadow/dispatch assumptions for the reset context. The §12.1 prose explicitly distinguishes retained cache contents from invalidated TLB/BTB; do not read the table's combined cache/TLB row as permission to retain old translations. XCR0/vector retention does not keep CR4.OSXSAVE enabled. |
| LAPIC | Clear pending/in-service/trigger/ICR/TPR/timer state and restore the specified masked LVT/spurious state; preserve APIC ID. Respect §13.12.5 mode-specific INIT: xAPIC remains xAPIC, x2APIC remains x2APIC with its full ID, and disabled remains disabled. Do not overwrite all APIC_BASE/mode state with a cold-reset default. |

This table selects the consequential field ownership. Any additional exposed
MSR/reset field remains bound to CPU16's finite register catalog; the table is
not permission to zero every unlisted bank or retain every MSR indiscriminately.
No TSC epoch, device clock, disk queue or peer-vCPU state is reset by this local
INIT. The code/memory generations invalidate old assumptions before subsequent
entry, while committed external effects retain their original outcome.

**SIPI and the real-mode boundary.** A target waiting for startup owns one
startup transition. The accepted SIPI carries its vector and target incarnation;
commit it once, leave the wait state, and ignore a later duplicate SIPI that no
longer addresses a waiting target. The standard Intel INIT-SIPI-SIPI sequence
cannot start two simultaneous native vCPU executions. A new INIT is ordered
before a subsequent startup transition and creates a new identity, so an old
SIPI/reply cannot satisfy the new wait accidentally. Pinned QEMU `apic_startup`
stores one vector before `apic_sipi` checks the wait flag; that callback ordering
is not sufficient evidence that two differently valued arrivals may overwrite
an already committed startup choice in this bridge.

The architectural INIT state above has PE/PG clear. It is **not** the prepared
protected-mode/long-mode tuple used by this project's direct boot and AP mailbox.
The standing exclusion of guest real-mode execution still requires the selected
startup-assist boundary: retain INIT state privately while waiting, then perform
the explicitly supported startup transition to the mailbox/startup32 entry.
Do not claim that setting EIP to a convenient protected-mode address is hardware
INIT, or that an arbitrary SIPI vector's real-mode program is supported. BSP INIT
or a restart outside that admitted startup ABI cannot silently be treated as
initial construction. The exact supported restart/assist scope remains with
question10; CPU and event state must distinguish it from hardware reset.

The Intel3C081 VMX activity-state discussion also describes blocked INIT and
ignored SIPIs outside wait-for-SIPI. It is not used to copy VMX exit behavior into
this non-VMX x86 guest or prove every native repeated-INIT case. The selected
bridge's serial transition order is explicit; any model-specific acceptance
limit must remain distinct from transport de-duplication.

**SMI is the decisive remaining scope conflict.** Intel3C081 §32.3.1 requires an
accepted SMI to reach an interruptible point, complete stores, save state in
SMRAM and enter the SMI handler. §32.3.2 makes RSM the SMM exit instruction;
RSM outside SMM #UD does not make the incoming SMI itself a #GP. It also describes
one additional SMI latched during SMM. This is a mode/state-save/return contract,
not a vector that can be placed in ordinary IRR. The inspected QEMU path calls
`do_smm_enter`; dropping that helper while retaining its legal ICR delivery mode
would remove behavior rather than adapt it.

The current machine deliberately has no SMM firmware/SMI-based ACPI enabling.
That establishes absence of a required firmware-generated SMI, **not** an
architectural reception rule for guest-programmed ICR/IOAPIC SMI. A no-op SMI,
remapped NMI, fabricated #GP, or contained VM termination would be a substantive
compatibility choice and is not accepted here. Unmodified generic real-mode/SMM
handler execution also cannot be silently added despite the standing real-mode
scope exclusion.

**Stop and next decision:** the NMI retention and INIT ownership/table above are
reviewable scoped choices. Whole question8 still needs an explicit supported
SMI/startup reception boundary consistent with the product's excluded real-mode
execution and the exposed CPU identity. The smallest next step is a source/design
comparison of a finite firmware-owned SMM service/return boundary versus an
explicitly justified unsupported-SMM machine contract, including how guest legal
SMI requests are reported/handled. It must preserve the stated ordinary Linux
scope and record compatibility costs; no user permission gate, silent feature
removal, general SMM implementation or runtime probe follows from this finding.
Question10 separately owns arbitrary restart-vector support. Parent independent
review comes before either next design choice is accepted.


### CLM-03-03-65 — Darwin supplies an unprivileged ICMPv6 echo mechanism; the libslirp adapter remains a separate obligation

**Author:** root; source-backed mechanism plus inferred adapter requirements,
independently accepted by translator Return43. Four Apple primary files from `xnu-12377.1.9` are
retained at `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-icmp6-source-01`, with URLs, sizes and
initial sandbox DNS failures in sources.json. Read-only public retrieval then
succeeded; no socket was created and no packet was transmitted. This source
release is not asserted byte-identical to the running macOS26.6.2 kernel.

Apple's [icmp6(4)](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/man/man4/icmp6.4)
explicitly provides `socket(AF_INET6, SOCK_DGRAM, IPPROTO_ICMPV6)` without root
privilege. The [protocol switch](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/in6_proto.c)
binds this form to `icmp6_dgram_usrreqs`, distinct from the raw socket.
[icmp6_dgram_attach/send/ctloutput](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/icmp6.c)
confirm that attachment omits raw attach's superuser check, unprivileged echo
request code0 is allowed, and unsupported message forms fail EPERM. The code
also permits particular node-information queries; that broader facility is not
needed to establish the missing external echo path. Socket admission still
passes the platform's other policy layers. This is not proof that any chosen
sandbox profile grants it, nor a reason to use a privileged raw fallback.

[raw_ip6.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/raw_ip6.c)
shows the shared send path adding the host IPv6 header and computing the
ICMPv6 checksum for that host source/destination. The guest checksum therefore
must not be treated as the outgoing host checksum. Receive paths strip IP and
extension headers before delivery; permitted packet-info/hop-limit options
provide ancillary context. The datagram API name does not imply UDP semantics
or automatic guest echo-ID demultiplexing. The inspected receive matching
checks protocol/address/filter state; an adapter must validate and match its
own outstanding echo identity, remote endpoint and quoted-error context.

**Concrete architectural consequence:** the missing branch in claim59 can be
adapted with a real unprivileged Darwin mechanism. It need not use UDP echo,
root, vmnet's restricted entitlement, or silently remove external IPv6 ping.
This does not establish that unchanged libslirp contains the adapter: its
retained `icmp6_input` still logs an unsupported external echo and frees the
packet. It also does not turn a packet-to-socket NAT component into arbitrary
Ethernet/IP passthrough.

The minimal adapter contract is an admission-bounded pending-request table,
nonblocking socket polling under the existing single broker owner, and private
packet copies only. Validate guest length/checksum/type before submission; keep
the guest identity needed to reconstruct replies and translated ICMP errors.
Retain no guest-RAM pointer. Allocate table/buffer/socket resources before
accepting work and use finite expiry and dispatch batches; overflow is explicit
network loss/error, not an unlimited queue. Guest reset drains callbacks and
invalidates internal request incarnations before reuse. Incoming network packets
remain untrusted and can be delayed or duplicated; an internal generation number
is not a claim that the wire authenticates a reply.

Host send errors, ICMP destination/time/packet-too-big errors and guest-visible
quoted headers require an explicit mapping; success from send is not proof of
remote delivery. The documented receive-error capability is useful support,
not a completed translation table. In particular the adapter must reconstruct
guest addresses/checksums and apply its MTU policy rather than forward a quoted
host packet as though it were the guest's original packet. Claim57's ring
completion and packet-loss rules remain applicable.

**Precise remaining decision:** select this adapted backend only after binding
that reply/error/MTU contract, the actual socket/resolver sandbox authority,
claim59's DNS family/scope correction and aggregate allocation bound. The
restricted vmnet alternative remains independently conditional. This pass
settles availability of a primary-source unprivileged mechanism, not complete
network question11, actual platform conformance or performance. Next action:
independent review of65, then one bounded adapter-authority/error-contract pass;
no repeated generic networking survey or native experiment follows automatically.

## Adapted userspace network contract (claim 67)

**Method recorded before work:** translator-authored 30-minute source/interface
pass, 2026-09-20, root independently reviews. Use retained claims59/65 libslirp
and Apple XNU sources plus existing broker authority contracts. Define the minimum
reply/error/MTU and DNS correction with aggregate admission bounds; distinguish
adapter construction from actual socket/resolver sandbox authority. No code,
socket, packet, native execution or broad backend survey. Stop at a reviewable
conditional contract or exact unresolved dependency. Ordinary host-service
network reachability is the deliberate grant; host files/tasks/peer RAM are not.

### CLM-03-03-67 — Select typed reply/error translation and budgeted allocation for the adapted userspace broker; platform admission remains conditional

**Author:** translator; source/interface finding independently accepted by root
Return45. This selects the following adapter construction inside claims57/59's
ordinary IPv4/IPv6 packet service. It does not select unchanged libslirp, close
every stack protocol, or convert claim65's available socket mechanism into a
working sandbox profile. The libslirp pin remains
`26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d`; Darwin source remains Apple
`xnu-12377.1.9`. Only retained primary sources were inspected; no new source
retrieval, code or native execution occurred.

#### Reply and error ownership is explicit

Use unprivileged Darwin datagram ICMP sockets, not raw/root or UDP-echo fallback,
for the new external IPv6 echo branch. Keep the stack's existing TCP/UDP and
local-control paths; the same receive-validation principle also applies to its
IPv4 echo adapter. One broker event owner manages bounded request records. Before
sending, reserve the record, retained original guest packet/quote bytes, a host
identity and socket/buffer capacity. Record guest source/destination, echo ID and
sequence, payload length/content, guest incarnation, host destination including
scope, actual host source context and a unique **currently outstanding** host
ID/sequence pair. Host ID reuse must not alias an outstanding request. An internal
incarnation cannot authenticate or disambiguate all delayed wire packets after
identity reuse; matching is a NAT demultiplexing rule, not a new network security
guarantee. Reset discards old records/replies and never replays an accepted send.

The adapter submits the ICMP body to the host API; XNU chooses/builds the host
IPv6 header and overwrites the outgoing checksum. Retain the guest's packet
separately. A successful datagram send means socket acceptance only. EAGAIN or
finite resource exhaustion may drop the admitted packet and complete its guest
TX buffer under claim57; no unbounded retransmit queue is introduced. Errors
that mean the required socket form/policy is unavailable fail backend readiness
(or contain a backend that loses the required authority), rather than silently
switching to UDP echo or calling every request ordinary packet loss.
[Darwin send/checksum source](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/raw_ip6.c#L702),
[unprivileged send restrictions](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/icmp6.c#L3233).

Receive with an admission-bounded buffer and ancillary space; reject truncated
or malformed input without allocating from its advertised length. A reply must
be echo-reply/code0 with a matching outstanding host identity, destination/source
scope context and expected payload. Restore the guest ID/sequence, construct its
IPv6 source/destination and recompute the guest checksum. Consume the record once;
a duplicate after completion has no live record and cannot cause a second
completion. Network replay after a later indistinguishable request remains
ordinary untrusted network input, not proof of internal generation failure.
Darwin's actual `icmp6_rip6_input` filters protocol/scoped addresses/types but not
echo IDs; its ancillary packet-info and returned source address must therefore
feed this adapter's checks. Do not require an ICMP **error's outer source** to
equal the echo destination: a router may generate it. Match its quoted host
packet instead, including address scope and sufficient ICMP identity; ignore
unrelated, malformed or insufficient quotes. Do not accept an error merely
because one request is outstanding.
[Receive source](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/icmp6.c#L2115).

Select a typed error-builder input
`{guest_request, error_kind, code, optional_mtu, optional_guest_pointer, reporter}`.
The retained original guest packet supplies the quote; no quoted host packet is
copied wholesale as a guest original. The builder initializes all fields, bounds
the quote to its output budget, and recomputes the guest checksum. Its cases are:

| Host observation | Selected guest-facing treatment |
| --- | --- |
| Matched destination-unreachable or time-exceeded message | Preserve a supported type/code and reporter where representable; rebuild the quote using the recorded guest packet. A router reporter is permitted. |
| ENETUNREACH / EHOSTUNREACH before a send was accepted | Local gateway-origin unreachable/no-route or address-unreachable respectively; no invented remote delivery. Other errno values are not all treated as port-unreachable. |
| Matched Packet Too Big with a validated MTU | Use that path MTU through the host-to-guest size mapping below; never substitute the configured interface MTU merely because it is available. |
| EMSGSIZE without a usable path-MTU value | No fabricated numeric PTB. Retain a bounded diagnostic/drop outcome while obtaining a supported MTU observation; this branch cannot be advertised as complete PMTU propagation. |
