| Parameter Problem | Export only when the offending host field has a known guest-field correspondence, with an explicitly translated pointer. A pointer into a host-generated-only header/option is not a guest pointer. Unrelated/unmappable diagnostics do not manufacture a guest error pointer. |
| Timeout, transient no capacity, duplicate/unknown quote | Finite loss/expiry with no false remote-success or invented error; retire resources. Persistent socket/configuration failure is a backend failure. |

There is a concrete required libslirp correction here:
[`icmp6_forward_error`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip6_icmp.c#L59)
has **no supplied MTU or parameter-pointer argument**. Its PTB case writes
`slirp->if_mtu`, and its Parameter Problem case is a TODO before quote/checksum
construction. The new adapter must replace/extend this builder; unchanged reuse
would turn a host path MTU of1280 into1500 in this machine and can leave the
parameter field unspecified. This is a source counterexample, not an observed
wire failure. Full initialization of its output buffer is mandatory.

Keep the guest link MTU1500 from57. The translated host echo preserves the ICMP
body length; with equal base IPv6 header lengths and no removed extension bytes,
a matched host path limit maps directly, bounded above by the guest link limit.
If normalization removes guest header bytes, retain the exact size/field mapping:
the guest-size limit is the host-size limit plus removed bytes, bounded by1500.
Do not add a fixed guessed delta or claim support for a guest option whose
semantics the adapter discarded. Fragment/reassembly and extension-header paths
must supply that mapping before using this rule. Lack of one is an explicit
unresolved path, not evidence that all IPv6 packets have the simple shape.
XNU's inspected receive path drops PTB values below1280; this mechanism is not a
promise to deliver every network diagnostic. Existing stack TCP/UDP PMTU behavior
is not proved by adding echo translation. The exact EMSGSIZE-to-MTU observation
and extension/fragment/Parameter-Problem correspondence remain the finite protocol
closure obligation; the table settles the interface without pretending to have
completed those paths.

#### DNS family/scope correction and authority boundary

Correct the retained Darwin `get_dns_addr_libresolv` selection to copy **inside**
the matching-family branch and stop at the first usable matching server. Bound
iteration by the supplied array capacity and validate the provider's count. For
IPv6, copy `sin6_scope_id` with the address; a scoped destination lacking usable
interface scope is not silently converted to scope0. Cache the complete typed
endpoint and configuration generation together. A missing family yields an
explicit unavailable result, not an address from the other union member or a
hard-coded public resolver. `socket.c::sotranslate_out6` already copies the
returned scope into the host sockaddr; the correction belongs in discovery and
cache currency, not in pretending the transport cannot carry scope.
[Discovery source](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/slirp.c#L266),
[endpoint use](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/socket.c#L972).

Keep automatic host resolver configuration as an explicit service, with bounded
query/result records and refresh on configuration change. A discovery callback
must not block the packet owner indefinitely; deliver its typed endpoint result
through the existing bounded control path and retain the old configuration only
under its explicit validity policy. Guest DNS packets cannot request arbitrary
host paths/services or inject new bootstrap grants. This fixes family/scope
selection; it does **not** claim that first-server forwarding implements Apple's
full split-DNS/search-domain routing. Deployment equivalence requiring those
semantics still needs the resolver provider's complete contract.

The retained installed `application.sb` supplies actual policy leads: read rules
for `/private/etc/resolv.conf` and `/private/var/run/resolv.conf`, named
SystemConfiguration configd/DNSConfiguration/NetworkInformation lookups, and an
mDNSResponder local-socket restriction conditioned on network entitlements.
Those are different authority mechanisms, not proof that calling libresolv needs
all of them or that any one is sufficient. Select only ordinary AF_INET/AF_INET6
stream/datagram and datagram-ICMP traffic plus the exact resolver-provider access
actually required. No generic filesystem, task/peer-memory, process control,
AF_SYSTEM/device, root or arbitrary Mach lookup grant follows. Ordinary host
service IP reachability remains allowed, including DNS on the host; local Mach/
Unix service capabilities are separately justified. The execution worker retains
its denied-socket boundary and byte-only broker channel.

#### Aggregate allocation is an admission invariant, not two staging buffers

Select startup-fixed finite limits `(B, F, S, R, T, D)`: budgeted dynamic bytes,
flows, sockets, reassembly/request records, timers/callback records and resolver
operations. Their configured values are resource-policy inputs, not performance
claims; readiness rejects absent, overflowed or unbounded values. All guest/
network-derived stack and adapter allocations debit **B before allocation**,
including mbufs, TCP send/receive windows, fragmentation/reassembly, saved error
quotes, ICMP requests, poll/timer structures and resolver result storage. Charge
actual reserved capacity plus accounting/alignment overhead; growth charges the
old+new peak until old storage is released. A shared socket/flow record is charged
once for bytes and against each applicable object-count limit. Do not count an
object released while a callback still pins it.

New flows/sockets reserve their count and minimum state before OS creation.
Datagram receive sizes, TCP windows and fragment accumulation cannot grow from
untrusted length/FIONREAD without a successful byte reservation. Flow count alone
is insufficient: pinned `socket.c::sorecvfrom` obtains FIONREAD then calls `m_inc`
for a larger UDP buffer; `socreate` and `tcp_newtcpcb` allocate directly with GLib.
The public callback/configuration interface has no aggregate allocator quota.
Therefore this is an **internal allocation-gateway adaptation**, not an assertion
that poll registration can retroactively bound the original library's heap.
[Socket allocations/growth](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/socket.c#L587),
[TCP allocation](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/tcp_subr.c#L251),
[public interface](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/blob/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/libslirp.h).

Exhausted normal admission drops/declines new traffic while servicing control and
teardown. An internal path that cannot safely unwind allocation failure contains
the broker/VM instead of allocating past the budget. A dedicated fixed control
reserve cannot be consumed by packet traffic. The user-space dynamic invariant
is `live_charged_bytes <= B`, independently of the two2048-byte staging slots and
fixed preallocated control state. The object-count bounds likewise include queued,
active and retiring objects until final release. Large legal datagrams/reassembly
consume the same finite budget rather than being falsely restricted to one link
frame; no unchecked jumbo allocation is authorized. Dispatch handles finite work
per turn and returns to the reserved control path. This is a design proof from
mandatory gateways, **not** a completed inventory proving every upstream/GLib
allocation already goes through them.

Kernel socket buffers and platform resolver service allocations are not magically
inside B. Cap broker socket count and request bounded send/receive buffers, verify
the granted settings, and account separately for these external resources. No
whole-kernel physical-memory bound follows from this user-space formula or a
file-descriptor limit. Backend startup must establish that its selected OS limits
are enforceable; existing source here does not supply that platform proof.

**Bounded conclusion / next decision:** typed identity/quote/error construction,
family/scope-preserving DNS selection and preallocation byte/object charging are
the selected adapted-component contracts. They explain what must change without
changing the guest network grant or generator choice. Complete backend selection
remains conditional on (1) exact unprivileged socket/resolver policy admission and
external-resource limits, and (2) the explicitly identified MTU/extension/error
paths. Allocation-gateway construction/conformance and numerical budget tuning
belong to planning under the selected byte/object invariant; no particular numeric
capacity is needed for its correctness proof. The next smallest bounded source
pass is the Darwin **EMSGSIZE/path-MTU observation and resolver-provider authority
binding**, using the already named API/source paths; it should return a concrete
usable binding or stop at the first missing platform contract. Do not rerun a
TCP-only mock or label an echo-only success full networking. Root independent
review comes first. No experiment, user permission gate or broader survey is
requested by this submission.


**Exact authority premise, no new generic containment campaign:** determine the
minimum continuing grants for `socket(AF_INET6, SOCK_DGRAM, IPPROTO_ICMPV6)`,
nonblocking `sendmsg/recvmsg`, packet-info/hop-limit reception and `IPV6_PATHMTU`
under the selected separate-broker sandbox; determine which named configuration
endpoint/read the actual `res_ninit/res_getservers` provider needs for a current
typed server list. The installed configd/DNSConfiguration/NetworkInformation and
mDNSResponder/read rules above are leads, not a blanket selected allow list.
The admitted ordinary TCP/UDP and packet capability does not imply these private
resolver service rights. Existing default-deny, sanitized launch, pipe and
MIG205 findings are reused unchanged; no repeat HVF/pipe/task probe is proposed.
This finite API/grant binding, plus the scoped MTU return semantics, is the
remaining platform question. Buffer/flow numbers are planning inputs; a numerical
tuning campaign is not required to accept preallocation accounting.

## SMI reception with immutable platform firmware

**Method:** machine-authored30-minute source/interface comparison after64,
September20. Read Track00's direct-boot/Linux/SMP envelope and Track03's explicit
real-mode exclusion/startup32 and lifecycle clauses first. Reinspect retained
Intel3C081 §§32.2–32.5/32.8/32.10/32.12, Intel3A092 §§7.8–7.9 and the existing
RSM reference in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-msr-audit/vol2b.txt`
(253667-092US pp4-592–593). No native execution, code, new platform privilege or
general firmware implementation. Claim65 belongs to the separately authored
network continuation; this is claim66.

### CLM-03-03-66 — Select a firmware-owned immediate-RSM service instead of inventing an unsupported-SMI instruction fault

**Conditional platform choice independently accepted by root Return46.** The
accepted envelope requires an unmodified modern Linux machine, not equivalence
to every OEM physical-Haswell firmware. Track03 excludes general guest real-mode
execution and treats optional lifecycle support explicitly. Neither document
says that a legal SMI request becomes reserved or may be silently dropped.
Select a preinitialized virtual platform whose immutable SMI firmware performs
only RSM, implemented as a finite resident semantic service. This preserves SMI
reception without adding a general guest real-mode/SMM code generator or OEM
firmware service interface.

| Alternative | Consequence and choice |
| --- | --- |
| Treat legal ICR/IOAPIC SMI as no-op, #GP or VM termination | No current source or accepted scope supplies that CPU behavior. Merely not advertising an OEM service does not establish it. Do not select. |
| Import unrestricted guest SMM/real-mode execution | Requires arbitrary SMRAM handler code, mode transitions and firmware/device services beyond the accepted phase. Not selected by this pass. |
| Fixed platform-owned RSM firmware | Supplies valid SMI entry/return/pending semantics with immutable protected code and a finite saved-state record. Select under the construction below; OEM-specific services remain absent because this platform never supplies them. |

**Boot and visibility are part of the choice.** The direct-boot manifest installs
one distinct64KiB SMRAM bank per admitted vCPU below4GiB, with disjoint reserved
physical extents and fixed relocated SMBASE values. Firmware initialization is
complete before the BSP/AP can accept SMI. Each bank contains the immutable
RSM instruction at SMBASE+0x8000 and the correct Intel64-format save region; use
[Intel3C081 §32.4/Table32-3](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/789585/326019-sdm-vol-3c.pdf),
not the older32-bit-only save map. Per-vCPU banks prevent simultaneous SMI saves
from aliasing. This is a prepared platform state, not a claim that hardware-reset
SMBASE defaults are already distinct; the manual explicitly says they are not.

SMRAM is closed and locked at direct boot. Normal guest CPU accesses, DMA,
compiler source capture and other vCPUs' service code cannot read/write its
private backing. The normal physical map treats those reserved extents as the
chosen non-RAM hole, never as a writable alias to the SMM bank. Only the owning
trusted event service can select its SMM address space. No chipset register
path may reopen it or let the guest replace RSM, alter the save area or relocate
SMBASE. If a reused bridge exposes SMRAM configuration, its boot lock/readback
and irreversible-until-platform-reset rules must agree with that closed state;
a model with an operative guest-writable open path is incompatible with this
selection. This uses the existing private-state address/physical-route authority,
not a new host entitlement or a claim that an E820 reservation alone protects
memory. Firmware-image replacement and OEM SMM driver interfaces are not exposed.

The fixed instruction can be represented by a permanent resident service rather
than decoded as arbitrary x86 firmware. Its validity rests on the established
immutable handler identity and inaccessible save area; a mutable handler could
not use this shortcut. A typed private save record may represent fields that
have no software observer, but its mapping and restore semantics must refine
the documented save image. This is semantic execution of the selected firmware,
not a claim that SMI has no effect or that all guest state can simply be copied
back after a delay.

**Entry and store completion.** At a legal SMI boundary, finish the admitted
instruction/partial-operation phase, materialize the required saved fields and
complete prior guest stores under the accepted memory/coordinator contract.
An existing DRAIN_SERVICE operation uses its current authority and completion
record; it never waits for a second epoch while holding the first. Native drains
and typed store completions establish visibility before the SMI entry commit.
This does not wait for unrelated asynchronous disk/network operations merely
because their guest descriptors were published earlier.

Set the SMM-active event state and perform the save/entry transition before
executing the immutable RSM service. Intel §32.3.1 requires retiring instructions
and completing stores; §§32.4–32.5 give save/entry state. The private record must
preserve the applicable registers, flags, hidden segment/descriptor state and
control state, including fields not writable/readable by an SMM handler. Live
banks that the manual says are **not** automatically saved/restored—such as
x87 state, CR2, MTRRs and APIC internal state—remain owned by their normal owners;
the one-instruction firmware does not modify them. In particular no restore
snapshot rolls back IRR/ISR/TMR, TSC/device time, another CPU, memory generations
or backend completions that changed during this service.

**Return, shadows and debug.**
[RSM in Intel2B092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922481/253667-092-sdm-vol-2b.pdf)
restores the SMM state and resumes the interrupted program; outside SMM it #UD.
Keep that ordinary guest decode rule. The immutable firmware cannot write an
invalid saved control tuple or request SMBASE relocation, so restoration uses the
previously validated tuple; internal corrupt state is a contained runtime failure,
not permission to publish arbitrary native mappings.

Select the explicitly permitted §32.2 option to inhibit SMI for the instruction
following STI, MOV-to-SS or POP-to-SS. Retain the pending SMI through those shadows;
internal repair/coordination does not clear them. Thus the fixed service does not
need to invent preservation of a live STI/SS inhibition through its RSM: it starts
after that selected inhibition ends through the genuine semantic boundary.
This is an SMI-specific choice; it does not change63's separately selected NMI
behavior after STI. Consecutive SS operations and exceptional termination still
use the existing instruction-semantic owner, not a counter of native checkpoints.

Prior-instruction debug candidates are resolved by actual event priority, not
wholesale snapshot restoration. Intel3A092 Table7-2 puts SMI above previous-
instruction traps and says lower-priority exceptions are discarded while lower-
priority interrupts remain pending. When SMI wins such a boundary, retire the
lower debug *candidate* by that rule; preserve already committed DR6 and saved
architectural flags, and let the debug owner establish subsequent conditions at
the resumed boundary/instruction. Do not resurrect an old pending #DB by restoring
an entire event-owner snapshot, nor manufacture single stepping of the trusted
RSM body. The existing SS/INT1 fusion uncertainty is not settled by this SMI
priority rule, and no new blanket rule for faults inside instructions is inferred.

**NMI state and concurrent event retention.** Save the pre-SMI NMI-blocking
state separately from the register image. Entry inhibits NMI, but §32.8 requires
RSM to keep NMI blocked if it was blocked before SMI—for example SMI interrupting
an NMI handler. If it was not blocked, RSM releases that SMM inhibition. One
pending NMI arriving during SMM remains pending, and is eligible after return
only under that restored blocking state. The service does not execute IRET or
enable interrupts inside SMM; neither a native repair nor RSM is mistaken for
completion of the preexisting NMI handler.

Retain at most one additional SMI while SMM-active, as §32.3.1 specifies. A
subsequent SMI can run after RSM, not recursively inside this service. The native
service invocation processes one entry/return and yields to the existing owner;
a continuously asserted source cannot create an unbounded inner service loop.
INIT is inhibited during SMM by §32.5.1; retain the ordered initialization request
for the post-return core-event transition, rather than resetting the saved tuple
mid-RSM. SIPI retains64's target wait-state/incarnation rules and cannot overwrite
an already admitted startup. These core-event records are distinct from fixed
IRR and its TMR fold. The existing selector orders admitted classes at the next
boundary; this service does not wait for a guest handler, introduce an unbounded
message log or roll back later arrivals.

A further explicit source case matters for AP startup: §32.2 says SMI is pended
while an AP waits for SIPI, then serviced when SIPI arrives **before** that startup
is handled. Keep one pending SMI and retain the accepted startup vector/identity;
run the immutable service against the waiting target's prepared state, then
complete the startup transition unless a higher-priority reset transition has
superseded that incarnation. It is incorrect either to run arbitrary SMM code
while the AP is waiting or to discard the pending SMI because the AP was not yet
executing. The service's state save/return is finite even for that PE/PG-clear
prepared state; it does not execute the guest real-mode trampoline.

**HLT and I/O are not transparent no-ops.** Under §32.10, leave the auto-HALT
restart flag unchanged. If SMI arrived during HLT, return to the HLT instruction
location through normal current code admission; do not simply restore a host
`halted` boolean. Another CPU may have changed the code page, and pending eligible
NMI/interrupts must be reconsidered before guest execution. Without auto-HALT,
resume the saved interrupted instruction/phase normally. §32.12 initializes the
I/O restart field to zero; the immutable RSM firmware never changes it. Completed
port/MMIO effects therefore are not replayed, including completed REP-I/O
iterations. A native service fault remains internal recovery, not an SMM-requested
I/O restart.

**Lifecycle and compatibility consequence.** Local INIT retains the platform's
initialized per-vCPU firmware bank/SMBASE according to its mode-specific state
contract; a whole platform reconstruction must initialize and lock the banks
again before any CPU admission. None of this adds arbitrary real-mode boot,
UEFI/Windows firmware, OEM management commands or guest replacement of SMM code.
The ordinary Linux machine has explicit non-OEM firmware identity and no advertised
OEM SMI service interface; a program issuing a legal SMI still receives the
specified entry/firmware-return effect. That is a concrete virtual-platform
choice rather than rejection of mandatory CPU behavior merely for convenience.

**Disposition:** prefer this finite firmware-owned service over unsupported-SMI
fault/drop semantics. Its architecture can be reviewed from state ownership,
protected visibility and source-defined entry/return without first implementing
a general SMM interpreter. It adds a bounded saved-state/service cost and a real
boot lock/physical-map obligation; the previous shorthand “no SMM” must mean
**no guest-programmable SMM firmware or SMI-dependent ACPI**, not absence of SMI
reception. Independent review must check that refinement before this choice or
question8 is accepted. Questions5/10 still own precise instruction/event delivery
and the concrete startup/boot manifest; no runtime or full-track result follows.
Next action is independent source/interface review of this proposed selection,
not an SMM implementation campaign or another unchanged event probe.

`res_ninit/res_getservers` to their actual Darwin providers and authority. Reuse
retained XNU/profile sources; fetch only missing primary files if needed. No
network socket/probe, code, build, native execution or general backend survey.
Stop at a usable conditional interface or an exact missing provider/grant premise;
do not demand numerical budget tuning to accept admission accounting.


**Claim66 disposition:** independent root Return46 accepts the protected
firmware choice and scoped question8 contract. The earlier no-SMM shorthand
means no programmable/OEM firmware or SMI-dependent ACPI, not missing SMI
reception. Concrete manifest/startup scope and precise delivery remain with

### CLM-03-03-68 — Darwin provides a connected route-MTU query and named DNS-configuration provider; both require specific adapter changes

**Current disposition:** independently accepted in root Return47, within the
source/interface scope and explicit premises below; no runtime acceptance.

**Author:** translator; source findings and conditional binding independently
accepted by root Return47.
The targeted source set is retained at
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-darwin-network-binding-01`: XNU
`xnu-12377.1.9`, Apple `libresolv-96` and `configd-1405.120.5`, with exact URLs,
lengths/hashes and tag-list responses. These are inspected releases, not a claim
of byte identity with installed macOS26.6.2. Initial sandbox DNS failure and a
wrong `dnsinfo.c` path/404 are retained; the directory listing identified
`dnsinfo_copy.c`. A later targeted Libnotify tag request returned403/rate-limit;
no Libnotify implementation was fetched or inferred. No native socket, resolver
or service API was invoked.

#### The real MTU API is useful, but not an unconnected error oracle

`icmp6_dgram_ctloutput` explicitly permits `IPV6_PATHMTU` for the unprivileged
ICMPv6 datagram form. XNU
[`ip6_ctloutput`, IPV6_PATHMTU](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/ip6_output.c#L3012)
requires `SS_ISCONNECTED`, returning ENOTCONN otherwise. On success it returns
`struct ip6_mtuinfo`, **not a bare integer**: the structure is zeroed and its
`ip6m_mtu` member is filled from `ip6_getpmtu`, capped at IPV6_MAXPACKET. Its
address member therefore is not a returned destination identity; associate the
result with the socket's recorded connection. Require the complete expected
option length and validate the value before attaching it to an error record.

`ip6_getpmtu` reads route MTU, bounded by interface MTU, or returns an error when
no route/interface can supply a value. This is the host's current route estimate,
not the independently measured remote path limit. The getter explicitly does
not consider source routing or optional interface-selection information. Its
route construction therefore does not prove applicability to every bound/scoped
routing variant. Use it for a demonstrably matching route context; leave that
match explicit for scoped/interface-pinned requests.
[XNU route-MTU helper](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/ip6_output.c#L2336).

EMSGSIZE alone is not a numeric MTU witness. The inspected output code returns
it for a don't-fragment/link-limit violation, but also for incompatible TSO
fragmentation, a route MTU below1280, unfragmentable jumbo payload or insufficient
fragment room. Querying after an error may produce a later route estimate;
do not attach it as though it identifies the precise earlier cause. A matching
usable value can support claim67's typed conservative guest limit; an absent,
inconsistent or inapplicable value retains the explicit unknown-MTU/drop branch.
The datagram ICMP option allowlist includes PATHMTU but not `IPV6_DONTFRAG` or
`IPV6_RECVPATHMTU`, so no automatic notification or controllable no-fragment
semantics is inferred from their general IPv6 definitions.
[Fragmentation/error branches](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/ip6_output.c#L1753).

There is a concrete composition constraint. `rip6_connect` records the foreign
address and source context, and `icmp6_rip6_input` rejects received outer sources
that do not match a non-unspecified foreign address. Connecting the one echo
socket to the target therefore filters a router's outer-source ICMP error, even
when its quote identifies the outstanding request. “Connect then receive every
remote error on that socket” is not a valid adapter proof.
[Connect](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/raw_ip6.c#L1138),
[actual receive matching](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/netinet6/icmp6.c#L2153).

Select a **separate bounded connected query socket** when a matching route-MTU
estimate is needed, retaining the unconnected datagram receive/send path for
router errors. Query sockets consume the same S/socket and byte/resource budget;
there is no per-error unbounded socket creation. The unconnected receive path
must use a narrow ICMP type filter plus claim67's quote/identity validation.
It can still receive unrelated host ICMP messages matching its address/type
filters: the source has no kernel echo-ID demultiplexing. Ignoring those messages
in the adapter does not prove a compromised broker cannot observe them. Thus the
precise network receive capability, including that fanout, must be accounted for
when binding the broker policy; a socket API's non-root availability is not an
isolation proof. No task/file/peer-RAM authority is implied. A requirement to
exclude even unrelated host ICMP quotations would need a narrower endpoint
mechanism; the inspected API does not establish one. The current ordinary
network grant is not silently promoted to arbitrary packet capture.

This resolves the existence and shape of a route-MTU query and rejects an
incorrect one-socket composition. It does not settle exact guest PMTU probe
semantics, extension/fragment field correspondence or every route-option case.
Source-socket fragmentation and asynchronous network loss remain distinct from
a numeric guest PTB result; no wrong MTU is manufactured to hide the distinction.

#### `res_ninit` really calls configd's DNS snapshot provider, then may fall back

In inspected Apple
[`res_init.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/libresolv/blob/libresolv-96/res_init.c#L600),
`res_ninit` clears the supplied state and calls `__res_vinit`. This first invokes
`dns_configuration_copy`; on failure it falls back to the resolver file path
`/etc/resolv.conf`. The successful service branch takes `resolver[0]`, registers
configuration-change notification, and constructs the BIND-compatible state.
`res_getservers` merely copies that state into caller slots; it opens no service.
It fills at most `cnt` slots but returns total `nscount`, so the caller must clamp
iteration to its supplied capacity. Installed SDK26.4 `resolv.h` maps these names
to the `res_9_*` API; this corroborates the API family, not the installed library's
exact implementation revision.

A second, upstream scope-loss boundary invalidates a copy-only repair:
`__res_vinit_from_dns` converts each IPv6 nameserver to address text with
`inet_ntop`, and `res_build("nameserver")` creates a zeroed sockaddr via
`inet_pton` without restoring `sin6_scope_id`. A scoped server can therefore
already have lost its scope before libslirp's corrected `memcpy` sees it.
`resolver[0]` also does not implement supplemental/scoped/service-specific resolver
selection. This is a primary-source limitation of this inspected provider path,
not a claim that all Darwin name-resolution APIs discard scope.
[Conversion](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/libresolv/blob/libresolv-96/res_init.c#L1102),
[reconstruction](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/libresolv/blob/libresolv-96/res_init.c#L449).

Select a **typed DNS configuration snapshot provider before that lossy conversion**
for the adapted backend, rather than claiming the unchanged res_ninit chain is
sufficient. Apple
[`dnsinfo_copy.c`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/configd/blob/configd-1405.120.5/dnsinfo/dnsinfo_copy.c#L82)
and its header expose `dns_configuration_copy/free`, raw sockaddr nameservers,
resolver interface index/flags and configuration generation. Preserve these
fields into bounded immutable endpoint records; never invent scope from an
unrelated default interface. A scoped endpoint with inconsistent/missing scope
is unavailable until resolved by the provider's explicit routing policy. The
installed SDK's `usr/lib/system/libsystem_configuration.tbd` exports both symbols.
This is a concrete private provider candidate under the project's admitted Apple
capabilities, not a promise of stable public ABI or full split-DNS equivalence.
The configured resolver-set selection still must match the deployment's stated
DNS policy; do not substitute an arbitrary public DNS server.

The exact principal service is now known. On native macOS,
`DNSINFO_SERVICE_NAME` is **`com.apple.SystemConfiguration.DNSConfiguration`**;
`libSC_info_client_create` uses `xpc_connection_create_mach_service` with
`XPC_CONNECTION_MACH_SERVICE_PRIVILEGED`, meaning the selected system service
namespace, not permission to run the broker as root. The copy request returns a
configuration data object, checked against a 1-MiB buffer-plus-expansion ceiling
in this inspected implementation. That check is useful service-input evidence,
not an aggregate bound on every libXPC or kernel allocation. The installed
application policy already contains this exact named lookup; it does not prove
that copying one rule produces a complete accepted custom sandbox.
[Service name](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/configd/blob/configd-1405.120.5/libSystemConfiguration/libSystemConfiguration_client.h#L39),
[connection](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/configd/blob/configd-1405.120.5/libSystemConfiguration/libSystemConfiguration_client.c#L113).

The convenience provider synchronously requests a reply and retries interrupted
connections in a loop without a finite retry counter. It therefore must not run
on the packet/control owner. Select at most one provider job at a time, with a
supervised deadline and bounded retained result; an overdue job cannot cause
unlimited replacement threads or retries. Process containment is the fallback
for an uninterruptible provider job, not unsafe thread cancellation. Startup and
configuration refresh remain independently serviceable from native guest RAM
transactions. The library's thread-level synchronous call is not itself an
accepted bounded adapter callback.

The old libresolv chain additionally uses `notify_register_check/notify_check/
notify_cancel` for key
`com.apple.system.SystemConfiguration.dns_configuration`. Those API/key dependencies
are explicit, but their low-level notification service/right closure was not
established by this pass. For the selected direct snapshot provider, choose
bounded configuration-generation refresh without this extra notification
registration initially; this avoids claiming a missing notify grant. Poll/refresh
frequency is planning policy, with explicit snapshot staleness, not immediate
notification equivalence. Do not additionally allow mDNSResponder, generic
configd, NetworkInformation or arbitrary resolver files merely because they
appeared in the broad installed profile. They are not the inspected direct copy
path. Failure of this chosen provider is explicit; do not silently fall through
to a file or different service with wider rights.
[Notification wrapper](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/libresolv/blob/libresolv-96/dns.c#L1039),
[synchronous retry](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/configd/blob/configd-1405.120.5/libSystemConfiguration/libSystemConfiguration_client.c#L189).

**Stop / exact remaining premise:** source now identifies a usable conditional
MTU-query shape and a concrete scope-preserving DNS snapshot interface/service,
with finite adapter ownership. Remaining platform admission is the selected
broker's ordinary IPv4/IPv6 and datagram-ICMP socket operations/options plus this
one DNSConfiguration lookup/client lifecycle and bounded provider supervision.
Unconnected ICMP receive fanout and getter route applicability must be reconciled
with the declared network authority, rather than disguised as task isolation.
Full PMTU/extension/error and resolver-set semantics remain named protocol choices;
no full backend selection follows yet. Existing launch/pipe/MIG205 findings are
reused, with no repeat generic containment test or numeric-budget campaign.
Next: root independent review, then choose the precise ICMP receive/route and DNS
scope contract or return a concrete incompatibility. No native experiment is
proposed automatically, and no further source survey starts in this pass.

**Retained-input check for68:** all 14 successful downloaded source-file records
were independently length/hash checked by the author against their manifests;
the two inspected SDK text inputs are retained with hashes in installed-inputs.json.
Tag lists and failed retrieval records remain alongside them. This is retention
verification, not a resolver/socket test or independent findings acceptance.

**Method:** machine-authored bounded 30-minute source/design synthesis, requested
September 20 after accepted66; root reviews independently. Read Track00/03,

only missing Linux loader/root-mount/reset consumers. No implementation, image
