### CLM-03-03-69 — Select a parameterized cold-boot manifest, installed-root route and reconstruction lifecycle

**Current disposition:** independently accepted in root Return48, within the
source/interface scope and explicit premises below; no runtime acceptance.

**Architectural choice independently accepted by root Return48.** This is a
loader/platform contract, not a claim that an unwritten loader boots these images.
The numerical addresses below are selected virtual-machine layout, not facts
inferred from the physical Haswell or an unchanged QEMU board.

**Target artifacts and installed systems.** Keep both inherited exact targets:
Ubuntu `linux-image-6.8.0-139-generic`, package `6.8.0-139.139`, and Debian
`linux-image-6.12.107+deb13-amd64`, package `6.12.107-1`. INV-01-34 supplies their
vendor-source/config provenance and distinct AP startup paths. For Debian the
already retained official installer kernel is an exact usable kernel byte input:
12,142,528 bytes, SHA256
`2b2358b37674d2505350528875bb17afae2a36522a9e8a9417eaca65a7da0e08`
(claim13). Its paired installer initrd retains its recorded digest and purpose;
it is **not** relabeled an installed-root initramfs. The Ubuntu image bytes have
not been retained by this pass; the headers-package digest is not a kernel-image
digest. Every concrete launch must bind actual kernel, optional initramfs and
initial root-volume bytes to lengths/digests and package provenance before
creation; an installed writable disk then has its own persistent object identity.
An exact version choice is not evidence that those missing bytes were inspected.

Choose two ordinary installed-root admissions, not an installer-only product:

- Ubuntu's audited config has `VIRTIO_MMIO=y`, `VIRTIO_BLK=y`, `EXT4_FS=y`,
  GPT/MSDOS partition parsers and `DEVTMPFS_MOUNT=y`. Therefore select an ordinary
  installed ext4 root on the first virtio disk's first partition, no external
  initramfs required, with `root=/dev/vda1 rootfstype=ext4 rootwait ro`.
  The retained [v6.8 root-mount consumer](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/init/do_mounts.c)
  waits for device probing, resolves the root device, mounts the requested
  filesystem and mounts devtmpfs. This is a source construction for an installed
  root with working `/sbin/init` and matching distro userspace/modules; it does
  not claim arbitrary encrypted/LVM/RAID roots work without their normal initrd.
- Debian selects its normal distro-generated initramfs for the same kernel ABI,
  containing virtio core/ring/MMIO/block plus ext4 and their recursive module,
  firmware-if-required and userspace loader dependencies. Use the standard
  distribution image-generation mechanism and matching packaged modules, not
  kernel or startup patches. Claims11–12 establish the normal broad module path;
  admission checks the actual resulting archive and its root-device script,
  rather than assuming defaults prove contents. Its root volume is an ordinary
  installed Debian system. An archive missing a required component is rejected
  before launch, not repaired by hoping the installer retrieves it after root
  mount. Claims13–16 remain a separate installer compatibility route.

The root filesystem is a **manifest input**, not one privileged research disk
whose digest all workloads must share. Filesystem format, partition, root locator,
init program and initramfs dependency closure are declared and checked per input.
This preserves diverse unmodified systems while admitting a concrete minimal
Ubuntu path. Kernel/initramfs/root provisioning is ordinary bootloader input
preparation; neither rebuilding a custom guest kernel nor modifying the audited
installer archive is selected. Actual stock boot is later implementation
conformance, not evidence supplied here.

**One physical map and topology.** The manifest fixes N, RAM extents, kernel and
initrd intervals, table graph, all I/O/MMIO ranges and device identities together.
N starts at2, stays within accepted CPU16's1..64 and actual resource admission;
one socket/one thread per core, APIC IDs0..N-1 agree across CPUID, MADT, mailbox,
LAPIC and SMRAM bank ownership. No disabled spare/hotplug CPUs are advertised.
Choose this concrete family (half-open byte ranges):

| Guest physical range | Selected owner / E820 disposition |
| --- | --- |
| `0..0x1000` | Reserved low page |
| `0x1000..0xa0000` | Conventional RAM; boot_params at0x10000, command buffer0x11000..0x15000, bootstrap stack0x16000..0x18000 and GDT0x18000..0x19000 are temporary loader uses |
| `0xa0000..0x100000` | Reserved legacy hole, no guest firmware execution required |
| `0x100000..0x200000` | Reserved loader page tables; keep until every boot reference is retired |
| `0x200000..0x600000` | Reserved normal-map hole; up to64 distinct64KiB SMRAM banks exist only in the owning CPU's protected SMM namespace, as66 requires |
| `0x600000..0x800000` | ACPI NVS for RSDP/table graph/FACS and one page-aligned4KiB version0 AP mailbox; tables are writable where Linux legitimately updates them |
| `0x800000..0x1000000` | ACPI NVS boot-service scratch: bounded per-AP stack/GDT/page-table records, with nonoverlapping allocations and explicit reference retirement |
| `0x1000000..L` | Ordinary low RAM; choose L=2GiB initially, configurable1..3GiB subject to fit/resource admission |
| `L..4GiB` | Reserved/non-RAM aperture, including selected devices below |
| `4GiB..4GiB+H` | Optional ordinary high RAM, admitted within accepted40-bit physical width and host resource budget; H=0 initially |

E820 describes actual RAM and reserved holes, not merely requested capacity.
The NVS allocations have finite size checks; oversized tables/boot scratch fail
creation before any runnable CPU rather than overlap another object. The guest
kernel may allocate its own below1MiB trampoline from conventional RAM after
consuming temporary BSP data. Do not reserve all low RAM or skip that trampoline.
Host shim/code/native page tables are **not** guest physical RAM in this map.

Use IOAPIC at0xfec00000 and LAPIC at0xfee00000; assign4KiB modern virtio-MMIO
windows at0xf0000000/0xf0001000/0xf0002000 for block/network/entropy with dedicated
level/high GSI16/17/18. Their selected profiles remain04-28,03-57 and03-56.
COM1 stays0x3f8..0x3ff/IRQ4; PIT/PIC/RTC and IRQ0→GSI2/SCI9 use reviewed23/63
wiring. PMBASE is0xb000 (64-byte decode), GPE0 remains the reused model's
0xafe0..0xafe3. Reserve the virtual ACPI reset byte at0xb040 separately: it
is outside PM0xb000..0xb03f, GPE0 and PCI configuration0xcf8..0xcff. SMBus
decode is disabled in this initial manifest, so it does not claim that byte.
The reset register is a virtual ACPI-described device, not the chipset CF9
register; no overlapping byte-versus-dword PCI decode arbitration is implied.
Reserve all other constructed PCI/ISA configuration ranges in the
same allocator; unused SMBus/hotplug functions are disabled/not advertised rather
than left with overlapping decode. Selected PC/PIIX components retain their
actual configuration access semantics; this does not instantiate every PC device.

Build conventional RSDP/XSDT/FADT/FACS/DSDT/MADT from those live configured
objects, then apply relocations/checksums. DSDT supplies LNRO0005 resources,
serial PNP0501, S5 and enabled CPUs; MADT supplies enabled APIC entries, IOAPIC,
IRQ0 override and version0 wakeup mailbox. SCI polarity/trigger is the accepted
level/low route. No S3/S4, PCI/CPU hotplug or absent HPET/EFI table is advertised.
Use ACPI-only PM mode with SCI_EN already set and zero SMI enable/disable command;
that device initialization choice is independent of66's CPU SMI reception.
Publish the final RSDP via `boot_params.acpi_rsdp_addr`, as the primary
[zero-page layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/Documentation/arch/x86/zero-page.rst)
defines. Linux6.12 mutates the version0 MADT mailbox address after caching it;
therefore making the ACPI graph globally read-only would be an incorrect shortcut.

**Loader constraint recipe.** Validate the supplied bzImage setup signature,
protocol,64-bit capability, file lengths and overflow before using header fields.
Zero boot_params, copy only the specified setup-header extent and populate E820,
RSDP, command line and optional initrd fields. Preserve kernel bytes. Load the
protected payload after `(setup_sects+1)*512` (zero setup_sects has the protocol's
legacy default), choose an aligned K>=16MiB satisfying relocatable/alignment rules,
or require the specified fixed address for a nonrelocatable input. Reserve the
entire `[runtime_start,runtime_start+init_size)` and loaded payload, not just
compressed file bytes. For the aligned relocatable choice runtime_start=K; for
a fixed-address input require K=pref_address and all reservations to fit there.
Reject an input whose payload interval or required initialization extent does
not fit the selected RAM region, even if its compressed file alone fits. Place
initrd downward in remaining low RAM: its exclusive end must be <=L and its
last byte <=initrd_addr_max, with checked arithmetic. Reject overlap with either
reserved kernel interval or any other live boot allocation.
Command text including NUL must fit both the selected buffer and header limit.

Enter K+0x200 in64-bit mode with four-level identity mappings covering the full
init_size interval, boot_params and command buffer; include other loader data
actually accessed before kernel mappings replace them. Set flat boot CS0x10 and
DS/ES/SS0x18, paging/PAE/long mode enabled, IF clear and RSI=boot_params. Initial
LA57=0 matches the selected48-bit linear CPU profile; no guest image is patched to
avoid a paging transition. The authoritative source is the
[Linux64-bit boot protocol](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst),
including its runtime-start calculation. Native HVF registers represent the shim;
these are x86 semantic state and shadows, never assumed native register values.
Default console parameters are `earlycon=uart8250,io,0x3f8,115200n8 console=ttyS0,115200n8`.

APs begin in the reviewed firmware-mailbox waiting service, not guest real mode.
The version0 responder snapshots the target/vector, admits its one wake and
initial long-mode identity map, acknowledges only after owning that state, then
executes the **actual** unmodified trampoline. Retain required bootstrap tables,
stack and descriptors until their references are retired; acknowledgement alone
does not release them. Ubuntu's paging-off protected32 and Debian's conditional
path retain INV-01-34's semantics. SMI-before-SIPI and ordinary INIT/SIPI reception
remain64/66's CPU rules; the supported cold Linux mailbox route is not a promise
to run arbitrary guest real-mode startup programs.

**Lifecycle selection.** Required cold start is a new machine incarnation:
validate inputs/grants, initialize memory/SMRAM/tables/device decode, establish
clock epoch, then publish BSP and AP-wait states. Required S5 follows22/30 and
04-29: stop new admissions, resolve admitted effects, drain backend lanes and
perform truthful full-sync barriers before final resource release. No guest
filesystem-consistency promise follows merely from S5 or a host stop.

Select optional **cold reconstruction reboot**, not in-place reset or firmware
execution: add an explicit FADT reset GAS (SystemIO,8bits,offset0,address0xb040,
value0x06, RESET_REG_SUP) to the PIIX-derived table and a corresponding owned
reset register. This is an explicit adaptation—the inspected PIIX FADT provider
does not supply the ICH9-only reset fields automatically. The launch command
selects `reboot=acpi`. The
[Linux ACPI reboot consumer](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/acpi/reboot.c)
checks the FADT revision/support flag and writes the published register; the
[x86 reboot dispatcher](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/reboot.c)
tries it before fallback mechanisms under that selection. On the admitted reset
write, stop x86 execution before it can run fallback BIOS real-mode code; drain
old references/backend effects, destroy the old machine and create a fresh one
from the same immutable boot inputs and current disk identity. A failed drain,
flush or reconstruction reports stopped-with-error, never successful restart.
No callback, translation, mailbox wake or IRQ from the old incarnation enters
the new one. Reset is not rollback of disk effects or a transparent RAM snapshot.
Alternate forced BIOS reboot methods are outside this lifecycle contract.

Do not offer CPU hotplug/offline-online, S3/S4, snapshot/migration or SMP kexec.
This is an explicit optional-lifecycle disposition permitted by Track03, not a
claim that accepting INIT or SIPI implies CPU recycling. The inspected
[Linux6.12 mailbox consumer](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/acpi/madt_wakeup.c)
disables CPU offlining for version0 and invalidates its table pointer against
second-kernel reuse; kexec may reach a single CPU but is not promised as a
supported lifecycle. Older target behavior is not upgraded by that newer check.
A user choosing an unsupported lifecycle may fail/stall that guest; native
coordination/management must remain responsive and containment remains mandatory.

**Implications and disposition.** UEFI boot services/runtime variables/Secure Boot
and Windows loader, device and AP-startup contracts are not supplied by this direct
Linux manifest. Their absence does not imply Windows impossibility, and immutable
SMI firmware does not stand in for UEFI. They require another firmware/loader and
compatibility assessment outside the present implementation envelope.

This choice closes a concrete loader/map/topology/lifecycle interface by
construction, conditional on already named CPU, memory, event, device and
containment contracts. Missing implementation or a missing stock boot run is not
an extra architecture gate. Actual Ubuntu image bytes and each installed-root
manifest still need input validation; Debian initramfs dependency closure cannot
be inferred from the installer artifact. Independent review must assess whether
those are normal admission/conformance obligations or a remaining target-specific
architectural contradiction. Next action is that review, not an automatic boot
probe or general firmware survey. No whole question10 acceptance is self-issued.

## Worker capability closure and teardown (claim 70)

**Method before work:** translator-authored 30-minute source/interface synthesis,
2026-09-20, root independently reviews. Reuse49–55's accepted creation/descriptor/
byte-pipe/special-port and MIG205 evidence, with65–68's distinct broker authority.
Inspect only finite residual startup/process-control routes in retained XNU/SDK/
installed policy sources. No profile changes, code, new denial probe or runtime
budget. Stop at a sufficient selected worker contract or an exact indispensable
reachable grant. Trust the named OS isolation/enforcement contract explicitly;
do not turn absence of a debuggable peer baseline into peer-isolation evidence or
require proof against every kernel defect.

### CLM-03-03-70 — a finite per-VM capability contract closes the worker boundary conditionally; ICMP fanout requires a protected trusted endpoint

**Disposition:** source/interface construction independently accepted in root
Return49, within its named premises. This selects a boundary and its enforcement premises, not a claim
that the production launcher/profile exists or every kernel implementation is
free of defects. The contained failure domain includes the translator, adapted
machine/device code and guest-facing network stack. The trusted supervisor,
minimal authority endpoints and macOS kernel remain outside that domain.
A defect in those trusted components is not asserted to be contained by their
own filtering. Separate processes protect them from a compromised worker's
ordinary memory writes; the following capability rules protect the other paths.

**Process roles.** “Worker” is not one undifferentiated socket policy. The
execution/device worker has no networking sockets or service lookup. The separate
fallible per-VM libslirp worker has authorized TCP/UDP sockets for ordinary host
network service access, but no unfiltered ICMP receive socket, DNSConfiguration
lookup, guest RAM, disk or general filesystem/task authority. The minimal trusted
ICMP endpoint owns only its ICMP/query sockets and byte channels. The distinct
trusted DNS provider owns only its named configuration-service connection and
snapshot channel. Apply role-specific manifests under the same trusted launcher;
none inherits another role's grant merely because both serve the same VM.

**Startup and capability conservation.** Use the non-root new-process spawn of
49–52, never SETEXEC as a substitute for a fresh Mach namespace. The supervisor
owns launch until the complete manifest is installed and the worker is admitted.
No guest bytes, packet parsing or generated code run during this trusted phase.
Pin the executable/dependency/signing and policy identity to the supported
macOS deployment; arbitrary inherited environment, loader injection and startup
callbacks are not part of the launch contract. Failure to install or validate a
required restriction terminates the new incarnation before admission.

| Route class | Selected authority and closure condition |
| --- | --- |
| File descriptors and filesystem | Claim49's explicit close-default allowlist: own disk objects with stated access mode, bounded logging and two one-way byte pipes, plus null input. No directory, shared writable logging file, peer disk, socket carrying rights or supervisor terminal. Paths and object identity are resolved by the supervisor; request bytes cannot open new paths. Existing open rights are audited because later path denial does not revoke them. |
| Mach inheritance | Fresh IPC namespace is necessary but insufficient. Inspect the explicit inherited registered, exception, bootstrap, host and task-access sets and all spawn actions identified in51. No peer task, thread, memory entry, service connection or arbitrary registered/watch port is admitted. Omit/clear optional inherited endpoints where supported; the non-null task-access endpoint that cannot simply be cleared is retained only with52's scalar authorization interface, not relabeled absent. |
| Own task/thread/HVF and memory entries | Own task/thread rights and owned HVF resources are admitted. Memory entries may derive only from owned allocations or explicitly granted per-VM RAM. Repackaging an existing right does not enlarge its backing authority. Supervisor task rights are never returned to the worker. Guest DMA and service operations use the reviewed per-VM RAM admission protocol, not a process-wide host pointer supplied by the guest. |
| PID-to-port or identity-token manufacture | Retain the reviewed Mach/BSD task-port restrictions, including distinct Mach96 protection. Claim50's flavor/identity conversion rules apply in addition to policy: no peer token/control right is seeded. Baseline failure to acquire peer rights remains inconclusive evidence; the construction relies on the named policy checks and capability provenance, not that failed baseline. |
| Non-port process control | Deny unneeded process-control entry points and deny non-self signals/process operations. No worker need is identified for ptrace, PID suspend/resume/socket shutdown, target process policy, credential/persona/coalition mutation or code-signing mutation. Do not copy installed application/airlock profiles' broad process grants. Any necessary own-process query is separately constrained; an exemption for an entire multiplexed syscall is not justified by one harmless flavor. |
| Name/service acquisition | No general bootstrap lookup, socket, named shared-memory/IPC namespace, directory or service capability is present in the execution/device worker. The separate libslirp worker admits only its named TCP/UDP socket role above; it does not gain arbitrary socket families or service discovery. Existing service rights are counted independently of name lookup. Claim55's MIG205 denial closes the particular ordinary-host-to-IOKit factory even without bootstrap; it is not a universal service filter. Other host factories with no required worker role are excluded by the private MIG policy. Own timing/runtime queries retain only their needed read authority. |
| Protocol authority | Byte pipes transport bounded data/status and opaque per-incarnation IDs, never file descriptors, Mach rights, arbitrary PIDs, paths or caller-selected kernel operations. The receiving trusted endpoint independently validates ownership, lengths, state transitions and operation kind. Message shape alone is not authorization. No request can turn the supervisor into a generic task/file/service proxy. |
| New execution | After bootstrap, no process fork/spawn/exec, arbitrary library/plugin loading or policy transition can create an unrestricted descendant. Shutdown cannot temporarily remove restrictions. Trusted supervisor relaunch is a new manifest and incarnation, not authority obtained by the old worker. |

The finite process-control distinction has direct source support in the retained
[XNU syscall table](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/xnu-12377.1.9/bsd/kern/syscalls.master):
ptrace26; csops169/170; process_policy323; proc_info336 and extended545;
pid_suspend433, pid_resume434 and pid_shutdown_sockets436; coalition458/459/532/
556/557 and persona494. These names identify concrete policy surfaces, not a
claim that every call currently defeats ordinary credential checks. Blanket
exclusion is available because these mutation interfaces are not required by
this worker architecture; necessary self operations must remain self-scoped.
The retained [mach_host.defs](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/xnu-12377.1.9/osfmk/mach/mach_host.defs)
also names clock, memory-entry, processor-set-name, voucher and diagnostic
interfaces in addition to MIG205. Their presence prevents equating an ordinary
host port with only time queries. Do not claim that a processor-set **name** is a
control port or that an existing pager capability can be manufactured from a
PID. The policy admits the required own-resource/query subset and excludes
unneeded factories/mutators; any added runtime dependency must preserve these
postconditions before admission.

**Exact platform premises.** This construction trusts macOS process address-space
isolation, Mach right/object-type and credential enforcement, close-default spawn
semantics and the installed custom Seatbelt policy's MAC/syscall/MIG enforcement
on the supported OS revision. It relies specifically on the private predispatch
filter facility traced in40–55, including the shipped kernel-MIG selector, rather
than public App Sandbox entitlement names alone. Existing replicated results
establish the stated finite controls and selected denials, not an exhaustive
production policy. Policy compilation/admission, complete inherited-right manifest
and required own-HVF operation conformance are implementation acceptance checks
against this contract. A future OS that cannot enforce one of these exclusions
is unsupported until the boundary is repaired; silently dropping a rule is not
fallback. No indispensable worker requirement to obtain a peer-memory or generic
process-control grant has emerged from the inspected interfaces.

**Network authority correction, not a filtering shortcut.** Claim68 identifies
an actual excessive receive capability: an unconnected Darwin ICMPv6 datagram
socket can deliver unrelated host ICMP error quotations. A connected destination
socket is not a guest echo-ID filter and loses router-origin error reception.
Ordinary authorized guest access to host network services does not authorize
reading unrelated host/peer packet quotations. Therefore the fallible libslirp
worker must not own either an unfiltered ICMP receive descriptor or a channel
that forwards all its raw packets.

For the adapted-libslirp route, select a separate **trusted minimal network
endpoint**, isolated from the guest-facing protocol stack. It owns the necessary
ICMP socket and bounded connected route-query sockets from68. The distinct named
DNS snapshot provider remains a bounded trusted supervisor service under68, not
a reason to expand the ICMP endpoint. It accepts only67's bounded send/query requests; it creates
and owns the outstanding request records, verifies complete reply/error
correlation and returns only matched guest-owned bytes or typed status. It never
exposes raw receive buffers, unmatched quotations, native socket handles or rights.
Network input is untrusted even here: bounded parsers and correlation constitute
part of the explicitly trusted endpoint, not containment of its compromise.
A guessed/spoofed packet that matches an outstanding network exchange remains a
network-authenticity issue; it grants no access to arbitrary stored host records.
The endpoint must fail closed on malformed, ambiguous or uncorrelated input.

This placement is the smallest authority separation identified by the inspected
Darwin mechanism: the component permitted to see fanout is trusted, and the
component whose compromise is contained cannot see that fanout. It adds a real
trusted parser/matcher and process/channel cost; no assertion of negligible cost
or a completed implementation is made. Its code is limited to socket lifecycle,
ICMP framing/correlation and typed result release: no libslirp, code generator,
guest RAM mapping, disk access or general filesystem service. Validate lengths,
quote offsets, request ownership and output bounds before releasing any bytes.
Run one endpoint instance per VM under the trusted launcher, with only its own
network handles and bounded byte channels. Its crash, allocation failure or hang
stops/fails that VM's network lane under supervisor control; no other VM depends
on its process or writable state. Ordinary process failure is therefore still
contained even though malicious compromise of this explicitly trusted policy
implementation is not promised contained by its own matcher. If the project requires containment of
this endpoint's own compromise too, this construction is insufficient: the exact
missing premise is an OS-enforced per-VM receive selector, or another backend
with that boundary. The separately conditional vmnet route remains available;
its entitlement/framework isolation must be established on its own contract.

The ICMP endpoint's deployment grant is precisely the required outbound ICMP
socket operations/options and bounded per-VM channels. It has **no**
`com.apple.SystemConfiguration.DNSConfiguration` lookup grant. That named lookup
belongs solely to68's distinct bounded DNS-provider process/service, which has
no ICMP socket grant or guest RAM/disk access. The untrusted network worker gets
only typed ICMP results and sanitized DNS snapshots, never either native handle.
Neither endpoint inherits generic bootstrap, filesystem, task or peer RAM access. TCP/UDP host-service networking
remains the authorized network surface; socket enumeration/quotations are not
silently added to it. An unavailable provider/grant fails backend admission,
without granting a broader service or falling back to host files. This is a
conditional platform interface choice; no new native grant was exercised.

**Resources and teardown.** Allocation admission covers the guest RAM maximum,
code/cache and metadata, device queues, outstanding disk and network work, parser
buffers, callback records and retiring objects. Charge bytes and object counts
before allocation or growth, including old-plus-new replacement peaks;67 supplies
the network aggregation rule. Preserve a reserved shutdown/control path. An
exhausted cache may evict only after pins retire; an exhausted request/heap budget
returns the specified error/backpressure/drop or contained VM termination. Never
reuse an ID/address while admitted work still references its old incarnation.
These are semantic admission rules; numerical capacity tuning does not require
another architecture probe.

Kernel socket/HVF resources are not counted as if userspace byte accounting
bounded them. Limit live objects and outstanding operations at admission and
handle kernel allocation failures. The supervisor retains independent management
and termination authority, timeouts and bounded logging; worker progress cannot
be required to initiate termination. This contract contains allocation failure
and resource reuse races. It does **not** promise a hard host-wide CPU/RAM/kernel
resource-exhaustion ceiling against arbitrary malicious native code: that stronger
availability guarantee would require separately established OS quotas. It does
not weaken the prohibition on unauthorized host/peer state access.

On ordinary shutdown/reset, stop admissions, resolve or cancel admitted effects,
drain endpoint callbacks and owned I/O, apply04-29's truthful durability barrier,
then retire mappings/rights/buffers. On worker crash or forced termination, trusted
owners mark the incarnation dead first, discard later replies, finish or cancel
already-issued host work according to its real completion state, reap the process
and release rights only after their owners can no longer use them. A timeout may
report stopped-with-error; it never fabricates successful flush/cancellation.
Disk effects already issued are not rolled back. Trusted endpoints hold only
copies/owned requests or explicit pinned per-VM grants, never dangling worker
pointers. Reboot creates a new incarnation after old authority is retired; failure
to drain prevents reuse rather than leaking old callbacks into the new VM.

**Conclusion and stopping point.** The selected worker boundary has a finite
capability provenance, enforcement, resource-failure and teardown contract.
There is no remaining identified indispensable peer/process grant for that
worker. Complete question12 acceptance depends on independently accepting this
trusted supervisor/endpoint boundary and the stated supported-OS/private-policy
premises; it does not require a production implementation or an unbounded series
of denial probes. The real network-fanout issue is resolved only by the explicit
trusted placement above, not by filtering inside a compromised broker. Next
bounded action is root's independent review of70 and its composition with68/69;
no new runtime budget, profile change or source survey is authorized by this
conclusion.

## IPv6 fragmentation and extension applicability (claim71)

**Method before work:** translator-authored, at most30 minutes, source-only,
2026-09-20; independent root review follows. Inspect a few primary files at the
retained libslirp pin26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d: IPv6 input/output,
UDP6 and their direct fragmentation/dispatch seams. Seek a real reassembly and
output fragmentation path for ordinary legal IPv6 UDP over MTU1500, or a concrete
gap and replacement boundary. No build/runtime or permission to narrow the
required normal IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP/ICMP behavior. Stop at that decision-changing
result rather than expanding into an unrestricted protocol audit.


**Claim69 disposition:** root Return48 accepts the scoped question10 contract
after the reset-port correction and loader-bound clarification. Actual boot
input validation and emitted-loader conformance remain future implementation
work; the admitted guest and lifecycle boundaries remain as stated.

### CLM-03-03-71 — the pinned IPv6 packet layer has no fragment reassembly or output fragmentation; large legal UDP requires an additional packet-layer adapter

**Source result, independently accepted in root Return50.** Four small primary files were
fetched at the same libslirp pin into
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-slirp-ipv6-01`: `ip6_input.c`, `ip6_output.c`, `ip6.h`
and `if.c`. `sources-fetched.json` and `source-if.json` retain URLs, lengths and
SHA256 values; `sources.json` retains the initial restricted-DNS failure.
The existing pinned `slirp.c`, `udp6.c` and `socket.c` copies supply their callers.
No source build, packet execution or observed network result is claimed.

**Guest-to-host path.** `slirp_input` removes the Ethernet header and calls
[ip6_input](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip6_input.c)
directly. That function validates the base header, rejects base-header length plus
payload above `if_mtu`, then switches **only** the base Next Header among TCP,
UDP and ICMPv6. Every other value frees the packet. There is no preceding fragment
queue or extension walker on this path. Next Header44 therefore drops both real
fragments and atomic fragments. A valid destination-options chain also fails
before reaching its upper protocol, even when the whole packet fits MTU1500.
[udp6_input](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/udp6.c)
assumes the UDP header follows the fixed40-byte IPv6 header; it does not supply a
hidden reassembly/extension fallback.

A concrete ordinary counterexample is a checksummed UDP datagram with2000 bytes
of application data between endpoints able to reassemble it. The UDP portion is
2008 bytes. The guest can send two IPv6 fragments containing1448 and560 bytes of
that portion: IPv6 packet lengths1496 and608, offsets0 and181 in eight-byte units,
M flags1 and0, shared source/destination/identification, fragment Next Header17.
Both fit MTU1500 and the first includes the complete UDP header. Both are dropped
by the current base-header dispatcher. This does not depend on jumbo payloads,
raw protocols or a too-large input Ethernet frame. Even a valid atomic Fragment
header around a small UDP datagram reaches the same unsupported switch arm.

**Host-to-guest path.** `sorecvfrom` in
[socket.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/socket.c)
uses FIONREAD and `m_inc` to accommodate a received UDP datagram. `udp6_output`
prepends one UDP and one base IPv6 header, computes the checksum and calls
[ip6_output](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip6_output.c).
That function fills header fields and queues the same packet.
[if_output/if_start](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/if.c)
perform queueing and pass it to `if_encap`; the latter's IPv6 path resolves NDP and
copies the complete packet to one Ethernet frame. Its fixed-buffer limit can
reject a large packet, but does not fragment it. Consequently the2000-byte reply
becomes one2048-byte IPv6 packet, not two MTU-compatible fragments. Whether the
current callback can carry that oversized frame is immaterial to the selected
MTU1500 contract. Advertising MTU1500, dropping every large UDP message, or
allowing only echo/TCP does not repair either direction.

**Protocol boundary and smallest remedy.** [RFC8200 §§4,4.5,5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8200.html)
specifies extension traversal and source fragmentation/destination reassembly.
It requires at least1500-byte reassembly; larger datagrams require capable
endpoints. Reassembly keys include source, destination and identification, with
length/offset validation, overlap handling and a60-second completion limit.
Atomic fragments are processed independently of matching queued fragments.
These rules apply to the adaptation; IPv4 fragment routines are not evidence
that IPv6 behavior is inherited correctly.

Retain the existing TCP/UDP host socket boundary, but add an explicitly owned
IPv6 packet layer **inside the fallible per-VM network worker**, not inside70's
minimal trusted ICMP endpoint:

- Before upper-layer dispatch, traverse validated extension headers and perform
  bounded fragment reassembly. Preserve the original header/offset correspondence
  for checksums and guest-visible errors. Hand transport code the real upper-layer
  offset/length and pseudo-header inputs, or a deliberately normalized equivalent.
  A reassembled datagram larger than1500 must not be fed back into the existing
  input MTU check: that check describes individual link packets. Validation of
  the completed datagram and validation of each link fragment are distinct.
- Replace the fixed40-byte UDP/TCP/ICMP assumptions at this boundary where needed.
  Simply stripping all options is incorrect. Valid options requiring no action
  can be consumed according to their defined semantics; unknown options/headers
  need their specified skip/discard/error behavior and accurate pointer. Routing
  headers and destination-dependent checksum semantics cannot be silently erased.
  This does not promise arbitrary IP-protocol tunneling.
- On host-datagram output, the socket proxy originates guest-facing IPv6 packets;
  fragment the complete checksummed transport datagram to the guest link MTU when
  necessary, with an owned identification, correct offsets and final-fragment
  length. Do not treat this as an IPv6 router fragmenting an arbitrary forwarded
  packet. Keep guest-side and host-side packetization/PMTU domains separate.
- Charge fragment nodes, bytes, incomplete contexts, timers, completed buffers and
  queued output fragments to67's aggregate budget before allocation, including
  coexistence during assembly. Admission failure may drop according to network
  semantics; it cannot justify a permanent small-datagram-only service. Reset
  discards contexts by incarnation; no partial datagram reaches a host socket.

This is a precise **new packet-layer adaptation requirement**, not an existing
inherited implementation established by this audit. The inspected pin supplies
no complete IPv6 reassembly/output-fragmentation component to select unchanged.
The separate vmnet packet interface is a possible replacement boundary, but its
platform/network contract is not proved by this finding. No replacement stack
survey is needed to establish the present counterexample.

**Decision consequence and stop.** Claims65–70 settle mechanism, authority and
some adapter interfaces; they cannot support selecting the inspected libslirp
plus only echo/DNS changes as complete ordinary IPv6 networking. The next bounded
action is independent root review and a concrete choice between the explicit
packet-layer adaptation above and an already identified packet backend. Full
network acceptance must include the selected extension/fragment contract; actual
implementation and packet conformance testing then belong to planning. No
runtime, new MTU restriction or full-network acceptance is implied here.


## IPv6 normalizer construction (claim72)

**Method before work:** translator-authored bounded30-minute source/interface
construction, 2026-09-20, independently reviewed by root. Use71's concrete pinned
libslirp seams and primary IPv6 specifications to select a finite packet
normalizer around the retained transport/socket components. Specify state,
validation, dispatch, checksum/quote preservation and output packetization,
including legal large UDP. No new runtime, implementation or broad stack survey.
Stop at a reviewable construction or an exact unresolved semantic dependency.
Also clarify70's per-process grants without changing its trust boundary.

### CLM-03-03-72 — select a bounded IPv6 packet normalizer around retained transport components

**Architectural choice independently accepted in root Return51, within the
scoped normalizer construction.** Add one packet-layer
normalizer in the fallible per-VM network worker. Retain pinned libslirp's TCP/UDP
socket translation and local control services, with explicit normalized ingress
and packetized egress interfaces. This is a concrete new adapter, not a claim of
inherited IPv6 fragmentation. It closes71's packet-layer gap by construction;
it does not make the network worker a transparent arbitrary-protocol router.
Required ordinary IPv4/IPv6 TCP, UDP and ICMP, including large legal UDP, remain
in scope. IPsec, mobile/source-routing services, jumbograms and arbitrary Ethernet
protocol pass-through are not promised by this socket-proxy backend. Unsupported
protocols/options receive the appropriate protocol disposition, not silent
reinterpretation as TCP/UDP. A different packet backend remains needed if those
additional services become requirements.

**Concrete retained seams.** The new entry replaces `slirp_input→ip6_input` for
IPv6. It never calls the old base-header dispatcher on a completed oversized
reassembly. Its successful output is `Normalized6`: an owned base IPv6 header
plus contiguous upper-layer bytes, transport kind, original source/destination,
link metadata, ingress/local-service classification, fragmentation provenance
and an owned quote/offset descriptor. Set the normalized base Next Header and
Payload Length to the upper protocol and its actual length. After header actions
are resolved, no remaining extension changes the ultimate destination; validate
transport checksum against those addresses, protocol and upper length. Then call
the existing fixed-header transport entry with its expected byte order: UDP/ICMP
network-order payload length, TCP's existing host-order conversion. This does
not repair a checksum by recomputing it over corrupted incoming data.

The quote descriptor is mandatory: errors in normalized coordinates must not
quote a fabricated header chain or use a shifted Parameter Problem pointer.
Keep original bytes/field correspondence with the admitted operation, using67's
bounded ownership. Preserve Ethernet provenance needed by the existing local
NDP path rather than inventing a source MAC. The adapter replaces the old input's
blanket hop-limit-zero check with explicit local-service versus forwarded/proxied
classification; retain the appropriate one-hop decrement for external traffic,
without applying it twice in UDP's existing code. No packet reaches a host socket
until the complete admitted transport unit has passed validation.

**Finite ingress state machine.** `CHECK_LINK → WALK_HEADERS → WAIT_FRAGMENTS`
(or directly `VALIDATE_TRANSPORT`) `→ DISPATCH`, with terminal `DROP/ERROR`.
Each transition consumes bytes or completes a queued fragment context. An input
frame must fit the configured link MTU and its declared length; trailing Ethernet
padding is excluded. The normalizer supports a full non-jumbo IPv6 payload of
65535 bytes after reassembly, including upper headers. UDP's no-extension maximum
application payload is therefore65527; MTU1500 does not become an application
message-length cap.

The ordered header walker validates each complete header before advancing.
Support Hop-by-Hop/Destination TLVs, padding and required unknown-option actions;
only a leading Hop-by-Hop header is valid. Unsupported routing types with no
segments remaining can be skipped; nonzero remaining segments produce the
specified routing error rather than rewriting a destination. No Next Header
terminates without a transport dispatch. Unrecognized upper protocols are
rejected with the appropriate error. Header repetition is not rejected merely
for exceeding a small implementation constant: finite packet length bounds
progress. These rules follow [RFC8200 §§4,4.2,4.4,4.6,8.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8200.html).
They define a socket-proxy interpretation, not an assertion that removing headers
is legal for an arbitrary transparent forwarding router.

For fragmentation, allocate a per-incarnation context keyed by source,
destination and identification; never by UDP port inferred from a non-first
fragment. The concrete representation can be one bounded byte buffer, byte-range
occupancy bitmap, saved offset-zero prefix/metadata, terminal length, first-arrival
deadline and a terminal-discard marker. Nonfinal fragment lengths and offsets
must be aligned and checked before arithmetic/copy; reject overflow or a completed
payload above65535. Use the offset-zero prefix and complete upper-header chain;
fragments may arrive out of order. Missing first fragments cannot cause premature
transport dispatch. A first fragment missing the complete upper header is rejected
under [RFC7112 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7112.html), with its prescribed
error and original coordinates. The per-context first-arrival deadline is60 seconds;
expiry releases payload and issues a permitted reassembly-timeout error only when
its required original information exists. Incarnation reset invalidates all contexts.

Any overlap terminates the whole context, retaining a bounded discard marker for
its remaining lifetime so later pieces cannot resurrect it. Choosing to reject
exact duplicate fragments too is conservative and avoids a second byte-comparison
policy; [RFC5722](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5722.html) establishes whole-datagram
overlap rejection. Atomic fragments bypass ordinary fragment queues and cannot
poison or complete a different pending datagram, following
[RFC6946](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6946.html). After a completed reassembly,
resume ordered header processing at the saved next header; do not inspect
unavailable fragment payload as if it were a full header. If another fragment
header is encountered, it traverses the same bounded state machine, retaining
already validated prefix/provenance; no unbounded native recursion is introduced.
All intermediate buffers and retained prefixes remain charged.

Retain an ECN aggregate with the context. Preserve a uniform codepoint; propagate
CE across ECT fragments, and discard a CE/Not-ECT mixture. Choose discard for other
inconsistent mixtures rather than manufacture ECN negotiation. This satisfies the
reassembly constraints in [RFC3168 §5.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3168.html)
without claiming the retained TCP implementation negotiates ECN. Keep a separate
`had_fragment_header` fact: fragmented Neighbor Discovery, including atomic cases,
must not be made acceptable merely by normalizing away the header. The local ND
entry rejects it under [RFC6980 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6980.html).

**Bounded output construction.** Interpose before the retained `if_output`/link
queue for all originated IPv6 transport packets. Local multicast control packets
whose rules forbid fragmentation retain their protocol-specific size rules.
TCP's existing segmentation remains useful but is not the UDP remedy. For a
normal originated base-header UDP/echo packet, compute the complete transport
checksum once. If its IPv6 length exceeds1500, divide upper bytes into chunks of
`floor((1500−40−8)/8)*8 = 1448` bytes except the last. Prepend a40-byte base and
8-byte Fragment header to each, with one identification, increasing eight-byte
offsets and correct final flag/payload length. Own the identification namespace
for the source/destination lifetime; do not wrap into a still-live allocation.
This is packet origination by the proxy, not router fragmentation of third-party
IPv6 traffic. Host-side sockets retain their own kernel packetization and route
limits; guest-side MTU and host-side PMTU are distinct quantities.

Before publishing the first fragment, reserve the full bounded output work and
validate lengths/checksum/identity. Queue failure before publication drops the
whole generated datagram; failure after publication may lose network fragments,
but cannot reuse live storage or report delivery that did not occur. The upper
payload remains pinned until all queued fragment copies/references retire. A
65535-byte no-extension upper unit needs at most46 fragments at this MTU; this is
a derived bound, not a new message-size restriction. Claim71's2008-byte UDP unit
produces1496- and608-byte IPv6 packets and reconstructs exactly the original
2000 application bytes in the opposite direction.

**Error and resource composition.** Use67's typed error builder and original
quote descriptor for structural errors, timeout and actual socket errors.
Apply [RFC4443 §2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4443.html)'s error eligibility
and rate limiting before allocating or emitting: error-on-error and multicast/
invalid-source restrictions must survive normalization. ICMP errors include as
much valid original material as fits the prescribed limit, not arbitrary stale
buffer bytes. A complete consumed datagram and its quote can coexist only while
both are charged. Fixed per-context bounds plus67's aggregate byte/object/context/
timer/output limits make this finite; exhaustion is network loss or contained
termination, not permission for unbounded reassembly or a permanent1452-byte UDP
application cap. Error generation uses the reserve and cannot recursively consume
the exhausted data budget. The trusted ICMP endpoint receives only validated
bounded echo requests and returns its correlated records; it does not acquire
this extension/reassembly parser, a libslirp instance or guest RAM.

**IPv4 stays a distinct inherited packet component.** The same-pin
[ip_input.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip_input.c)
actually invokes `ip_reass` before TCP/UDP/ICMP dispatch, and
[ip_output.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/slirp/libslirp/-/raw/26be815b86e8d49add8c9a8b320239b9594ff03d/src/ip_output.c)
fragments over-MTU output when DF permits, constructing an mbuf chain with offsets,
MF bits and per-fragment checksums. Retain these IPv4 paths under the common
allocation/teardown and typed-error adapters; do not transplant IPv4 overlap rules
into IPv6. Source files/URLs/hashes are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-slirp-normalizer-01/sources.json`; a guessed `ip6.c`
lookup returned404 and is retained as a failed lookup, not evidence of absent
checksumming. No new checksum-source claim depends on that filename.
This establishes concrete inherited IPv4 fragmentation seams, not a fresh audit
of every IPv4 option, transport algorithm or socket error.

**Decision and limits.** Select this finite normalizer construction for the
adapted-libslirp candidate rather than make another stack survey a prerequisite.
It adds one owned parser/reassembly/packetizer component, with existing transport
and socket endpoints on either side. Its exact field/state transitions above
replace the missing IPv6 behavior; coding the component and checking its conformance
are planning work. Independent review must establish that the construction
preserves the required ordinary protocol envelope and the70 authority boundary.
It does not accept all unmodified libslirp behavior, performance, a production
sandbox, transparent IPsec/source routing or full networking merely from echo.
Root Return51 accepts this scoped construction; Return49 accepts corrected70
per-process grants. The current summary owns subsequent integration work; no
new runtime or implementation is authorized here.

## Combined required-device choice (claim73)

**Method:** translator-authored bounded existing-evidence composition,
2026-09-20; root independently reviews. Use56/57/60/67–72, accepted boot69 and
INV03-04 claims26–29. No new source survey, implementation or runtime. Determine
whether the selected required devices have a coherent discovery, authority,
completion, reset and failure contract; distinguish architectural contradictions
from production conformance. Claim72's independent disposition remains a premise
of this submission until root records it.

### CLM-03-03-73 — select the staged device set and adapted-libslirp network construction for question11

**Combined question11 decision independently accepted in root Return52, within
its named premises and scoped composition.** Select the following
headless device/backend set. Adapted libslirp with67/68/70/72's packet, authority
and resource changes is the initial network implementation direction. The vmnet
shared/NAT route of57–58 remains a conditional alternative, not a required launch
dependency or silent fallback when an admitted libslirp service fails. This is a
bounded construction choice, not a claim of lower cost, better performance or an
unchanged upstream stack. Required normal IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP/ICMP, including legal
large UDP, remain covered by the selected protocol construction; optional
transparent raw protocols are not silently added.

| Device/discovery | Selected backend and ownership | Completion and failure contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Modern virtio-MMIO block at0xf0000000, GSI16; exact04-28 feature/command/queue profile | Supervisor opens the owned regular disk object; disk lane owns its FD and bounded private staging. No packet/ICMP/DNS process receives that FD. |04-26–29 govern checked IN/OUT/GET_ID/status/used writes, short I/O, barriers and truthful F_FULLFSYNC. FLUSH negotiation controls the accepted cache/persistence behavior; omission does not imply unsafe writeback. |
| Modern virtio-MMIO network at0xf0001000, GSI17;57's one RX/TX pair, MTU1500 and no offloads | Fallible per-VM libslirp worker owns authorized TCP/UDP sockets and72's normalizer. Minimal trusted ICMP endpoint owns real ICMP/query sockets; distinct DNS provider owns only its named service grant. |57's bounded TX snapshot and RX commit;67/68 typed reply/error/MTU/DNS/resource rules;72 fragments complete large datagrams into valid link frames. No UDP-echo substitution, raw-socket fallback or broader service grant repairs failed admission. |
| Modern virtio-MMIO entropy at0xf0002000, GSI18;56's eight-entry profile | Bounded native entropy acquisition under56, separate from untrusted network input. | Initialized owned bytes only, admitted guest completion, bounded acquisition/failure/reset. No fabricated entropy result on host error. |
| COM1 at0x3f8–0x3ff, IRQ4, PNP0501 and selected console command line |60's host-owned16550A and bounded byte streams. Supervisor owns external endpoint configuration; guest byte contents cannot choose paths or management commands. | Preserve FIFO/register/IRQ semantics, partial stream progress, backpressure/drop policy and incarnation drain. Logging/serial output never becomes an unbounded allocation or shared writable authority. |
| PM/S5 and optional reconstruction-reset register0xb040 |69's configured PIIX-derived ACPI and trusted lifecycle owner; no arbitrary firmware execution | Stop admission and apply the common drain/durability sequence. Failed drain/full-sync reports stopped-with-error, not successful shutdown/reboot. |

**Discovery is generated from the same objects.** Claim69 supplies LNRO0005
resources, PNP0501, MADT routes and the nonoverlapping physical/port map. The CPU
profile, enabled devices, queue features, tables and loader command line must be
one immutable launch manifest. The accepted installed-root route admits actual
kernel/initramfs drivers and dependencies before start; an installer staging
artifact alone is not evidence that an arbitrary root image boots. This does not
require a new machine architecture for every validated image or a new boot probe
to settle the interface.

**One completion protocol across all devices.** A device owner snapshots guest
requests under the accepted RAM authority, releases that authority before host
service work, and retains owned data plus typed IDs. Host callbacks never retain
unrestricted guest-backed IOVs or write guest memory. On completion, acquire the
appropriate admitted RAM transaction, commit payload/status/ring writes with
source/PTE alias tracking, publish ring progress, then offer the typed IRQ under
suppression rules. Level/high GSI16–18 feed the accepted IOAPIC/LAPIC interface;
serial and SCI retain their separately specified polarity/trigger behavior.
A driver declining notification does not eliminate completed state. No device
owner waits for the host while holding exclusion that a callback needs.

The network's link-frame slots remain57's2048 bytes despite72's larger complete
datagrams. Reassembly and packetization live only in the separately budgeted
network worker; individual virtio frames still meet1500-byte IP MTU/1518-byte
frame bounds. Thus large UDP does not require larger virtio DMA buffers or hidden
offloads. An exhausted reassembly or output budget causes the specified packet
loss/error, not oversized DMA or permanent narrowing to small UDP.

**Authority and lifecycle composition.** Apply70's separate role manifests at
launch, before guest activity. Genuine ICMPv4/v6 service belongs behind the trusted
endpoint; inherited raw/UDP-echo fallback branches are disabled. Ordinary host
network service access is deliberate; peer memory, host files, arbitrary task
rights and unrelated ICMP quotations are not. Exact socket/provider and private
policy admission remains the named supported-platform premise, with fail-closed
launch. A changed OS that cannot admit the required service within that boundary
reopens the backend choice rather than enlarging the worker's rights.

Queue/device reset closes the old admission generation, retires admitted RAM
commits and waits for owned callback/channel references before reuse. Old network
fragments, DNS jobs, socket replies and disk completions cannot complete new
requests with reused IDs. Releasing a guest TX/OUT buffer means the backend owns
its snapshot, not that remote delivery or disk persistence occurred. Already
issued writes or sent packets are not rolled back. Full-machine S5/reconstruction
uses69's incarnation boundary and04-29's disk barrier; a stuck backend is contained
without falsely acknowledging successful reuse. Management and termination do
not depend on the failed worker making progress.

**Decision consequence.** Subject to independent acceptance of72 and this
composition, no additional architecture-changing blocker is identified for the
required device set. The remaining work is implementing the stated adapters,
validating actual role policies/launch inputs, checking protocol/queue/reset
conformance and measuring costs. Those checks cannot be replaced by assuming
upstream behavior, but unwritten code alone is not a new architectural unknown.
A discovered incompatibility with the stated native service, protocol semantics
or completion/authority contract would reopen the affected choice. Question11
acceptance would still not accept question2/5 or the combined architecture.
Root Return52 accepts this question11 composition. The current summary owns
combined-architecture integration; no native budget or implementation
authorization follows.
